Per a SECOND explicit follow-up request ("add the max-width back to the session rows - sorry it looks weird") — kept below for history, now itself superseded by the third request above: the full-width experiment this section originally documented was reverted — the outer wrapper went back to `w-full max-w-[1200px] mx-auto px-6`, constraining both the separator row and message content to one shared column; `TranscriptSessionSeparator`'s own `px-6` and the message-content wrapper's own `max-w-[1200px] mx-auto px-6` were both dropped as redundant at the time. This is no longer the current state — see the third request above.

Per the ORIGINAL explicit follow-up request ("make the session row not have a max-width... this may affect the sticky effect of multiple session rows so take that into account") — kept below for history, now superseded by the revert above: `InteractionTranscript`'s own transcript body (agent-next-gen-transcript.tsx): the `TranscriptSessionSeparator` row (the sticky "N Messages | #contactId · date" bar) rendered edge-to-edge across the full scrollable width instead of being boxed into the same centered 1200px column as the message bubbles below it — visibly narrower than the full-width record header above it once the window got wide enough to matter.

- The single outer wrapper around the whole per-session `.map()` used to be `max-w-[1200px] mx-auto px-6`, constraining BOTH the separator row and the message content underneath it to one shared centered column. It's now just `w-full` (no max-width at all).
- The 1200px-centered column moved DOWN onto a new per-session wrapper around just the message content (`w-full max-w-[1200px] mx-auto px-6`, replacing the old plain `sessionContentDimmed && "opacity-50..."` div) — message bubbles are unaffected visually, still centered at the same width and alignment as before; only the separator row above them broke free.
- Bug fix (first attempt broke stickiness, per explicit follow-up bug report "the only problem is it's not sticky at the top anymore"): the first version of this change wrapped `TranscriptSessionSeparator` in its own new `<div className="w-full px-6">` sibling to the message content, to get it the full-width look without a max-width. That broke `sticky top-0` outright — a sticky element's stickiness bounds are constrained to its own CONTAINING BLOCK, which for a plain (non-positioned) element is its immediate parent's padding box. A wrapper holding ONLY the separator has zero extra height beyond the separator's own, so there's nowhere for it to "stick" through as the page scrolls — the exact same class of bug this component's own call site doc comment already documents for why live messages have to share the per-session container (see the "Live messages" comment further down). Fixed by NOT wrapping it at all: `TranscriptSessionSeparator` is a direct child of `<div key={session.id}>` again (a sibling of the message content div, exactly as before this whole change), and `px-6` moved directly onto its own root `className` (`"sticky top-0 z-[1] bg-lyra-bg-surface-base px-6"`, agent-next-gen-transcript.tsx) instead of a wrapping div. Since that per-session `<div key={session.id}>` itself sits inside the now-unconstrained `w-full` outer wrapper (no max-width), the separator still renders full-width with no wrapper needed — it just needed to stay a direct sibling of the tall message content beneath it for its sticky containing block to have real height to scroll through.
- Sticky STACKING (multiple sessions' separators piling up as the agent scrolls past several) was never actually at risk from the max-width change itself — `position: sticky` resolves against the nearest scrolling ancestor (the `overflow-y-auto` div, two levels up), not against either max-width wrapper — but the broken first attempt above is a reminder that a sticky element's own immediate parent still matters for whether it can stick AT ALL, independent of the scrolling ancestor question.
- Per a further explicit follow-up request ("add a border to the bottom of the session row"): a first attempt added `border-b border-lyra-border-subtle` to `TranscriptSessionSeparator`'s root (`AccordionPrimitive.Root`, same className as the sticky/`px-6`/`bg` above) — per explicit bug report, it didn't render at all. Fixed by moving it one level down instead, onto the actual collapsed row's own plain `<div className="flex flex-wrap items-center justify-between gap-3 py-2">` (NOT a Radix primitive) — matching the exact same `border-b border-lyra-border-subtle` convention already proven working elsewhere in this app (e.g. `page-header.tsx`'s own record-header divider). The row's existing `py-2` was left unchanged; the border just sits at its bottom edge.
- Per a further explicit follow-up request ("put the border on the div above the one you currently have the border on for the session row so the border is below the session information when it is open," with a screenshot of the expanded "Session Details" panel): the border above put it right after the collapsed row's own text, which sits ABOVE `AccordionPrimitive.Content`'s expanded "Session Details" panel in DOM order — so opening a session showed the border sandwiched between the row and the expanded panel, not below it. Moved up one more level, from the row `div` onto `AccordionPrimitive.Item` itself (`className="border-b border-lyra-border-subtle"`, the shared parent of both the row `div` and `Content`) — since `Item` wraps both children, its own bottom border now sits below whichever is actually rendered last: the row alone when collapsed, or the row plus the expanded "Session Details" panel when open.

## 28. Record-header subtitle: "{N} channels open | Last Customer Response: {date/time}" once 2+ channels are open (all 3 tiers)

Per explicit follow-up request ("if multiple tabs are open update the subhead of the interaction page header to say '{N} channels open | Last Customer Response: {Date} {Time}'"): once `showChannelTabRow` reads `true` (2+ threads on the active interaction), the record-header `PageHeader` subtitle in all 3 page files switches from the single active channel's own "{icon} {Channel label} | {date/time}" line (§23) to this aggregate line instead — that per-active-channel line stops answering the question that actually matters once several channels are live on the same interaction at once.

- `{N}` is `activeInteraction.threads.length` — the same count `showChannelTabRow`'s own `>= 2` threshold already reads, so the two can never disagree about when this branch is showing.
- `Last Customer Response: {date/time}` is built by a new duplicated-per-file helper, `resolveInteractionLastCustomerResponseLabel(interaction, pageMountTimeMs)`, which scans EVERY thread on the interaction (not just the active one) and returns the single most recent real customer-response timestamp, formatted with the existing `formatCompactDateTime`. Resolution per thread, most authoritative first:
  1. `lastCustomerMessageTick` (set the instant a real/simulated customer reply lands, `handleSendMessage`'s reply timeout) — a `clockTick` value, which is real elapsed seconds since the page mounted (`clockTick`'s own 1-per-second `setInterval`). Converted back to an exact real `Date` via a new `pageMountTimeRef = useRef(Date.now())` captured once per page load: `new Date(pageMountTimeRef.current + tick * 1000)`. This is the only branch backed by a genuine wall-clock timestamp rather than mock/canned data.
  2. `reopenedContacts`' last entry — a real historical date/time for a thread reopened from Contact History, even with no live reply yet this session.
  3. A genuinely fresh (`interaction.startedFresh`) thread with neither of the above is skipped outright — deliberately NOT falling through to canned session data, which would surface stale, unrelated mock history for a channel the customer hasn't actually touched (mirrors `resolveActiveChannelDateTimeLabel`'s own "isFreshLaunch short-circuits before the canned branch" reasoning).
  4. Otherwise (a pre-existing/real customer channel with no live activity this session) — that channel type's last canned session's own last CUSTOMER-sender message timestamp (chat/sms/whatsapp only; Voice/Email's canned sessions ship an empty `messages: []`, so those fall back to the session's own `startTime`, same fallback `resolveActiveChannelDateTimeLabel` already uses).
  - The MAXIMUM (most recent) `Date` found across every thread wins. If NO thread has any qualifying response at all (e.g. every open channel is a still-untouched draft), the helper returns `undefined` and the subtitle reads just "{N} channels open" with no trailing clause, rather than a broken "Last Customer Response: undefined."
- `pageMountTimeRef` had to be declared AHEAD of `activeChannelDateTime`/this new computation in all 3 files (not next to `clockTick`, where it's conceptually related, since `clockTick` itself is declared further down each component) — using it before its `useRef` line would otherwise throw a real "used before initialization" error, not just a lint complaint.
- 1-channel interactions are unaffected — they keep the exact "{icon} {Channel label} | {date/time}" subtitle §23 already established; Premium/Advanced's plain-customer-ID fallback (for the rare case neither branch applies) is also unchanged.

## 29. Record-header channel tab row: tighter left inset (`tabPaddingX="3"`, shared `TabList` prop, all 3 tiers)

Per explicit follow-up request ("update the tab padding in the interaction page header to be px-3 so the tabs left align better"): the channel tab row directly below the record header (`TabList overflowMenu overflowBreakpoint="compact" growToFillRow`, all 3 page files) now sits its tabs flush(er) with the tighter insets in the record header above it.

- Root cause investigated first (the user's own follow-up question, "will this affect other tabs?"): `TabList` (`lyra-ui/src/components/tabs.tsx`) has always forced `px-5` onto every direct `[role="tab"]` child via a `[&>[role='tab']]:px-5` selector, REGARDLESS of `Tab`'s own smaller inherent `px-3` base padding (`Tab`'s own className) — that selector's extra specificity (an attribute-combinator selector beats a plain utility class) wins over `Tab`'s own padding no matter what a consumer passes as `className`, so there was no way to dial an individual `TabList`'s tab padding down without changing this shared default — which every OTHER `TabList` consumer in this live-source-aliased `lyra-ui` package would have inherited too (the Customer Information panel's own tabs, Dashboard tabs, `DesktopDesignsPage.tsx`, every Storybook story, and every other app in the workspace that depends on this same `lyra-ui` checkout).
- Fixed by adding a new opt-in `TabList` prop instead of touching the shared default, per CLAUDE.md's Rule zero: `tabPaddingX?: "3" | "5"` (default `"5"`, i.e. today's existing behavior, unchanged for every consumer that doesn't pass it) — `"3"` swaps the forced override to `[&>[role='tab']]:px-3` instead, landing back on `Tab`'s own natural base padding.
- Wired `tabPaddingX="3"` into ONLY the 3 record-header channel `TabList` call sites (`AgentNextGenPage.tsx`, `AgentWorkspace2WithDeskPage.tsx`, `AgentWorkspaceAdvancedPage.tsx`, alongside their existing `overflowMenu overflowBreakpoint="compact" growToFillRow`) — every other `TabList` in the app (Customer Information panel, Dashboard, Settings, etc.) keeps the unchanged `px-5` default.

## 27. Interaction record header: borderless + compact, directly above the session/tab row (lyra-ui core change, all 3 tiers)

Per explicit follow-up request ("remove the border-bottom for the Interaction Page Header ONLY... also make the min-height:54px of the Page-header for the interaction and remove the padding bottom"), with two reference screenshots showing a tight, seamless transition from the record header straight into the session/tab row below it, no gap or double border:

- `PageHeader` (`lyra-ui/src/components/page-header.tsx`) gained two new optional props, since its existing `className` prop only reaches the OUTER wrapper div (`.lyra-page-header-container`) — the INNER row that actually carries `min-h-[68px]`/`border-b`/`px-6`/`py-4` (`.lyra-page-header-suffix-wrap`) was never reachable via `className`, so a plain override from a consumer was structurally impossible; per CLAUDE.md's "Rule zero," this became first-class `PageHeader` props rather than a local CSS hack:
  - `bordered?: boolean` (default `true`) — whether the row draws its own `border-b border-lyra-border-subtle`. Off for a header that sits directly above content that already establishes its own visual separation right underneath it (a tab row, a session/record separator with its own divider) — leaving it on doubles that line into two parallel borders with an empty-looking gap between them.
  - `compact?: boolean` (default `false`) — shrinks the row from the default `min-h-[68px]`/`py-4` down to `min-h-[54px]` with only the BOTTOM padding dropped (`pt-4 pb-0`, top inset unchanged) — for a header whose `actions` never grow past a small icon-sized control, sitting directly above other tightly-packed content.
  - Every other existing `PageHeader` consumer is unaffected — both default to the prior behavior (bordered, full height).
- New Storybook story, `RecordHeaderCompactBorderless` (`lyra-ui/src/components/__stories__/PageHeader.stories.tsx`): `bordered={false}` + `compact` on a mock "Priya Shah / Email | 7/19/2025 03:41 PM" record header, directly above a mock session row (`# CTX-20250719-05532 · July 19, 2025`, its own `border-b border-lyra-border-subtle`), inside a bordered container card — demonstrates the intended seamless stacking.
- Wired into all 3 page files' interaction record-header `<PageHeader>` call site ONLY (`AgentNextGenPage.tsx`, `AgentWorkspace2WithDeskPage.tsx`, `AgentWorkspaceAdvancedPage.tsx`) — each gained `bordered={false} compact`, right after `ref={recordHeaderRef}`. No other `PageHeader` usage in any of the 3 files (the Settings header, the Dashboard greeting header) was touched — this session's record header sits directly above the session row/channel tab row (`TranscriptSessionSeparator`/`TabList`), which already draws its own bottom border (§26 above), so the header's own border/extra bottom padding is no longer needed there.

## 30. Search-opened interactions never show a customer name unless chat (2.0 ONLY), and ad-hoc phone channels prompt SMS/Voice (all "+" Add Channel flows)

Two related identity/channel-type fixes from the same explicit follow-up request ("for 2.0 none of the interactions opened from search should have customer names - they should all be the email/phone number/handle unless it's a chat then it can have a name since the customer must give one - also - when a channel is added that is a phone number and Continue with {N number} is selected, display radio buttons for sms or voice (like you do with new outbound) so the agent can choose what type of interaction to open"), with a screenshot showing "Miguel Santos" as an interaction's title despite its only channels being two phone numbers, plus a screenshot of the ad-hoc Add Channel popup mid-flow.

**Hide customer names on search-opened interactions (2.0 only):**

- `handleOpenInteractionRow` (`AgentNextGenPage.tsx`) is the handler behind opening an `InteractionHistoryRecord` row from the Search panel's Interactions tab — it used to set the new interaction's `customerName` straight from `record.customerName` unconditionally, regardless of channel type. That's a real (if synthetic) name attached to every seeded `InteractionHistoryRecord` for realism, but it isn't a verified identity for a phone/email-originated interaction — nothing actually confirmed the person on the other end of a raw phone number or email address is who the record says they are.
- Same business rule `contactHistoryDisplayIdentity` (`agent-next-gen-contact-history.tsx`) already enforces for Contact History reopens: `customerName` is now `record.type === "chat" ? record.customerName : address` (`address` is the already-computed `synthesizeChannelAddress(...)` result used for the new `Thread`'s own `value`/`addressLabel` just above it) — chat is exempt because the customer types their own name into the chat widget before the session starts, making it genuinely customer-supplied rather than assumed from a directory match; voice/email/sms fall back to the reach-back address itself (phone number/email) as the record-header title instead.
- Only the `idx === -1` ("brand new interaction") branch needed the fix — the `idx !== -1` ("reopening an interaction already in `interactions`") branch never touches `customerName` at all (spreads `...interaction`), so a previously-opened interaction's identity, set correctly at creation time, is left alone on reopen.
- Scoped to `AgentNextGenPage.tsx` (2.0) only, per the user's own explicit "for 2.0" — Premium/Advanced's own Search-opened-interaction flow (if/when one exists there) was not touched.

**SMS/Voice choice for ad-hoc phone channels (`AddChannelAdHocButton`, shared by all 3 tiers' "+" Add Channel fallback):**

- `AddChannelAdHocButton` (`agent-next-gen-add-channel-button.tsx`) used to fire `onLaunch(trimmed, "voice")` unconditionally for any phone-shaped typed value the instant "Continue with '{value}'" was pressed — no way for the agent to open an SMS thread through this popup at all.
- Added a second in-popup step, phone-shaped input only (email-shaped input is unaffected — still launches immediately on "Continue with"): pressing "Continue with '{value}'" for a phone number no longer launches directly; it swaps the popover's `content` over to the SAME phone `Input` (still editable, same `value`/`onChange`, no longer `autoFocus`) plus a `RadioButtonGroup` (`label="Select Channel"`, options `SMS`/`Voice`) stacked underneath it, and its `footer` over to a "Start Interaction" button, disabled until the field still holds a valid phone-shaped value AND a channel is picked. New local state: `choosingPhoneChannel` (which step is showing) and `phoneChannel` (the pending `"sms" | "voice" | ""` choice, empty by default — no silent default selection). Confirming fires `onLaunch(trimmed, phoneChannel)`, reading the (possibly just-edited) live `trimmed`/`value`.
- Per a further explicit follow-up request ("the updated add channel should display the phone input as well as the select channel radios (so the agent can change the phone if they want) then add the start interaction button"): the phone number typed on step 1 isn't locked in once step 2 shows — the agent can still correct a typo directly on this step before launching, since the same `Input` (bound to the same `value` state) simply carries over into step 2's `content` rather than being replaced by a read-only recap of what they typed.
- Reuses the exact `RadioButtonGroup` component (`lyra-ui/src/components/radio-button-group.tsx`) `create-new.tsx`'s own New Outbound detail screen already uses for its own "Select Channel" picker (`lyra-ui/src/components/create-new.tsx`, ~line 2209) — same component, same label, per the user's own "like you do with new outbound" — rather than a locally hand-rolled pair of buttons, per CLAUDE.md's Rule zero.
- `ChannelType` (`lyra-ui/src/components/channel-row.tsx`) already included `"sms"` — no lyra-ui type change was needed. `handleAddAdHocChannel` (`AgentNextGenPage.tsx`), the `onLaunch` callback wired into this button's only current call site, was already generic over `ChannelType` end to end (channel id, `Thread.type`, `messageCount` gating) — it required no changes to accept `"sms"` through the same path `"voice"`/`"email"` already used.
- `closeAndReset` now also resets `choosingPhoneChannel`/`phoneChannel` alongside the existing `open`/`value` reset, so closing the popover mid-choice (via the header's close button, or clicking away) doesn't leave stale step/selection state behind for the next time it's opened.

## 31. Every genuinely new channel opens as an empty draft, regardless of the interaction it lands on (all 3 tiers) — `Thread.startedFresh` replaces `Interaction.startedFresh` for per-channel freshness

Per explicit follow-up request ("whenever a new channel is open unless it is re-opening a channel it should open as a draft - you can see if you go to 2.0 and open an interaction from the search then add a channel it populates the content"): reported bug was that opening an interaction from Search (`handleOpenInteractionRow`, real canned history — correct), then adding a new ad-hoc channel via the "+" button (`handleAddAdHocChannel`, §30/§23), showed that new channel populated with the SAME shared mock `TRANSCRIPT_SESSIONS`/`_VOICE`/`_EMAIL` conversation instead of an empty draft.

**Root cause:** every "should this look like an empty draft?" read site keyed off `Interaction.startedFresh` — a single flag set once, at interaction-CREATION time, describing whether the whole card was born from an agent-initiated outbound launch (`handleStartCall`/`handleQuickDial`/`handleRedial`) versus resumed from history (`handleReopenContactHistoryEntry`/`handleOpenInteractionRow`/`handleOpenAssignmentFromNotification`, which all deliberately leave it unset — see each handler's own doc comment). That's correct for an interaction's own ORIGINAL channel, but any channel added to an EXISTING card later inherited whatever that one interaction-wide flag happened to be, regardless of whether the new channel itself had any history behind it. A card resumed from Search has `startedFresh` unset (right, for its own resumed channel) — so a later ad-hoc channel added on top wrongly read as "not fresh" too, and fell through to the shared mock log.

**Fix:** added a new per-Thread `startedFresh?: boolean` field (`agent-next-gen-interaction-dashboard.tsx`'s `Thread` interface — full reasoning in that field's own doc comment) alongside the existing per-Interaction one (which is untouched and still serves `CustomerInfoHoverPreview`/`CustomerInformationSidePanel`'s own unrelated "is this a brand-new/possibly-unmatched customer record" question — that really is interaction-wide, not per-channel).

- Set `startedFresh: true` on every Thread built by `handleStartCall`, `handleQuickDial`, `handleRedial`, and `handleAddAdHocChannel` (the exact handler behind the reported bug) — these are the only 4 handlers that ever build a channel with no real history behind it, in all 3 page files.
- Left unset (falsy) on every Thread built by `handleReopenContactHistoryEntry`, `handleOpenInteractionRow`, and `handleOpenAssignmentFromNotification` — these three already carry doc comments from earlier work explaining why they deliberately don't mark their Interaction fresh; the same reasoning now applies at the Thread level instead, unchanged.
- Every render site that used to read `activeInteraction.startedFresh`/`interaction.startedFresh` for a per-channel question now reads the relevant Thread's own `c.startedFresh`/`activeChannel?.startedFresh` instead, across all 3 page files: `resolveInteractionLastCustomerResponseLabel`'s per-thread canned-fallback skip (§28), `activeChannelIsNewOutboundThread` (drives `InteractionTranscript`'s `isNewThread` prop and the 2.0-only header action-cluster collapse), `resolveActiveChannelDateTimeLabel`'s `isFreshLaunch` argument (§22's "Draft" subhead, 2.0/unknown-contact only), the LeftNav card loop's own `isNewOutboundThread` (drives Consult/Transfer/Outcome/Unassign & Dismiss lockdown — §22), the record-header channel-tab loop's own `isNewOutboundThread` (same lockdown, tab-row context), and — the actual fix for the reported bug — `InteractionTranscript`'s `isFreshLaunch` prop itself, which decides whether a channel shows the shared mock log or a genuinely empty "Session Details only" draft.
- A reopened/merged channel (`existingChannel` truthy in `handleStartCall`'s own merge branch) still unconditionally gets `startedFresh: true` too — harmless either way, since its own `reopenedContacts` entry already renders its own synthetic empty session on top regardless of this flag (§14/§23's reopen-stacking mechanism, untouched).
- Verified tsc-clean (70-error baseline unchanged) and eslint-clean (2-error/4-warning baseline unchanged, all 3 page files) after every edit.

## 32. Record-header channel tab row: `ChannelToggleGroup`/`ChannelToggle` segmented-pill experiment tried, then reverted back to `TabList`/`ChannelTab` (all 3 tiers)

Per explicit follow-up request ("let's make the interaction tabs a toggle group instead of tabs but keep the same functionality"), the channel-switching strip directly under each interaction's record header was briefly swapped from the underlined `TabList`/`ChannelTab` bar to a bordered, rounded pill-strip control (`ChannelToggleGroup`/`ChannelToggle`, matching the `ToggleGroup` visual family used by the combined-panel Detail/Interactions/Copilot switch — §37-39), with a new `PlainToggleTab` sibling for the read-only past-session-conversation tab.

**This was reverted** per an immediate follow-up request ("dammit - revert to the tabs - this isn't working"). All 3 page files are back to the original `TabList`/`ChannelTab` implementation — same wrapping-row className (`flex min-w-0 border-b border-lyra-border-subtle bg-lyra-bg-surface-base px-6`, no `items-center`), same `<TabList overflowMenu overflowBreakpoint="compact" growToFillRow tabPaddingX="3" className="flex-1 min-w-0 self-stretch border-b-0">` wrapper (including its overflow-collapse-to-"N More" behavior, which the toggle-group version lacked), same `<ChannelTab>` per-channel entries, same trailing `<Tab>` for the past-session tab. `TabList`/`Tab`/`ChannelTab` imports restored in `AgentNextGenPage.tsx` and `AgentWorkspaceAdvancedPage.tsx`; `AgentWorkspace2WithDeskPage.tsx` never lost them (still used elsewhere for its Home/Customers/Accounts/Tickets/WEM desk-tab row).

The lyra-ui additions from the experiment (`ChannelToggle`'s expanded props, `PlainToggleTab`/`PlainToggleTabProps`, `ChannelToggleGroupProps.children` widened to `React.ReactNode`) were left in place in `channel-row.tsx`/`src/index.ts` — they're additive, unused by any page file post-revert, and harmless (lyra-ui's own 3-error tsc baseline unaffected).

Verified tsc-clean (70-error app-wide baseline, 3-error lyra-ui baseline, both unchanged) and eslint-clean (2-error/4-warning baseline unchanged, all 3 page files) after the revert.

## 33. "Customer is typing" indicator during the simulated reply wait (chat/SMS/WhatsApp, all 3 tiers)

Per explicit request ("add a customer typing animation when a customer is responding to a sms/chat/whatsapp"): a three-dot bubble now shows in the transcript for the whole 2.5s window between the agent sending a message and the simulated customer reply landing — same idea as the reference screenshot (avatar + a rounded bubble of three dots), but built entirely from this app's own existing customer-message styling rather than new colors/shapes.

Per a later explicit follow-up request ("do not display the typing indicator until 2s after the agent message is sent" — a correction of an earlier misread of "wait 2s before initiating a customer response," which had briefly shortened the total reply delay itself instead; see §41), the bubble's own start was decoupled from the agent's send instant: it now appears only once its own separate 2000ms `window.setTimeout` fires, not immediately. The reply itself still lands at the original 2500ms mark, so the indicator's visible window narrowed to the LAST 500ms before the reply, rather than the whole wait.

**`agent-next-gen-transcript.tsx`:**

- New `TypingIndicator` component, placed right after `TranscriptMessageBubble` — reuses that component's own customer-side markup verbatim: the same `h-7 w-7 bg-lyra-accent-green-soft text-lyra-accent-green-strong` avatar circle (with the same real-customer-name-substituted initials every actual customer message already shows), the same `rounded-lyra-lg rounded-tl-none bg-lyra-state-hover` bubble shape. Inside the bubble, three `h-1.5 w-1.5 bg-lyra-fg-secondary` dots use Tailwind's stock `animate-bounce` keyframe with a staggered 0ms/150ms/300ms `animationDelay` each — no bespoke keyframe needed.
- `InteractionTranscript` gained an `isCustomerTyping?: boolean` prop (default `false`). Rendered only for the CURRENT session (`session.id === lastSessionId`, the same scoping `outcomeOpen`/`showSessionActionCluster` already use) and only when `isTextChannel` (chat/SMS/WhatsApp — the same flag that already gates `isFreshLaunch`'s mock-log skip) — Voice/Email never show it regardless of what the caller passes. Sits as the trailing item inside the same `flex flex-col gap-5 py-4` block the live-message bubbles render in, so it inherits identical spacing.

**Page-file changes (`AgentNextGenPage.tsx`, `AgentWorkspace2WithDeskPage.tsx`, `AgentWorkspaceAdvancedPage.tsx`, identical in all 3):**

- New `customerTyping` state — `Record<string, boolean>`, keyed `${interactionId}:${channelKey}` (same scheme `threadLaunchTimestamps`/`outcomeDraftKey` already use, since a literal channel id/type can repeat across interactions).
- `handleSendMessage` resolves `channelTypeAtSend`/`isTextChannelAtSend` right alongside its existing `channelKeyAtSend` derivation, then: sets `customerTyping[typingKey] = true` (only for chat/SMS/WhatsApp) inside its own 2000ms `window.setTimeout` — not the instant the agent's message is applied, per §41 — and clears it back to `false` at the top of the existing 2500ms `window.setTimeout` callback that builds and applies the simulated customer reply, so the bubble shows only for the last 500ms before the real message appears, never both at once.
- `<InteractionTranscript>`'s call site passes `isCustomerTyping={!!customerTyping[`${activeInteraction.id}:${activeChannelKey}`]}` — scoped to whichever channel is ACTIVE right now, same "the agent may have switched tabs while waiting" reasoning `applyToChannel` itself already handles for `channelKeyAtSend`.

Verified tsc-clean (70-error baseline unchanged) and eslint-clean (2-error/4-warning baseline unchanged, all 3 page files) after the typing-indicator feature.

## 34. Fix: transcript scroll position no longer sticks on the wrong channel after switching tabs (shared `transcript.tsx`, all 3 tiers)

Per explicit bug report ("when I open a new channel and then go back to an sms/chat that has multiple threads the chat should default to the bottom... currently... going to the top"): `InteractionTranscript` is one long-lived component instance for the whole interaction — it's never remounted as the agent flips between that interaction's channel tabs (no `key` at any of the 3 page files' call sites). The original "scroll to the newest message on open" `useLayoutEffect` had an empty dependency array, so it only ever fired once — the very first time an interaction's transcript opened. Every LATER channel switch left `scrollContainerRef`'s raw `scrollTop` pixel value untouched while the DOM underneath it was completely swapped for a different channel's (usually differently-sized) content, so the agent landed wherever that stale pixel offset happened to fall — in practice, very often reading as "stuck at the top" for a multi-session chat/SMS/WhatsApp thread.

**Fix:** the effect now depends on `contactId` (the active channel's own base Contact id — unique per `Thread`, so it changes every time the agent switches channels) instead of `[]`, and also calls `setIsAtBottom(true)` alongside the scroll jump — same "as if they'd clicked Scroll To Latest" reset the agent's-own-just-sent-message branch already does — so the "N new" chip/count starts clean for the newly-viewed channel too, not still comparing against whatever channel was last read. Had to move the effect itself (not just its dependency) to after `isAtBottom`'s own `useState` declaration, since `setIsAtBottom` didn't exist yet at the effect's original position in the component body.

Verified tsc-clean (70-error baseline unchanged) and eslint-clean (2-error/4-warning baseline unchanged, all 3 page files) after the fix.

## 35. Record-header subtitle hidden "for now" (all 3 tiers) — flag, not a deletion

Per explicit request ("hide the subhead from the interaction header under the user's name for now - I may bring it back"): the channel/date (or, once 2+ channels are open, "N channels open | Last Customer Response") line that renders directly under the customer name in each tier's record-header `PageHeader` no longer shows.

Rather than deleting the derivation (`activeChannelDateTime`/`lastCustomerResponseLabel`/the subtitle ternary itself, each page file's own `resolveActiveChannelDateTimeLabel`/`resolveInteractionLastCustomerResponseLabel` helpers), each page file gained one named flag — `SHOW_RECORD_HEADER_SUBTITLE = false` — right alongside its other similar "one flip to reverse later" consts (2.0's own `showCustomerInformation`, Premium/Advanced's `showChannelTabRow`). The `subtitle={...}` call site itself just gained a leading `!SHOW_RECORD_HEADER_SUBTITLE ? undefined :` before its existing ternary, in all 3 page files — the exact same subtitle logic still sits right there, just short-circuited. Flipping the flag back to `true` restores it verbatim, no other changes needed — chosen deliberately over the "fully remove, keep history in git" pattern §32 used, since this one was framed as temporary from the start ("for now... I may bring it back").

Verified tsc-clean (70-error baseline unchanged) and eslint-clean (2-error/4-warning baseline unchanged, all 3 page files) after the change.

## 36. Record-header channel tab row: fix outer row padding, not just per-tab padding (all 3 tiers)

Per explicit follow-up ("make the padding px-3 for the interaction tabs (padding-left and right 12px)"), with a DevTools screenshot showing the rendered row's own wrapper carrying `px-6` (24px): §32/§333's earlier `tabPaddingX="3"` change on `TabList` only ever affected the padding INSIDE each individual tab (`[&>[role='tab']]:px-3`, overriding `Tab`'s own base inset) — it did nothing to the SEPARATE wrapping `<div>` that the channel tab row itself sits inside, in each page file, which had its own hardcoded `px-6` (`className="flex min-w-0 border-b border-lyra-border-subtle bg-lyra-bg-surface-base px-6"`). That outer inset is what was actually producing the wider gutter the screenshot flagged — the two paddings stack (row wrapper + first tab's own left inset), so the visible left/right margin was 24px + 12px, not the 12px alone the request asked for.

Changed that wrapper's `px-6` to `px-3` in all 3 page files (`AgentNextGenPage.tsx`, `AgentWorkspace2WithDeskPage.tsx`, `AgentWorkspaceAdvancedPage.tsx`) — the per-tab `tabPaddingX="3"` from §32 is left as-is (still correct/needed), this is a second, independent padding source on the row's own container.

Verified tsc-clean (70-error baseline unchanged) and eslint-clean (2-error/4-warning baseline unchanged, all 3 page files) after the change.

## 37. Session-row Delete Draft button: labeled ghost/critical, not icon-only (shared `transcript.tsx`, all 3 tiers)

Per explicit follow-up ("make the delete icons in the session rows ghost button error variants that say 'Delete Draft'"): the `isNewThread` session row's trash-icon close button — previously `variant="icon"`, icon-only, label only in the `title` tooltip — now renders `variant="ghost"` at `size="sm"` with the "Delete Draft" text visible next to the icon. Color treatment is the same "ghost + critical" composition (`text-lyra-status-critical-strong`, `hover:bg-lyra-status-critical-subtle`, `active:bg-lyra-status-critical-medium`) the row's own Unassign & Dismiss button already uses just below it — there's still no named `ghost`+`critical` `Button` variant in lyra-ui, so both stay composed via `className`.

Scoped to this one session-row copy only, per the request's own wording ("the session rows") — the LeftNav card kebab and record-header tab kebab's matching `removeVariant="delete-draft"` fallback (`ChannelRow`, lyra-ui's channel-row.tsx) intentionally kept their original icon-only treatment; neither was mentioned, and both sit in much tighter spaces (a card row / a tab) than a full session row has to spare.

Verified tsc-clean (70-error baseline unchanged) and eslint-clean (all 4 touched files at their pre-existing baselines — `transcript.tsx`'s own baseline is 0 errors / 14 pre-existing `react-refresh` warnings, unaffected by this change) after the restyle.

## 38. Customer "Full Screen" opens a persistent desk tab instead of expanding in place (Agent Workspace 2.0 Premium only)

Per explicit request ("in agent workspace 2.0 premium, when an agent clicks the Full Screen [button on a Customers-table row], instead of toggling to a full screen mode, open a new tab with a customer icon on the left of the tab, the customer name in the center and a 'x' button to remove the tab — put it to the far right of the tabs in the home screen container"): clicking Full Screen on `CustomerRowInfoPanel` (the docked panel opened by clicking a Customers-table row) now opens that customer as its own tab in the Dashboard/Customers/Accounts/Tickets/WEM/Interactions tab row, instead of `InteriorPanel`'s own built-in overlay-to-100%-width toggle.

Confirmed with the user up front: (1) multiple customers can each have their own open tab simultaneously — opening a new one does NOT replace an already-open one, unlike the interaction record header's single-slot "history conversation tab" precedent (§8); (2) the tab's content is the same `CustomerInformationPanelBody` every other consumer of this file already renders (Overview/Detail/Notes/etc.), just stretched full width instead of docked.

**`agent-next-gen-customer-info-panel.tsx`:**

- New `CustomerFullScreenTabContent` component — composes `PanelHeader` (= `ContainerHeader`) + `PanelContent` + `PanelFooter` directly, the same lower-level pieces `InteriorPanel`/`SidePanel` are themselves built from, rather than reusing `CustomerRowInfoPanel` with `InteriorPanel`'s `isFullScreen` forced on. `isFullScreen` is genuinely internal/uncontrolled state inside `InteriorPanel` (no prop to force it on from outside), and `InteriorPanel` itself is fundamentally a flyout (open/close animation, drag-resize handle, docked-vs-overlay positioning) — none of which a permanently-inline desk tab needs. Owns its own `activeTab`/`recordDraft`/`overviewEditing` state, independent of whatever `CustomerRowInfoPanel` row might currently be docked open. No header close button or prev/next chevrons — closing happens via the desk tab's own trailing remove control; there's no "next customer" concept once a tab is anchored to one specific customer.
- `CustomerRowInfoPanel` gained an opt-in `onOpenFullScreenTab?: (row) => void` prop. When set, `allowFullScreen` is passed as `!onOpenFullScreenTab` (suppressing `InteriorPanel`'s own built-in toggle) and a stand-in `Button` is rendered in `headerActions` at the exact same position/size the built-in toggle used to occupy, calling the new callback instead. Omitted (the default), every other consumer keeps the original real-fullscreen-toggle behavior verbatim — this only changes for whichever page explicitly wires the new prop.
- Per a later explicit follow-up request, this stand-in button's icon/tooltip were swapped from `Maximize2`/"Full Screen" (misleading — this button never toggles an "exit" state of its own) to `SquareArrowOutUpRight`/"Open Tab" — the conventional "open elsewhere" glyph, matching what the button actually does.

**`AgentWorkspace2WithDeskPage.tsx`** (Premium only — `AgentNextGenPage.tsx`/`AgentWorkspaceAdvancedPage.tsx` untouched, keep the original in-place full-screen toggle):

- `activeDeskTab`'s type widened from the plain `DeskTabKey` union to `DeskTabKey | \`customer:${string}\`` (a template-literal type, not a bare `string` widening, so every existing `activeDeskTab === "customers"`-style comparison stays meaningfully typed).
- New `openCustomerTabs` state — `{ id: \`customer:${contactNumber}\`; name; row }[]` — plus `returnDeskTabRef`, a `DeskTabKey` ref capturing which fixed tab was active right before the FIRST customer tab opened (not overwritten while additional ones stack on top), so closing back down to zero open customer tabs lands wherever the agent actually started, not hardcoded.
- `handleOpenCustomerFullScreenTab` dedupes by `contactNumber` (re-clicking Full Screen on an already-open customer just activates its existing tab); `handleCloseCustomerTab` removes one and, only if it was the active tab, falls back to the last remaining customer tab or `returnDeskTabRef.current`.
- The desk `TabList` renders `openCustomerTabs` as additional `Tab`s after `deskTabOrder`'s fixed ones (always far-right, per the request's own wording) — leading `User` icon, trailing `onRemove` using `Tab`'s existing built-in close affordance (`Trash2`, the same glyph/color every other removable tab in the app already uses — see that prop's own doc comment, tabs.tsx — not a bespoke "×", for consistency with the rest of the app). `reorderable`'s `onReorder` is filtered to drop any customer-tab ids before writing back to `deskTabOrder`, since only the fixed tabs are real, persistable order state; a customer tab dragged elsewhere in the row just snaps back to its fixed trailing position on the next render (this `TabList`'s children are always `deskTabOrder` first, `openCustomerTabs` after, regardless of drag order).
- Each open customer tab's `CustomerFullScreenTabContent` stays mounted via the same always-mounted opacity-0/`inert` treatment the "Customers" tab body already uses (see that block's own long-standing doc comment on why `display:none`/`visibility:hidden` both have real bugs here) — applied per open tab, so switching between several open customer tabs never remounts either one and each keeps its own independent Overview/Detail/edit state.

Verified tsc-clean (70-error baseline unchanged) and eslint-clean (`AgentWorkspace2WithDeskPage.tsx` at its 2-error/4-warning baseline; `agent-next-gen-customer-info-panel.tsx` introduces no new errors, only its own pre-existing warning set) after the feature.

## 39. Docked "Customer Information" panel gets its customer-name subhead back (shared `CustomerInformationSidePanel`, all 3 tiers)

Per explicit request ("add a subhead to the customer information panel in an interaction that is {Firstname Lastname} of the customer"): the docked panel's header — flattened to static "Customer Information" with no subhead per an earlier explicit request (§61 and the "was the customer's own name + record id" comment this touches) — now shows the customer's plain `{firstName} {lastName}` as its subhead again. Not name+id the way it briefly was before that flattening; just the name, matching the request's own wording.

Scoped to the ordinary case only: while `matchState` (the outbound "possible matches"/"create new customer" flow) is active, the header keeps its own existing subhead treatment untouched — `matchSubhead` during the search step, no subhead at all during the create step (there's no confirmed customer yet for a name to describe). `customerName` was already a prop this component destructures, so this is a pure `headerSubhead` ternary change, no new prop or state.

Lives in the shared `CustomerInformationSidePanel` (agent-next-gen-customer-info-panel.tsx), so this applies to all 3 tiers automatically — no page-file changes needed.

Verified tsc-clean (70-error baseline unchanged) and eslint-clean (all 4 touched-by-verification files at their pre-existing baselines) after the change.

## 40. Record-header channel tab row stays visible for a single open channel (all 3 tiers)

Per explicit request ("if only one interaction tab is open, display the tab instead of removing it"), with a screenshot showing a single-channel interaction's record header with no tab row at all (just the "+" trigger, then straight to the SLA banner): `showChannelTabRow` — previously `!activeInteraction || activeInteraction.threads.length >= 2`, hiding the tab row entirely until a SECOND channel existed — is now `!!activeInteraction`, so a genuine single-channel interaction still shows its own one-tab row instead of empty space.

Confirmed safe to widen this far in all 3 page files: `showChannelTabRow` used to also gate `showSessionActionCluster` at each file's `<InteractionTranscript>` call site, but per §317–320 ("channel controls always in the session row... even if there is only one channel open") that prop is already unconditionally `true` regardless of channel count — it no longer reads this constant at all. The one other place this constant feeds, each file's record-header `subtitle` ternary ("N channels open" vs. the single-channel date/time line), explicitly re-checks `activeInteraction.threads.length >= 2` itself rather than trusting this constant alone, so it still picks the correct copy — moot either way today since `SHOW_RECORD_HEADER_SUBTITLE` is `false` (§368).

Verified tsc-clean (70-error baseline unchanged) and eslint-clean (2-error/4-warning baseline unchanged, all 3 page files) after the change.

## 41. Typing indicator delayed 2s behind the agent's message, not shown immediately (all 3 tiers)

Per explicit request ("wait 2s before initiating a customer response after the agent comments in the interaction"), then corrected by an immediate explicit follow-up ("no i meant do not display the typing indicator until 2s after the agent message is sent"): the FIRST reading of this request shortened the total simulated-reply delay itself (`window.setTimeout`, 2500ms → 2000ms) — wrong, and fully reverted (back to 2500ms, all 3 page files, including every "2.5s"-referencing doc comment that had been changed to "2s" alongside it).

The actual, corrected fix: `setCustomerTyping[typingKey] = true` — previously set synchronously, the instant the agent's own message was applied — now happens inside its OWN 2000ms `window.setTimeout`, separate from the existing 2500ms one that lands the actual reply and clears the flag back to `false`. Net effect: nothing visible happens for the first 2s after the agent sends a message (no typing indicator, no reply); the three-dot bubble then appears at the 2s mark and stays visible for the remaining 500ms until the reply lands at 2.5s — narrowing the indicator's own visible window from "the whole wait" to "just before the reply," rather than shortening the wait itself.

Verified tsc-clean (70-error baseline unchanged) and eslint-clean (2-error/4-warning baseline unchanged, all 3 page files) after the change.

## 42. Customer full-screen tabs get an "×" close, not the app-wide trash-can convention (Agent Workspace 2.0 Premium only)

Per explicit request ("make the trash icons 'x' for the customer info tabs so they don't look like delete in agent premium 2.0"): the §38 customer full-screen tabs' close control — `Tab`'s standard `onRemove` glyph, which is unconditionally `Trash2` for every other tab in the app (see that prop's own doc comment, tabs.tsx, established over two prior explicit requests) — now renders a plain `X` for these tabs specifically.

Added a new opt-in `removeIcon?: React.ReactNode` prop to lyra-ui's `Tab` (tabs.tsx) rather than touching the shared default: `undefined` (the default) keeps every existing consumer's `Trash2` exactly as before; only a caller that explicitly passes `removeIcon` gets a different glyph. `AgentWorkspace2WithDeskPage.tsx`'s customer-tab `<Tab>` call site is the one and only consumer that passes it (`removeIcon={<X .../>}`) — reasoned specifically because closing one of these tabs just stops viewing a customer's profile, it doesn't delete a draft the way the trash-can convention's original consumers do, so the trash icon read as more misleading here than helpful.

Verified tsc-clean (70-error baseline unchanged, app) and tsc-clean (3-error baseline unchanged, lyra-ui's own separate check) and eslint-clean (2-error/4-warning baseline unchanged, `AgentWorkspace2WithDeskPage.tsx`) after the change.

## 43. Unified ADA-compliant focus ring across every lyra-ui input field (design system, all consumers)

Per explicit request, with two DevTools screenshots ("Search" input, "Customers" tab) showing the tab's focus ring reads as a clearly visible blue box while the search input's own focus state is far more subtle: "for ADA compliance - we need to have a focus border around input fields... make sure anything that is in a focus state has this border."

Investigated every interactive control in lyra-ui and found two competing focus patterns already in the codebase: `Button`/`Tab`/`Checkbox`/`Switch`/`Radio` used a clearly visible, keyboard-only `focus-visible:outline-none focus-visible:ring-2 focus-visible:ring-lyra-border-focus focus-visible:ring-offset-2` (the "reference" pattern — full-opacity color, detached offset ring). Every text-entry field (`Input`, `Textarea`, `Select`, `SearchInput`, `EmailInput`, `PasswordInput`, `PhoneInput`, `DatePicker`/`TimePicker`/`DateTimePicker`) instead used a visibly weaker `focus:` (not `focus-visible:`) inset ring at 20%-opacity using a *different* color token (`lyra-border-active` instead of `lyra-border-focus`), with no offset. `NumberField` and `TagsInput` used a third, JS-`focused`-state-driven variant of the same weak pattern. `Autocomplete`, and two secondary buttons buried inside `PasswordInput`/`PhoneInput`/`TransferBox` (a label-help tooltip trigger, the phone country-code selector), had no focus ring at all.

Standardized every one of these onto the reference pattern (`ring-lyra-border-focus`, full opacity, `ring-offset-2`), using `focus-within:` instead of `focus-visible:` for composite/wrapper-based fields (EmailInput, PasswordInput, PhoneInput, DatePicker, TimePicker, DateTimePicker, Autocomplete) since the focusable element is a real `<input>` nested inside a styled shell div, not the shell itself. Error-state fields keep their own `lyra-status-critical-strong` ring color (now also full-opacity + offset, matching the same visual weight) rather than switching to blue, since red-on-error is an intentional, pre-existing distinction. `NumberField`'s stepper buttons and `TagsInput`'s tag-remove buttons intentionally keep a small inset ring (no offset) since they're small controls packed edge-to-edge with siblings — the same exception `Checkbox`'s `readonly` branch already established. `DateRangeFilterChip` needed no change — it already inherits the reference pattern via `filterChipVariants` shared with `FilterChip`.

Files touched (all in lyra-ui, consumed live by `agent-next-gen-v2` and every other consuming app): `input.tsx`, `textarea.tsx`, `select.tsx`, `search-input.tsx`, `email-input.tsx`, `password-input.tsx`, `phone-input.tsx`, `number-field.tsx`, `date-picker.tsx`, `time-picker.tsx`, `date-time-picker.tsx`, `autocomplete.tsx`, `tags-input.tsx`, `transfer-box.tsx` (backs `DualSelect`).

Known follow-up, not addressed here: `TransferBox`'s option rows are plain `<div onClick>` elements with no `tabIndex`/keyboard handling at all, so they can't receive focus in the first place — giving them a visible focus ring would require first making them keyboard-operable (role, tabIndex, arrow-key/Enter handling), which is a larger behavioral change than a focus-style pass and was left out of scope for this request.

Verified tsc-clean (3-error baseline unchanged, lyra-ui's own check) and tsc-clean (70-error baseline unchanged, app-wide, since the app consumes lyra-ui via live source alias). No app-side files were touched, so the app's eslint baseline is unaffected by definition.

**Follow-up (later session): split mouse focus from keyboard focus.** Per direct follow-up, with a screenshot of the reference "Enter" input's bold blue ring: "I want the focus state of the input to be what it was before BUT have the focus border when a user is not using the mouse (keyboard only)." §43 above had unconditionally replaced every field's old, weaker `focus:` ring with the bold `focus-visible:` one — correct for keyboard users (that's what made it ADA-compliant), but as an unintended side effect it also LOST the old, subtler ring for plain mouse-click focus, since `:focus-visible` only ever matches keyboard-triggered focus in the first place — there was nothing left rendering on a mouse click.

Restored the ORIGINAL pre-§43 ring (20%-opacity, `lyra-border-active`/`lyra-status-critical-strong` color, no offset) specifically for mouse/programmatic focus, layered underneath the unchanged keyboard-only bold ring from §43 — both now coexist, gated by input method rather than one replacing the other:
- Fields where the real `<input>` itself is the focused element (`Input`, `Textarea`, `Select`'s trigger + both search inputs, `SearchInput`, `PhoneInput`'s country-search input) use the plain CSS pseudo-class split `:focus:not(:focus-visible)` (mouse/programmatic) vs. `:focus-visible` (keyboard) directly on that element, via Tailwind's arbitrary-variant syntax (`[&:focus:not(:focus-visible)]:...`).
- Fields where a styled wrapper `<div>` shells a real `<input>` (`EmailInput`, `PasswordInput`, `PhoneInput`'s own shell, `DatePicker`/`DateRangePicker`'s shared trigger, `DateTimePicker`, `TimePicker`) can't use that same pseudo-class on the wrapper — `:focus-within` fires for ANY focus inside it regardless of input method, and there's no `:focus-within-visible` pseudo-class. Used `:has(:focus-visible)` instead (`[&:has(:focus-visible)]:...` for keyboard, `[&:focus-within:not(:has(:focus-visible))]:...` for mouse) — `:has()` lets the wrapper ask "does my focused descendant specifically have `:focus-visible`," which is exactly the same distinction one level removed.
- `Autocomplete` and `NumberField`/`TagsInput` had NO ring at all before §43 (§43's own investigation found these three missing focus indication entirely) — for these, there's no "original" ring to restore for mouse focus; only the new keyboard-only ring is kept, scoped the same way as above (`:has(:focus-visible)` for Autocomplete's CSS-driven `focus-within` ring; a new `keyboardFocused` state, captured via `e.target.matches(":focus-visible")` at the moment `onFocus` fires, for `NumberField`/`TagsInput`'s existing JS-`focused`-driven ring, since neither of those two has a real CSS pseudo-class to hook — the ring lives on a non-focusable wrapper `<div>` driven entirely by React state).
- `Select`'s multi-select search input, `TransferBox`'s own list box and icon buttons, and `DateRangeFilterChip` (inherits from `FilterChip`) were left untouched — none of them had gained a `focus:`-to-`focus-visible:` swap in §43 to begin with (the multi-select search input was never touched by §43; `TransferBox` and `DateRangeFilterChip` already used `focus-visible:` exclusively, with no prior mouse-focus ring to lose).

Files touched (all in lyra-ui): `input.tsx`, `textarea.tsx`, `select.tsx`, `search-input.tsx`, `email-input.tsx`, `password-input.tsx`, `phone-input.tsx`, `date-picker.tsx`, `date-time-picker.tsx`, `time-picker.tsx`, `autocomplete.tsx`, `number-field.tsx`, `tags-input.tsx`.

Verified tsc-clean (3-error baseline unchanged, lyra-ui's own check, no errors in any of the 13 touched files) and tsc-clean (73-error baseline unchanged, app-wide) — no app-side files were touched.

**Follow-up (later session): root-cause correction — the mouse-vs-keyboard split above was fundamentally broken.** Per direct follow-up, with a screenshot of the "Search contact history" search box still showing the bold keyboard ring after a plain mouse click: "when i clicked on the search it didn't give me the soft border." This invalidated the entire mechanism the previous follow-up shipped, not just one field's styling.

Root cause: native `:focus-visible` (and, by extension, `:has(:focus-visible)`) cannot distinguish mouse from keyboard focus on text-type `<input>`/`<textarea>`/`[contenteditable]` elements at all — this is documented, intentional browser behavior (the WICG focus-visible heuristic: a sighted mouse user clicking into a text field still needs to see the caret, so browsers always report `:focus-visible` as true for a mouse-focused text field, unlike buttons/links where it correctly stays keyboard-only). So every `:not(:focus-visible)` / `:not(:has(:focus-visible))` "mouse ring" branch the previous follow-up added never actually fired for any of the library's text-entry fields — the bold keyboard ring was unconditionally winning regardless of input method, exactly as reported.

Fixed by replacing native `:focus-visible` entirely with a custom, JS-tracked input-modality signal: a new `src/lib/input-modality.ts` module attaches real `document`-level `keydown` (→ `"keyboard"`), `pointerdown`, and `mousedown` (→ `"mouse"`) listeners once at load, reflecting the result as `document.documentElement.dataset.lyraInputModality`. Imported as a side-effect-only import at the top of `src/index.ts` so it self-registers exactly once for every consumer automatically. Every CSS ring class touched by the previous follow-up was rewired from `:focus-visible`/`:has(:focus-visible)`-based selectors to Tailwind arbitrary-variant ancestor-attribute selectors instead — `[html[data-lyra-input-modality=keyboard]_&:focus]:ring-2` (direct-input fields) or the `:focus-within` equivalent (wrapper-shell fields), with the inverse `html:not([data-lyra-input-modality=keyboard])` branch for the mouse/programmatic ring. Verified this selector syntax compiles to the intended CSS via a sandboxed `tailwindcss` CLI test before rolling it out broadly.

`NumberField`/`TagsInput`'s JS-`keyboardFocused` state (there's no CSS pseudo-class to hook at all here — the ring lives on a non-focusable wrapper `<div>`) had the identical bug in JS form: `setKeyboardFocused(e.target.matches(":focus-visible"))` inside `onFocus` has the same text-field heuristic problem as the CSS pseudo-class itself. Both now read `document.documentElement.dataset.lyraInputModality === "keyboard"` instead.

`Select`'s trigger button was also converted to the new attribute-based pattern for consistency, even though its trigger is a real `<button>` where native `:focus-visible` would actually have worked correctly (buttons aren't subject to the text-field heuristic) — kept aligned with every other field in the library rather than leaving one outlier mechanism in place. Every previously-established exception (Select's multi-select search input, Autocomplete's separate `open &&` "dropdown is open" ring, `TransferBox`, `DateRangeFilterChip`, the password/phone toggle buttons) was left untouched, unaffected by this correction.

Files touched (all in lyra-ui): new `src/lib/input-modality.ts`, `src/index.ts` (side-effect import), plus the same 13 files from the previous follow-up: `input.tsx`, `textarea.tsx`, `select.tsx`, `search-input.tsx`, `email-input.tsx`, `password-input.tsx`, `phone-input.tsx`, `date-picker.tsx`, `date-time-picker.tsx`, `time-picker.tsx`, `autocomplete.tsx`, `number-field.tsx`, `tags-input.tsx`.

Verified tsc-clean (3-error baseline unchanged, lyra-ui's own check, no errors in any of the 15 touched files) and tsc-clean, app-wide — confirmed zero errors in any of the touched files; the app-wide error count itself no longer matches the 73 figure recorded above (51, spread across long-standing, untouched files including the inert `AgentNextGenPage.pre-split-backup.tsx` snapshot), which reflects drift accumulated over the session's later, unrelated work rather than anything introduced by this fix.

## 44. Customers table: channel icons overlay the row's leading edge on hover only, no reserved column space (Agent Workspace 2.0 Premium + Advanced only)

Per explicit request, with two screenshots (one of `CustomerChannelCell`'s existing side-by-side "Channels" column icons, one a reference example of icons overlapping each other, bleeding slightly past the row's own left edge): "instead of having the channel icons in a row - can you have them overlay on the far left of the row in the customer list customer table... but use lyra-ui." Confirmed via `AskUserQuestion` that this should be a fixed leading decoration (removed from the column drag-reorder/show-hide system entirely, always pinned first), not a real column that just renders differently.

The FIRST version of this reserved a real fixed-width leading column (`TableHead`/`TableCell`, 64px) and rendered the icons as an always-visible, overlapping "avatar stack" (`-space-x-3`, bordered white chips). This was corrected by an immediate explicit follow-up: "I don't want the channels to display until the agent hovers on the row - then also you have them overlapping each other which is not desirable - also remove the extra column space in the header - these icons should overlay whatever content is in the row when they display."

The corrected design: `CustomerChannelStack` (agent-next-gen-customers-table.tsx) no longer occupies any column at all — no leading `TableHead`, no leading `TableCell`, nothing added to `tableMinWidth`. Instead it renders as a `position: absolute` overlay (`inset-y-0 left-0`) painted directly on top of the row's own leading edge, positioned against a `relative` `TableRow` (added only when `leadingChannelStack` is on). It stays `opacity-0 pointer-events-none` until the row is hovered/focused (`group-hover:`/`group-focus-within:`, the same `group` class the row already carries). Icons are laid out with a plain `gap-1.5`, no negative margins, so they no longer overlap each other. `CustomerChannelPopoverButton` gained a new plain `overlay` variant (replacing the earlier `stacked` one) — no per-icon chip/border/ring, since the shared strip around all of them now supplies one background and one fade for the whole cluster.

The overlay's background went through one more fix, per immediate follow-up ("add a solid background (white) to the whole group of channel icons so there is no transparency on hover", with a screenshot showing the row's own text bleeding through the icons): the first attempt used `bg-lyra-state-hover` to match the row's own hover tint, but that token turned out to be a translucent "opacity" color (`--lyra-color-state-bg-hover-opacity`), not a real solid — so the cell's own text underneath still showed through faintly. Swapped to `bg-lyra-bg-surface-base` (a genuinely opaque color, white in light mode / dark surface in dark mode, not an alpha overlay) plus a small `shadow-sm` so the now fully-opaque overlay still reads as "lifted" above the row.

Sizing tuned once more per a further explicit follow-up, with a reference screenshot: "make the padding-left/right 4px for the channel icon button container, make the full border radius 6px and add 4px margin-left so it feels contained within the row." Now `pl-1`/`pr-1` (4px), `rounded-lyra-sm` (the design system's 6px radius token — the prior version only rounded the trailing edge via `rounded-r-lyra-md`), and `ml-1` (4px) so the strip sits with a small gap from the row's own left edge instead of flush against it.

Sizing corrected again per a further explicit follow-up, with a reference screenshot: "add margin-top and bottom: 4px and remove the padding-left and right so it looks like this." Swapped `pl-1`/`pr-1` for `my-1` — the strip is `inset-y-0` (top:0/bottom:0) against the row, so top/bottom margin on top of that shrinks its own height (rather than the row's) to a shorter pill vertically centered with an visible 4px gap above/below, instead of widening the icons' horizontal footprint with padding. `ml-1` (left margin) was untouched — the request only called out padding-left/right, not the left margin.

`CustomersListView`'s `leadingChannelStack?: boolean` prop (default `false`) still exists and still strips `"channels"` out of the column-key/column-def arrays feeding `useColumnReorder`/`visibleCols`/`ColumnToggle` (unchanged from the first version) — only the *rendering* of the icons changed, not the column-removal mechanism. Wired only into `AgentWorkspace2WithDeskPage.tsx`'s and `AgentWorkspaceAdvancedPage.tsx`'s own `<CustomersListView>` call sites, matching "both premium and advanced" — Agent Workspace 2.0's own Desk-tab usage stays on the original "Channels" column. `CustomerChannelCell` itself is untouched.

Follow-up (explicit request: "apply these updates to the agent workspace advanced customer table as well," with a screenshot of the Search panel's own Customers sub-tab still showing the old inline "Channels" column): `agent-next-gen-search-panel.tsx`'s `useSearchPanelContent` also renders a `<CustomersListView>` — per that file's own existing doc comment, its `"customers"` tab only ever renders from `AgentWorkspaceAdvancedPage.tsx` (2.0 and Premium's own `tabs` lists omit `"customers"` entirely), so this is in fact the same Advanced tier, just reached through the Search panel rather than the main Customers desk tab. Added `leadingChannelStack` to that one call site too. Verified eslint-clean (0 errors/0 warnings, unchanged) and tsc-clean (70-error baseline unchanged, app-wide).

Verified eslint-clean (3-error/5-warning baseline unchanged, confirmed via `git stash` diff on `agent-next-gen-customers-table.tsx` — its errors/warnings are pre-existing and unrelated to this change) and eslint-clean (2-error/4-warning baseline unchanged, both page files) and tsc-clean (70-error baseline unchanged, app-wide — confirmed via `git stash` that the two `agent-next-gen-customers-table.tsx`-attributed errors in that count are pre-existing, not new).

## 45. Customers table: keyboard row-to-row navigation via ArrowUp/ArrowDown (CustomersListView, all consumers)

Follow-up request: "allow the icons buttons to be reachable by tab for keyboard users," referencing §44's channel-icon overlay with a screenshot of a phone icon showing a visible keyboard-focus ring inside the revealed overlay.

Investigated at length before touching any code, since the project convention is to diagnose root cause before fixing: traced the full render chain for `CustomerChannelPopoverButton` (`overlay` variant) — `ActionIconButton` forwards `ref` and spreads props via `React.forwardRef`; `Button` renders a real native `<button>` (`Comp = asChild ? Slot : "button"`, and `ActionIconButton` never passes `asChild`) with `ref` attached directly; no `tabIndex` override exists anywhere in the file; `CustomerChannelStack`'s `opacity-0 pointer-events-none` (until `group-hover`/`group-focus-within`) doesn't remove descendants from the Tab order in any browser — only `display: none`, `visibility: hidden`, or an explicit `tabIndex={-1}` do that, and none apply here. `Button` additionally wraps icon-variant buttons that have a `title` (every one of these) in a `<Tooltip>`, and `Popover`'s own trigger uses Radix's standard `asChild`/`Slot` composition — both are well-established, safe compositions that don't strip native focusability. No live browser was available in this sandbox to empirically confirm (playwright's browser-binary CDN is network-blocked here, and no system Chromium was present), so this was asked directly: the user's own screenshot already showed a Tab-focused, ring-visible icon, matching what the code review predicted.

Follow-up clarification from the user revealed the actual, different request — not a bug in the overlay buttons' own focusability, but a missing *row-to-row* navigation shortcut: "when I am tabbing through a table - the tabs should go through the columns, and if a user hits the down button it should go through the rows, when on a row if they tab it should tab through the buttons (or any clickable columns within the row) and then if the user hits the down/up arrow it should move through the rows." Plain Tab already satisfies the first half for free (browsers visit focusable elements — the channel-icon buttons and the trailing delete button, the only interactive "columns" a row has — in DOM order with zero code needed). The missing piece was ArrowUp/ArrowDown jumping focus directly to the same positional button in the row above/below, instead of requiring a keyboard user to Tab through every row in between.

Implemented as one delegated `onKeyDown` handler (`handleBodyKeyDown`, `agent-next-gen-customers-table.tsx`) on `CustomersListView`'s `<TableBody>`, not per-row/per-button — event bubbling means it fires once no matter which row's button has focus. Original version: on ArrowUp/ArrowDown, found the focused element's containing `tr[role="row"]`, its index among the tbody's rows, and the same-index focusable element in the adjacent row, then `.focus()`ed that button directly.

**Correction** (explicit follow-up bug report): "the issue I'm seeing with the tables is when the user tabs to the rows, then uses the up/down arrows to go through the rows, when they tab to the specific buttons within the rows, they can no longer use the up/down arrows to go back to the full row - this means the row can no longer be selected." Root cause: rows themselves were never a focusable/selectable target at all — only their buttons were — so once a keyboard user tabbed from a row into one of its buttons, the original same-position-button arrow-jump logic only ever moved between buttons in other rows, with no way back to a "row selected, Enter/Space will open it" state.

Fixed by making each `<TableRow>` a real, independent tab stop and selection target, not just a hover/click target: added `tabIndex={0}`, `data-contact-number={row.contactNumber}` (so the delegated handler can map a focused `<tr>` back to its row data), and the same reference ADA focus-ring pattern as everywhere else this session (`focus-visible:ring-2 focus-visible:ring-inset focus-visible:ring-lyra-border-focus` — `ring-inset` rather than `ring-offset-2` since the row's own box already is the visual bounds). `handleBodyKeyDown` was simplified accordingly: ArrowUp/ArrowDown now *always* resolves to the row itself (`targetRow.focus()`) regardless of whether the key originated on the row or on one of its own buttons — so arrow-key navigation reliably lands a keyboard user back on a selectable row from anywhere inside it, rather than chaining button-to-button. Enter/Space, when the focus target is the row itself (checked via `e.target.matches('tr[role="row"]')`, not a descendant button — native buttons handle their own Enter/Space and never reach this branch), now calls `onRowClick(row)` — the actual missing keyboard equivalent of a mouse click, i.e. "the row can be selected" via keyboard, which didn't exist in any form before this fix.

Plain Tab is unaffected and still descends naturally from a focused row into that row's own buttons before continuing to the next row (default DOM-order tab-sequencing, no code needed), so "tab through the buttons within the row" from the original request is preserved.

**Generalized into lyra-ui** (explicit follow-up, testing lyra-ui's own Storybook directly rather than the app: "testing in the table of lyra-ui I cannot tab/up/down through the rows as desired," with a screenshot of the plain `Table.stories.tsx` "Auto-Fit (Dashboard)" demo — a bare `Table`/`TableRow`/`TableCell` usage with no app-side wiring at all). This confirmed the feature belonged in the shared component, not hand-rolled per-consumer as it was above — every table built on lyra-ui's `Table` primitives should get this for free, including a plain Storybook demo with zero custom code.

Moved the behavior from `agent-next-gen-customers-table.tsx` into lyra-ui's own `table.tsx`:
- `TableRow` gained an opt-in `selectable?: boolean` prop (default `false` — not every row is a clickable unit; plenty of tables are purely presentational). When `true`: `tabIndex={0}` (unless the consumer already set their own), the same reference ADA focus-visible ring (`ring-2 ring-inset ring-lyra-border-focus`) used everywhere else this session, and an `onKeyDown` that calls the row's own native `.click()` (not a hand-rolled re-invocation of `onClick`, so it fires exactly like a real mouse click, correctly typed, no `MouseEvent`/`KeyboardEvent` mismatch) on Enter/Space, gated to `e.target === e.currentTarget` so a descendant button's own Enter/Space handling isn't double-fired.
- `TableBody` gained the same ArrowUp/ArrowDown row-to-row delegated handler unconditionally — safe to always run since `targetRow.focus()` is a no-op if that row was never made focusable (no `selectable`/`tabIndex`), so it's completely inert for any table that doesn't opt in.

`agent-next-gen-customers-table.tsx`'s `CustomersListView` was then simplified to match: removed the local `handleBodyKeyDown`, the manual `tabIndex`/focus-ring classes/`data-contact-number` plumbing, and the now-unused `useCallback` import — replaced with a single `selectable` prop on its own `<TableRow>`. Behavior is unchanged for that table; the code just moved to the shared component. Also added a `selectable` + `onClick` demo to `Table.stories.tsx`'s own `AutoFitDemo` (the exact story from the bug report) so the base component's keyboard support is directly visible/testable in Storybook without any app.

Verified eslint-clean (3-error/5-warning baseline unchanged for `agent-next-gen-customers-table.tsx`, 0-error/0-warning unchanged for `agent-next-gen-search-panel.tsx`), tsc-clean (70-error baseline unchanged, app-wide), and tsc-clean (3-error baseline unchanged, lyra-ui's own check).

**Fallback for non-`selectable` rows with their own interactive elements** (explicit follow-up, screenshot of `Table.stories.tsx`'s "With Selected Rows" story — a leading bulk-select `Checkbox` + trailing kebab `MoreVertical` button per row, neither row using `selectable`): "for rows with interactive elements - the checkbox (for instance) does not allow the up/down of the rows or selection of the elements on enter." Root cause of the up/down half: `TableBody`'s ArrowUp/ArrowDown handler always called `targetRow.focus()` — correct for a `selectable` row (real `tabIndex`), but a silent no-op for a plain, non-`selectable` `<tr>` (native `.focus()` does nothing on an element the browser doesn't consider focusable), which is exactly this story's shape — a table with real per-row controls but no primary "click the row" action, so the row itself was never made a tab stop.

Fixed by trying two landing targets in `TableBody`'s handler, in order: (1) the target row itself, if `targetRow.tabIndex >= 0` (i.e. actually made focusable via `selectable`); (2) otherwise, the same-position focusable element in the target row (checkbox-column → checkbox-column, kebab-menu → kebab-menu — the same clamped-index approach the very first version of this feature used, reinstated here specifically for the non-`selectable` case). Both remain fully inert for tables with neither `selectable` rows nor any focusable content in them at all.

On the second half of the report ("selection of the elements on enter"): traced this to `@radix-ui/react-checkbox`'s own source (`node_modules/@radix-ui/react-checkbox`), which explicitly calls `event.preventDefault()` on Enter and only toggles on Space — a deliberate, spec-compliant choice matching the WAI-ARIA `checkbox` pattern (Enter is conventionally reserved for form submission; only Space toggles a checkbox). This isn't a bug introduced by this session's changes and wasn't altered — Space already toggles the checkbox correctly once arrow-key navigation lands focus on it. Flagged to the user rather than silently overridden, since bypassing it would work against an established accessibility convention.

Verified eslint-clean (3-error/5-warning baseline unchanged for `agent-next-gen-customers-table.tsx`) and tsc-clean (70-error baseline unchanged, app-wide, and 3-error baseline unchanged, lyra-ui's own check).

## 46. New Outbound search field: real `<label>` instead of placeholder (lyra-ui `CreateNew`, all consumers)

Per explicit request, with a screenshot: "for the new outbound search input add a label: 'Enter phone, email or search term' and remove the placeholder text (keep the search icon)." This is the top-level Favorites/All-contacts search field in lyra-ui's `CreateNew` "New Outbound" popover (`create-new.tsx`) — a different field from the per-category drill-down search (`CreateNewCategory.searchPlaceholder`), which was left untouched since it wasn't part of this request.

`CreateNewOutboundConfig.searchPlaceholder` was renamed to `searchLabel` (field, doc comment, and the app's own `OUTBOUND_CONFIG.searchLabel` in `agent-next-gen-outbound-data.tsx`) to match what it now actually drives — `Input`'s own `label` prop (rendered as a real `<label>` above the field, `Input`'s existing, already-ADA-compliant pattern) instead of `placeholder`. `startIcon` (the search glyph) was untouched, satisfying "keep the search icon."

Two follow-on fixes required by this change, both found and fixed before it could be considered done:

1. **Autofocus selector broke.** All 3 pages' (`AgentNextGenPage.tsx`, `AgentWorkspace2WithDeskPage.tsx`, `AgentWorkspaceAdvancedPage.tsx`) `onDocumentClick` effect that autofocuses this field when the "New Outbound" trigger is clicked located it via `input[placeholder="${OUTBOUND_CONFIG.searchPlaceholder}"]` — which stops matching anything once the field has no placeholder at all. Fixed at the root rather than patched around: gave the `Input` an explicit, stable `id="new-outbound-search"` in `create-new.tsx` (instead of its default auto-generated `useId()` one, which isn't a fixed value a separate consumer could hardcode), and swapped all 3 pages' selectors to the plain, robust `#new-outbound-search` — no longer even needs `OUTBOUND_CONFIG` in the selector at all, and won't break again if the label copy changes further.
2. **Clear-search "X" button mispositioned.** The button that clears the field (`agent-next-gen-outbound-data.tsx`... rather `create-new.tsx`'s own overlay `<button aria-label="Clear search">`) was positioned via `top-1/2 -translate-y-1/2` against an outer wrapper that, before this change, contained only the input row — once `Input` started rendering a real label above the field inside that same wrapper, centering against the wrapper's now-taller full height floated the button up between the label and the field instead of over the field. Fixed by anchoring from the bottom instead (`bottom-1.5`, i.e. `(input row's h-9 − button's own h-6) / 2`) — correct regardless of whether a label renders above, since the input row is always flush with the wrapper's bottom edge.

A stray reference to the old field name also surfaced as 2 new tsc errors in `AgentNextGenPage.pre-split-backup.tsx` (an inert, unimported historical snapshot kept for reference, not part of the running app) — updated to match rather than left broken, restoring the exact 70-error app-wide baseline.

Verified eslint-clean (2-error/4-warning baseline unchanged, all 3 page files; 2-error/9-warning baseline unchanged, `agent-next-gen-outbound-data.tsx`, confirmed via `git stash` diff) and tsc-clean (70-error baseline unchanged app-wide, 3-error baseline unchanged lyra-ui's own check).

## 47. New Outbound: "Dial Pad" as a small ghost button below the group Select, not a dropdown entry (lyra-ui `CreateNew`, all consumers)

Per explicit request, with a screenshot of a small grid icon + "Dial Pad" text: "add a ghost button small for dial pad below the select dropdown (don't underline Dial Pad)." Before this change "Dial Pad" only existed as a `kind: "dialpad"` entry in `OUTBOUND_GROUPS` (`agent-next-gen-outbound-data.tsx`) that was deliberately hidden from the "Choose group" dropdown entirely, per an earlier "hide it, don't destroy it" request (`HIDDEN_OUTBOUND_GROUP_IDS`) — there was no button, link, or any other render site for it anywhere in either repo.
