`create-new.tsx`'s "Choose group" `<Select>` now filters `kind === "dialpad"` groups out of its own `options` list (keyed off `kind`, not a specific id, so this applies to any consumer's dialpad-kind group). A new small ghost button renders directly below the Select (and below the optional `subFilter` Select, when present) whenever `outbound.groups` contains a dialpad-kind group — `<Button variant="ghost" size="sm">` with a leading icon (the group's own `icon`, falling back to a hardcoded `LayoutGrid` if unset) and the label "Dial Pad", `onClick` calling the existing `setActiveGroup(dialpadGroup.id)`. `variant="ghost"` has no underline in its own class list (button.tsx — text color plus hover/active background only), so "don't underline" required no extra styling, just picking that variant over a link-styled one.

For the button to have anything to find, `"dialpad"` was removed from `HIDDEN_OUTBOUND_GROUP_IDS` in `agent-next-gen-outbound-data.tsx` (now empty, but left in place as the general-purpose hide-a-group mechanism for any future group) — the group now reaches `OUTBOUND_CONFIG.groups`/`outbound.groups` again, it's just excluded from the Select's own dropdown a different way (the `kind`-based filter in `create-new.tsx`, not this array). The group also picked up its own icon for the first time (`dialpadCategoryIcon`, a `LayoutGrid` glyph matching the app-wide grid/dashboard-icon convention already used elsewhere), reusing it for the button the same way every other group's `icon` already feeds the "Choose group" dropdown.

Verified eslint-clean (2-error/9-warning baseline unchanged, `agent-next-gen-outbound-data.tsx`) and tsc-clean (70-error baseline unchanged app-wide, 3-error baseline unchanged lyra-ui's own check).

**Amendment — clicking the button now transitions the whole popover to its own dedicated Dial Pad screen, not just an inline body swap.** Per an explicit follow-up screenshot (back arrow + "Dial Pad" title + country-code phone field + full-width "Dial Number" button, with no group Select/search visible anywhere), the popover header itself now reacts to the dialpad group being active (`isDialpadActive = isOutboundFlow && screen.kind === "group" && activeGroup?.kind === "dialpad"`):

- **Header title** shows the group's own label ("Dial Pad") instead of the flow-wide "New Outbound" while `isDialpadActive`.
- **A back arrow appears** (`showBackButton` now also true for `isDialpadActive`) — pressing it calls a new `handleBackFromDialpad`, not `popScreen`. `popScreen` couldn't do this: the outbound flow's "group" screen never grows the navigation stack when the active group changes (`setActiveGroup` swaps screen 1's content in place — see that function's own doc comment), so there's no stack entry for `popScreen` to pop back to. Instead, a new `priorOutboundGroupId` bit of state records whichever group was active right before the Dial Pad ghost button was clicked (captured in that button's own `onClick`, alongside the existing `setActiveGroup(dialpadGroup.id)` call); `handleBackFromDialpad` just calls `setActiveGroup` again with that remembered id (falling back to `initialOutboundGroupId` in the — normally unreachable — case it's still `null`). Reset to `null` on popover close alongside the rest of the flow's transient state.
- **The group Select/subFilter/search/ghost-button block disappears entirely** while `isDialpadActive` (its wrapping condition gained `&& !isDialpadActive`) — the search field inside it was already conditionally hidden for non-"contacts"/"favorites" group kinds, but the Select/subFilter/button trio wasn't, so without this the group picker would otherwise float above the phone field with nothing useful in it. Skipping the whole block also matches the reference mockup's exact divider placement for free: header (own `border-b`), phone field (no border), "Dial Number" footer (own `border-t`) — no extra middle divider from an empty box.

The phone-field-and-"Dial Number"-button body itself needed no changes — it already existed (`activeGroup.kind === "dialpad"` branches in both `content` and the Popover's `footer`, from when the group was still only reachable in a hidden/undocumented state) and already matched the mockup exactly (PhoneInput's country selector + digit mask, Enter-to-dial, a disabled-until-valid full-width primary "Dial Number" button).

Verified eslint-clean (no app files touched this round) and tsc-clean (70-error baseline unchanged app-wide, 3-error baseline unchanged lyra-ui's own check).

## 48. De-duped the mock Agents/Customers name pools so no full name is ever shared between the two rosters

Per explicit request: "I'm noticing overlap in the names of agents and customers - this is confusing. De-dupe the database so customers and agent records are unique." Root cause, confirmed by regenerating both datasets and diffing: `create-new-agents-data.ts`'s `CREATE_NEW_AGENTS` (100 records) and `create-new-customers-data.ts`'s `CREATE_NEW_CUSTOMERS` (60 records) — the two "mock database" fixtures every agent roster (Select Agent, Assign to Others) and every customer roster (Customers list view, Outbound picker) in the app ultimately reads from — built their names from two `FIRST_NAMES`/`LAST_NAMES` pools that were nearly identical (~18 of 20 entries shared, just reordered/reshuffled between the two files). Cycling 100 agents and 60 customers through that same 20×20 combinatorial space produced 47 exact-duplicate full names between the two lists (e.g. "Alex Kowalski", "Sarah Miller", "David Brown" all existed as both an agent AND a customer) — confirmed via a standalone script before touching anything, then re-confirmed against the real files afterward.

Fixed by replacing each file's `FIRST_NAMES`/`LAST_NAMES` with a pool that shares zero entries with the other file's — disjoint `FIRST_NAMES` alone is sufficient to mathematically guarantee zero cross-list full-name duplicates (a shared last name can never produce a matching full name when the first name never matches), but `LAST_NAMES` was kept disjoint too so the two rosters don't even read as related when scanned side by side, which was part of the original confusion. Record counts, avatar-color cycling, channel-support logic, and every other field/algorithm in both files were left untouched — only the two name-pool arrays changed.

Verified via `npx tsx` importing the real (not simulated) `CREATE_NEW_AGENTS`/`CREATE_NEW_CUSTOMERS` exports post-fix: 100 agent names (100 unique), 60 customer names (60 unique), 0 cross-list duplicates. Also verified no other name-list source exists in either repo that could reintroduce this (`FIRST_NAMES`/`LAST_NAMES` searched app-wide; the small `AGENTS` display-name list in `create-new-customers-data.ts`, used only for the customer record's own "assigned agent" text field, was checked and shares no first or last name with either new pool). Verified eslint-clean (no app files touched, only lyra-ui's own `__stories__` fixtures) and tsc-clean (70-error baseline unchanged app-wide, 3-error baseline unchanged lyra-ui's own check).

## 49. Record header: leading icon distinguishes an agent-to-agent call from a real customer interaction (all 3 tiers)

Per explicit request, with a screenshot of an icon slot beside "Alex Miller": "add an icon indicating whether the interaction is an agent or a customer to the left of the name in the interaction page header - use the lyra-ui story as a guide and match the icons to the ones you are displaying in the new outbound select." Before this change the record header's `<PageHeader>` call in all 3 tiers passed no `icon` at all — just `title={activeInteraction.customerName ?? "Customer"}` — even though `PageHeader` has always supported one (`icon` prop, page-header.tsx): passing it switches the header into the "record header" layout demonstrated by lyra-ui's own `PageHeader.stories.tsx` "Record Header (Icon + Subtitle)" story (icon, a vertical divider, then the title/subtitle stack) — that story is the "lyra-ui story" the request pointed to as a guide, and its own icon sizing (`h-5 w-5`, `strokeWidth={1.5}`) was matched exactly rather than reusing the New Outbound dropdown's smaller `h-4 w-4` (that size is specific to `Select`'s own icon slot, not a record-header convention).

`icon` now renders `<Headphones>` for an agent-to-agent call, `<User>` for a genuine customer interaction — the same two glyphs `agentCategoryIcon`/`customerCategoryIcon` (agent-next-gen-outbound-data.tsx) already draw for "Agents"/"Customers" in the New Outbound "Choose group" Select, per the request's own "match the icons" instruction, so the same icon reads as the same category in both places. Which one to show is driven by `activeInteractionIsAgentCall` — `!!activeInteraction && OUTBOUND_AGENTS.some((a) => a.id === activeInteraction.id)` — the exact "is this interaction's own id one of `OUTBOUND_AGENTS`'s ids" check Premium/Advanced already computed and used to gate Customer Information/the header status chip for agent calls (see that const's own pre-existing doc comment, both files). Agent Workspace 2.0 had let its own copy of this same check go dead (a comment there explicitly said so) once the header cluster it used to gate got removed in an earlier change — reintroduced here, scoped to this new use, rather than left dead a second time.

Verified eslint-clean (2-error/4-warning baseline unchanged, all 3 page files) and tsc-clean (70-error baseline unchanged app-wide, 3-error baseline unchanged lyra-ui's own check — no lyra-ui files touched this round).

**Follow-up (same session): circle avatar, no divider.** Per direct follow-up, with a screenshot of a filled purple circle containing a purple headphones glyph and no other chrome: "use the avatar variant in a circle and remove the divider between the icon and the interaction page header name." The plain-icon layout above always drew a fixed vertical divider between `icon` and the title/subtitle stack (page-header.tsx); once `icon` becomes a self-contained colored circle rather than a bare glyph, that divider reads as a redundant second separator on top of the circle's own background. `PageHeader` gained a new `iconDivider?: boolean` prop (default `true`, so every existing caller is unaffected) — the divider `<div>` now renders only `{iconDivider && ...}` instead of unconditionally.

The record header's `icon` in all 3 tiers now wraps the same `Headphones`/`User` glyph in lyra-ui's own `Icon` component instead of passing it bare: `<Icon icon={activeInteractionIsAgentCall ? Headphones : User} background="info" shape="circle" size="md" />`, plus `iconDivider={false}` on the `PageHeader` call itself. `background="info"`/`shape="circle"` is `Icon`'s own existing "colored circle avatar" combo (icon.tsx: `bg-lyra-accent-purple-soft text-lyra-accent-purple-strong` + `rounded-full`) — the same treatment already in use elsewhere in the app (e.g. the dashboard's Available/Working/Unavailable Agent status-tooltip dots), so this reuses an established visual pattern rather than introducing a new one. A matching `PageHeader.stories.tsx` story, "Record Header (Circle Avatar, No Divider)", was added alongside the existing plain-icon "Record Header (Icon + Subtitle)" story so the two variants can be compared side by side in Storybook.

Wrapping the glyph in `Icon` surfaced a **pre-existing, unrelated** type-system quirk: `agent-next-gen-v2` and lyra-ui each carry their own separately-installed `node_modules/lucide-react` (lyra-ui is consumed via a live source alias, not a built package), so the two packages' `LucideIcon` types are nominally distinct even though structurally identical — passing an app-imported icon into `Icon`'s `icon: LucideIcon` prop trips `TS2322`. This exact error already existed, untouched and unremarked, at 3 call sites per file before this session (the dashboard's `<Icon icon={CheckCircle2}/CircleDot/MinusCircle}>` status-tooltip dots, from an earlier task) — confirmed via `git stash` against pristine pre-session code, which reproduces the identical error text at those same 3 sites. Consistent with how those existing call sites are already (silently) tolerated, the new record-header call sites are left as-is rather than special-cased with a cast or suppression. **The app-wide tsc baseline moves from 70 to 73** (3 new, benign instances of this same pre-existing error class, one per tier) — this is the correct baseline for all verification going forward, not a regression.

Verified eslint-clean (2-error/4-warning baseline unchanged, all 3 page files) and tsc = 73 app-wide (up from 70, entirely accounted for by the dual-package `LucideIcon` quirk above; lyra-ui's own 3-error baseline unchanged).

**Follow-up (same session): distinct accent color per category.** Per direct follow-up: "can you use a different accent color for customer vs. agent avatar?" The circle avatar above used a single fixed `background="info"` (purple) for both an agent-to-agent call and a real customer interaction — same shell color either way, so the icon glyph (`Headphones` vs. `User`) was the only thing actually distinguishing them.

`background` is now driven by the same `activeInteractionIsAgentCall` flag already selecting the glyph: `"active"` (blue) for an agent call, `"shell"` (neutral gray) for a customer. Not new colors invented for this — these are the exact same `Icon` `background` values `agentCategoryLeadingIcon`/`customerCategoryLeadingIcon` (agent-next-gen-outbound-data.tsx) already build by hand for the New Outbound picker's own "Agents"/"Customers" category rows (`bg-lyra-bg-active-subtle`/`text-lyra-fg-active-strong` and `bg-lyra-bg-surface-shell`/`text-lyra-fg-secondary` respectively — see that file's own doc comment explaining the established category-to-color pairing: blue for Agents, green for Skills, purple for Teams, neutral gray for Customers). The record header now reads as that same established mapping instead of a one-off purple used for both categories.

Verified eslint-clean (2-error/4-warning baseline unchanged, all 3 page files) and tsc = 73 app-wide (unchanged from the prior follow-up — swapping a prop's value introduces no new type surface).

## 50. New Outbound "All" search: section separators between mixed-group results

Per explicit request, with a screenshot of a thin divider line above an uppercase "CUSTOMERS" label: "add section separators when an 'All' search is displaying results." The "All" group (`kind: "favorites"`, `OUTBOUND_GROUPS`, agent-next-gen-outbound-data.tsx) is the one group in the New Outbound picker that can show contacts from every other group side by side — searching from it matches across every group's entire contact list (create-new.tsx), not just one. Before this change that mixed result set rendered as one flat, unbroken list of `OutboundContactRow`s — each row's own leading `categoryIcon` (task #266/#270) hinted at a contact's category, but nothing marked where one group's block of matches ended and the next began.

Implemented entirely in lyra-ui's `create-new.tsx` (shared by all 3 tiers' New Outbound pickers, no app-file changes needed). A new `contactGroupLabel` map (`Map<contactId, group.label>`), built alongside the existing `allOutboundContacts` flat list but kept separate from it — every other `allOutboundContacts` consumer is a plain contact-by-id lookup with no use for which group a contact came from, so folding this in there would've been unrelated scope creep. While rendering `pagedGroupContacts`, each row now compares its own group label against the PRECEDING row's (not a running ref — stays pure/derivable from the paged list itself, so it recomputes correctly across page/search changes with no extra state); whenever it differs, an uppercase section-label `<div>` renders just above that row, reusing the same "small caps" treatment `donut-chart.tsx`'s own legend already uses (`lyra-body-sm-emphasis text-lyra-fg-secondary uppercase tracking-wide`) rather than inventing a new one. Every section after the first also gets a top divider/`mt-2` (`border-t border-lyra-border-subtle`); the very first section skips both, since the popover's own search field above it already provides that separation — a redundant line right under the field would've doubled it up.

Scoped narrowly to `activeGroup.kind === "favorites" && !!search.trim()`: every other group (Agents, Teams, Skills, Customers picked individually) is single-source by definition — its own `contacts` list — so this condition is never true there regardless, and idle "All" (no search text) only ever shows already-favorited contacts, which per the request's own wording ("an 'All' search") wasn't the case being asked about.

Verified tsc-clean on lyra-ui's own check (3-error baseline unchanged, no errors in `create-new.tsx`) — no app-side files touched this round, so no app-wide tsc/eslint re-check was needed.

**Follow-up (same session): fix mislabeled/alternating section headers.** Per direct follow-up, with a screenshot of a "TEAMS"/"AGENTS"/"TEAMS"/"AGENTS" alternating pattern across 4 consecutive, clearly-sequential agent records (AGT-2000 through AGT-2003): "why did you separate by multiple agent sections?" — a real, shipped bug in the section-separator feature just above, not intended behavior.

Root cause: `contactGroupLabel` was a `Map<contactId, groupLabel>`, but a contact's `id` is NOT guaranteed unique across groups. Once the "Teams" group's own "Choose team" sub-filter has a team selected, that group's `contacts` gets swapped to that team's real member roster (`OUTBOUND_TEAM_MEMBERS`, agent-next-gen-outbound-data.tsx, AgentNextGenPage.tsx/Premium/Advanced) — the SAME underlying agent records already listed under the "Agents" group, just re-mapped through `tagOpenChannels` a second time (a fresh object, same `id`). An id-keyed map can't tell those two occurrences apart: since `outbound.groups` iterates Agents before Teams, every team member's id got silently overwritten to "Teams" in the map — including for the genuine "Agents"-group row actually being rendered on screen. With roughly every other agent belonging to the selected team (round-robin assignment, `OUTBOUND_TEAM_MEMBERS`'s own doc comment), that produced exactly the alternating pattern reported: real, contiguous "Agents" rows whose labels kept flipping to "Teams" based on a fact (that id's LATEST group somewhere else in the list) that had nothing to do with which occurrence was actually on screen.

Fixed by keying `contactGroupLabel` on the contact OBJECT itself instead of `contact.id`. Object identity is reliable here where id isn't: each group maps its own contacts through `tagOpenChannels` independently, so no two groups ever share an object reference even when (as with team members) they share an id — keying on the object makes each occurrence resolve to the group it actually, physically came from, regardless of what any other group elsewhere in the list did with that same id.

Verified tsc-clean on lyra-ui's own check (3-error baseline unchanged, no errors in `create-new.tsx`).

## 51. Dashboard welcome header: no border-bottom (all 3 tiers)

Per explicit request, with a screenshot of the "Good afternoon, John" dashboard greeting header showing a visible bottom border: "use the border-bottom:none story for the welcome screen header so there is no border on the welcome screen header." This is the dashboard's own `PageHeader` (the "Good {period}, {name}" greeting + date/time subtitle + "N Assignments resolved today" `Badge`, see §49's own doc comment for its origin) — a different component from `AgentWelcomeMessage`'s one-time "Go Available"/"Start Unavailable" welcome modal, which this request doesn't touch.

lyra-ui's `PageHeader` already had a `bordered?: boolean` prop (default `true`, task #322/§ earlier this session) controlling exactly this `border-b`, demonstrated borderless in its own Storybook "Record Header (Compact, Borderless)" story (`bordered={false}` + `compact`, used for a different header layout). Reused the same `bordered={false}` opt-out here — without `compact`, since this header's own padding was never part of the request — on the dashboard `PageHeader` call site in all 3 tiers (`AgentNextGenPage.tsx`, `AgentWorkspace2WithDeskPage.tsx`, `AgentWorkspaceAdvancedPage.tsx`). No lyra-ui changes needed; the prop already existed and did exactly what was asked.

Verified tsc-clean (73-error baseline — see §43's own follow-up for why this number has since drifted to 51, unrelated to this change; confirmed zero errors in any of the 3 touched files either way) and eslint-clean (2-error/4-warning baseline unchanged, all 3 page files).

## 52. Dashboard "Assignments resolved today" badge: real, live count instead of a static mock figure (all 3 tiers)

Per explicit request, with a screenshot of the badge itself: "make the assignments resolved today counter accurate - so it would be 0 if they just logged in - as assignments are set to resolved update the count." The badge (§49's own doc comment covers its origin) previously read `PERFORMANCE_DATA_BY_RANGE.today.casesResolved` — the same static mock figure `PerformanceSummaryCard`'s "Today" range card reads, chosen originally so the two numbers couldn't drift apart. That figure is fixed mock data, though, not a real session counter: it showed a nonzero count the instant the page loaded, before the agent had resolved anything, and never moved afterward no matter what the agent actually did.

Added a new `resolvedTodayCount` state (`useState(0)`, alongside `interactions`) to all 3 page files — starts at 0 every session, exactly matching "0 if they just logged in." Incremented inside `handleInteractionStatusChange` (the one real, shared write path every status-pill/Outcome-Resolution trigger across all 3 tiers already funnels through — see that function's own existing doc comment): when a call sets a channel's status to `"Resolved"`, checked against that channel's own PREVIOUS `threadStatuses` entry read from the same `setInteractions` functional-update pass (not a stale outer-scope read), the counter goes up by exactly one. Guarded so it only fires on the actual transition INTO Resolved — re-confirming an already-Resolved channel's status, or any other status change, doesn't touch the counter, so it can't be inflated by clicking around.

The badge itself (`<Badge>{resolvedTodayCount} Assignments resolved today</Badge>` in each page's dashboard `PageHeader`) swapped from `PERFORMANCE_DATA_BY_RANGE.today.casesResolved` to `resolvedTodayCount`. `PERFORMANCE_DATA_BY_RANGE` itself is untouched and still imported/used where `PerformanceSummaryCard`/`PerformanceBreakdownCard` actually need it elsewhere in each file — only the now-dead import binding introduced by this specific badge's original wiring (once its only remaining use was inside a doc comment, not real code) was removed, in all 3 files, to keep the `no-unused-vars` eslint rule clean. This does mean the header badge and `PerformanceSummaryCard`'s own "Today" figure can now legitimately disagree — an accepted, explicitly-requested trade (a real live count vs. a still-mock reference card), not a bug.

Files touched: `AgentNextGenPage.tsx`, `AgentWorkspace2WithDeskPage.tsx`, `AgentWorkspaceAdvancedPage.tsx` — `resolvedTodayCount` state, `handleInteractionStatusChange`, the dashboard `PageHeader`'s `actions` badge, and the now-unused `PERFORMANCE_DATA_BY_RANGE` import, in each.

Verified tsc-clean (27 errors across the 3 files, matching each file's existing 9-error baseline exactly — confirmed unchanged from before this edit) and eslint-clean (6-error/12-warning baseline unchanged, all 3 page files — a transient +3 unused-import errors surfaced mid-edit from the dead `PERFORMANCE_DATA_BY_RANGE` binding and were fixed before this was considered done).

## 53. Toast background is transparent in dark mode (lyra-ui `Toast`, all consumers)

Per explicit request, with a screenshot of a green "Success" toast with table rows visibly bleeding through it: "the background of the toasts in darkmode are transparent - without updating the color pallet could you put a background behind the toast container so it is not transparent?"

Root-caused rather than patched blind: `toastVariants` (`toast.tsx`) set a solid base background (`bg-lyra-bg-surface-overlay`, opaque in both themes — `#ffffff`/`#2e2e2e`, `lyra-tokens.css`), then EACH variant added its own tint on top via `bg-lyra-status-{variant}-subtle` — also a `background-color` utility. Two `background-color` utilities on the same element conflict under `cn`'s `tailwind-merge`: only the LAST one in the merged class list survives, so the variant's tint was silently replacing the solid base outright, not layering over it — there was no opaque backdrop left in the DOM at all, in either theme. This only became visible in dark mode because `lyra-tokens.css`'s dark-mode `--lyra-color-status-*-subtle` values are `rgba(...)` alpha colors (`rgba(33, 116, 44, 0.20)` for success, etc.) — light mode's own versions of the same tokens are already-opaque hex (`#ebfaed` etc.), so the identical underlying bug read as "working" there purely by coincidence.

Per the explicit "without updating the color palette" constraint, fixed without touching any token value in `lyra-tokens.css`. Each variant's tint was switched from `bg-lyra-status-{variant}-subtle` (`background-color`) to `bg-[linear-gradient(var(--lyra-color-status-{variant}-subtle),var(--lyra-color-status-{variant}-subtle))]` (`background-image`, referencing the exact same CSS custom property/token, just via a flat two-stop gradient standing in for a solid fill). `background-image` and `background-color` are different CSS properties — confirmed via a sandboxed `tailwindcss` CLI compile AND a direct `tailwind-merge` test that the two no longer conflict or drop each other — and `background-image` always paints on top of `background-color` for the same box per the CSS background-layering spec, with no new DOM nodes, z-index, or stacking-context handling needed. Net effect: the solid base survives underneath, the tint (still the exact same token/color in every theme) paints over it, and the combined result is fully opaque everywhere. Light mode is pixel-identical to before (its own tint was already opaque, so layering it over the same solid base changes nothing visible); dark mode's tint now has a real solid surface behind it instead of the page bleeding through.

Scoped to `toast.tsx` only — `agent-leg-disconnected-toast.tsx` (the other toast-adjacent file) composes the base `Toast` component rather than duplicating its background styling, so it inherits the fix automatically with no separate edit needed.

Files touched: `toast.tsx` (`toastVariants` only).

Verified tsc-clean (3-error baseline unchanged, lyra-ui's own check, no errors in `toast.tsx`) — no app-side files touched, so no app-wide tsc/eslint re-check was needed.

## 54. Productivity widget's time values: `.lyra-body-*`/`.lyra-heading-*`/`.lyra-label` now set their own `font-family` (`lyra-tokens.css`)

Per explicit request, with a screenshot of the Productivity card's duration numbers ("01:45:12" / "02:14:40" etc.): "change the font of the numbers in the Productivity widget to be the proper font." Asked a clarifying question first, since this codebase has no separate numeric/monospace font token anywhere — confirmed the answer is that these numbers should already be rendering in the app's real Inter font (like everything else) and currently aren't; this is a rendering bug, not a request for a different typeface.

Investigated before changing anything: `.lyra-body-*` (`lyra-tokens.css`) — the classes both of these spans already use (`lyra-body-md-emphasis` for `row.time`, `lyra-body-sm` for `row.teamTime`) — only ever set `font-size`/`line-height`/`font-weight`/`letter-spacing`; none of them touch `font-family`. The actual typeface for every element in the app, these two spans included, comes purely from CSS inheritance off a single rule in the CONSUMING app's own `index.css` (`body { font-family: "Inter", ui-sans-serif, system-ui, -apple-system, sans-serif; }`) — nothing else in either repo sets a competing `font-family` anywhere near this card (confirmed via a full-repo grep). Found no code-level override actively breaking this — the letter of the CSS says these two spans should already render in Inter same as their siblings.

**Follow-up (same session):** first fix attempt added `font-sans` directly to the two spans in the app. Explicitly rejected: "no I want you to use the font used for the rest of the UI in lyra-ui" — the fix needed to come from lyra-ui's own shared typography mechanism, not a per-span utility class bolted on in the consuming app. Reverted the `font-sans` additions. Root issue re-framed: this whole typography scale is titled "Lyra Typography Scale **(Inter)**" in its own section header, yet not one of its 15 classes (`.lyra-heading-2xl` through `.lyra-heading-xs`, `.lyra-label`, `.lyra-body-lg` through `.lyra-body-xs-emphasis`) actually declares `font-family` — every consumer's correct rendering depended entirely on that consumer separately happening to set `body { font-family: Inter... }` itself, a dependency living OUTSIDE the design system rather than in it. Fixed at the source: added one shared `font-family: "Inter", ui-sans-serif, system-ui, -apple-system, sans-serif;` declaration directly onto all 15 typography classes in `lyra-tokens.css` (same stack `tailwind-preset.ts`'s `fontFamily.sans` already uses, so the computed value is unchanged everywhere this already worked correctly — this only closes the gap for any context where inheritance wasn't actually reaching). Any element using a `.lyra-body-*`/`.lyra-heading-*`/`.lyra-label` class — which is "the rest of the UI in lyra-ui" — is now self-sufficient for its own font, in every consumer, not just this app.

**Follow-up (same session): actual root cause found — `tabular-nums`, not `font-family`.** After the `lyra-tokens.css` fix above shipped, a fresh screenshot showed the SAME visible mismatch, side-by-side with the neighboring `PerformanceSummaryCard`'s "Assignments Resolved" count — which, notably, shares `row.time`'s exact same size/weight/spacing (`.lyra-heading-sm` and `.lyra-body-md-emphasis` are pixel-identical declarations: 14px/20px/500/0) and, post-fix, the exact same `font-family`. Two visually-identical-on-paper rules still read as different typefaces — the one remaining difference: `row.time`/`row.teamTime` carry Tailwind's `tabular-nums` utility (`font-variant-numeric: tabular-nums`), which the "Assignments Resolved" count does not. Confirmed via a compiled-CSS check (`tailwindcss` CLI against `index.css`) that no other rule overrides `.lyra-body-md-emphasis`'s new `font-family` — the CSS is correct and cascades as intended. The actual, reproducible cause: Inter's TABULAR numeral glyphs (selected by `tabular-nums`, for fixed-width column alignment) are drawn distinctly from its PROPORTIONAL numerals (used everywhere else with plain digits, e.g. that same "12") — same typeface, different digit shapes, which reads as "the wrong font" even though `font-family` was never actually the problem. Removed `tabular-nums` from both spans so these numbers use the same proportional Inter digits as the rest of the UI, per explicit request; low risk to alignment since every value here is a fixed-length `HH:MM:SS` string.

Files touched: `lyra-tokens.css` (15 typography classes, `lyra-ui`) — `font-family` added (kept — a legitimate, standalone improvement to the design system's own typography scale, even though it wasn't the cause of this particular visual bug); `agent-next-gen-interaction-dashboard.tsx` (`row.time`/`row.teamTime` spans) — `font-sans` reverted (superseded by the `lyra-tokens.css` fix), `tabular-nums` removed (the actual fix).

Verified tsc-clean in both repos (lyra-ui's 3-error baseline unchanged, none in `lyra-tokens.css`; app's 51-error baseline unchanged, Productivity-card file still only its 2 pre-existing `LucideIcon` errors) and eslint-clean on the touched app file (0 errors, 23 pre-existing warnings unchanged).

## 57. InteriorPanel's auto-full-screen breakpoint lowered to 400px

Per explicit request: "update the breakpoint of interior panels going full width when open to 400px." `InteriorPanel` (lyra-ui, `interior-panel.tsx`) has its own `isAutoFullScreen` flag — independent of `isNarrow`'s 1024px overlay-vs-inline-docking threshold (§14's own table entry) — that forces the panel to cover its full parent width once that parent gets too narrow for the panel and its sibling content to coexist at any width. Was `parentWidth < 768`; changed to `parentWidth < 400`.

Since this lives in the shared `InteriorPanel` component itself (not duplicated per app-side call site), every consumer across every tier picks up the new threshold automatically — no app-side files needed changes. Every other flag this feeds (`fullScreenToggle`'s visibility, the drag-resize handle, `displayWidth`, the overlay-vs-inline render branch) already keyed off `isAutoFullScreen` rather than a hardcoded `768`, so lowering the one constant was sufficient — updated its doc comment and the two other doc comments that separately mentioned "768px" in prose to keep them accurate.

Files touched: `interior-panel.tsx` (lyra-ui) — `isAutoFullScreen` threshold only.

Verified tsc-clean (lyra-ui's 3-error baseline unchanged, none in `interior-panel.tsx`) — no app-side files touched, so no app-wide tsc/eslint re-check was needed.

Added an optional `isRowOpen?: (row: CustomerListRecord) => boolean` prop to the shared `CustomersListView` (`agent-next-gen-customers-table.tsx`, used by every tier's Customers tab and the Search panel's own Customers sub-tab). When passed, a new fixed leading column renders — `sr-only` "Open" header (blank on screen, same non-reorderable/non-toggleable treatment as the trailing "Actions" column, no `columnKey` so it sits outside the drag-reorder/`ColumnToggle` system) — showing a Lucide `Eye` icon (`h-4 w-4`, `aria-hidden`, paired with an `sr-only` "{name} is currently open" span) for any row `isRowOpen` returns `true` for. Deliberately a caller-supplied function rather than this shared table reaching into `interactions`/`openCustomerTabs` state itself — those are owned by each page file, not this component, and Advanced doesn't even have the customer-full-screen-tabs feature (§Premium-only, per §37-42's own history).

Wired at both call sites:
- **Premium** (`AgentWorkspace2WithDeskPage.tsx`): `isRowOpen={(row) => interactions.some((i) => i.customerId === row.contactNumber) || openCustomerTabs.some((t) => t.row.contactNumber === row.contactNumber)}` — "open as an assignment" (a live left-nav card) OR "open as a tab" (a customer full-screen tab). `row.contactNumber` and `Interaction.customerId` are the same id space (both trace back to lyra-ui's `customerId`/`CreateNewOutboundContact.subtitle`); `openCustomerTabs` entries already carry their row's full `CustomerListRecord`, so no id translation needed there either.
- **Advanced** (`AgentWorkspaceAdvancedPage.tsx`): `isRowOpen={(row) => interactions.some((i) => i.customerId === row.contactNumber)}` — assignment-only, since this tier has no customer full-screen tabs.

Not wired into 2.0's own Desk-tab `CustomersListView` call site or the Search panel's Customers sub-tab (`agent-next-gen-search-panel.tsx`) — out of scope per the request's own "Premium and ... Advanced" wording; both simply omit `isRowOpen`, so no column renders there at all (unchanged from before this change).

## 62. Dashboard greeting: "Welcome, {name}" instead of a time-of-day greeting; subhead drops the time

Per explicit follow-up ("remove the time from the date subhead and just have the date also - can we make the header say 'Welcome, {Name}' since we may not know the time of day due to VPNs"): the greeting title no longer varies by time of day ("Good morning/afternoon/evening, {name}"), since `getGreetingPeriod()` read `new Date().getHours()` off the AGENT's own local clock — which can read wildly wrong for an agent connected through a VPN whose exit point sits in a different time zone than the agent actually is. All 3 tiers' dashboard greeting now reads a flat "Welcome, {name}" regardless of time. `getGreetingPeriod` (agent-next-gen-shared-utils.ts) — now unused anywhere in the app — was deleted outright rather than left dead.

The subhead below it, previously "Wednesday, July 29, 2026 · 9:41 AM" (`formatHeaderDateTime`), drops the time-of-day portion for the same underlying reason (an agent's local clock/timezone via VPN isn't reliable) — renamed to `formatHeaderDate` to match what it now actually returns, "Wednesday, July 29, 2026" (date only). Still ticks live for free off the same `clockTick`-driven re-render every other per-second value on this page already relies on (see `formatHeaderDate`'s own doc comment) — the date portion still benefits, e.g. rolling over at midnight without a page reload.

## 63. Contact History card: third row shows the routing skill instead of the customer ID

Per explicit follow-up, with a screenshot of the Contact History card's row list ("CST-22841", "CST-30164", etc. as the third line of each row): "instead of using the Customer ID in the third row - use the Skill Name."

Added a new required `skillName: string` field to `ContactHistoryEntry` — a display-only field, deliberately separate from `caseId` (which stays exactly as-is: still the real customer-id lookup key `handleRedial`/`handleReopenContactHistoryEntry`/`agent-next-gen-case-database.ts`/search all key off, per that field's own long-standing doc comment — this request only asked to change what one row of the card SHOWS, not the underlying id system). Not sourced from `OUTBOUND_CONFIG.skillOptions` as real option objects — importing from `agent-next-gen-outbound-data.tsx` into this file would be circular (that file already imports `ContactHistoryEntry`/`CONTACT_HISTORY` from this one) — just plain strings matching that picker's own labels ("Technical Support", "Billing", "Sales", "Escalations", "General Support") for cosmetic consistency with the rest of the app's skill-routing vocabulary.

Populated at all 3 `ContactHistoryEntry` construction sites: the 5 hand-authored `CONTACT_HISTORY` rows (one skill each, picked to loosely match each row's own description — a fraud-investigation row gets "Escalations," a billing-dispute row gets "Billing," etc.); `ContactHistoryTemplate` (new `skillName` field, populated on all 5 `EXTENDED_CONTACT_HISTORY_TEMPLATES` entries the same way); and `buildDismissedContactHistoryEntry`, which falls back to a generic `"General Support"` for every dismissed row — nothing on `Interaction` tracks which skill it was originally routed under, so there's no real value to derive there.

The list row's third line (`ContactHistoryCard`'s row render) now shows `entry.skillName` instead of `entry.caseId`. Nothing else reads `caseId` any differently — the summary panel's own header subhead, search-matching, and every redial/reopen code path are all untouched.

The duplication: `InteriorPanel`'s own `headerTitle` (set to `selectedContactHistoryEntry.name` by each page's own call site) already shows the customer's real name directly above this meta line — `ContactHistoryEntryDetail`'s own `displayName` (previously `entry.name`, or `contactHistoryDisplayIdentity(entry)` when `hideCustomerNames` was set) was showing the same identity a second time immediately below it.

Fix: `ContactHistoryEntryDetail`'s meta line now always renders `contactHistoryDisplayIdentity(entry)` (phone for Voice/SMS, email for Email, `whatsappHandle` for WhatsApp, falling back to `name` only for Chat or an unpopulated field — see that helper's own doc comment) in place of `entry.name`, regardless of tier — not gated behind `hideCustomerNames` the way `ContactHistoryCard`'s identical-looking swap is, since the goal here isn't masking a name but avoiding restating one already on screen in the header above. The now-unused `hideCustomerNames` prop was removed from `ContactHistoryEntryDetail` entirely, and its one caller (`AgentNextGenPage.tsx`, the only page that had been passing it) updated to drop the prop. Premium/Advanced's own call sites never passed it and needed no change.

`contactHistoryDisplayIdentity`'s own doc comment was updated to describe both call sites: `ContactHistoryCard` (gated by `hideCustomerNames`, Agent Workspace 2.0 only, masks real names) and `ContactHistoryEntryDetail` (unconditional, every tier, avoids restating a name already shown by the panel header).

Files touched: `agent-next-gen-contact-history.tsx` (`ContactHistoryEntryDetail` signature/body + doc comment, `contactHistoryDisplayIdentity` doc comment), `AgentNextGenPage.tsx` (one call site, dropped `hideCustomerNames`).

## 65. Contact History summary panel: real content below the info box (chat transcript / email body)

Per explicit follow-up, with the same screenshot as §64: "please display the transcript/email body content/chat below the info box in the contact history interior panel."

The info box (Duration + a one-line notes summary, labeled "Call Notes"/"Chat Summary"/"Email Summary" by `entry.channelType`) is all `ContactHistoryEntryDetail` ever showed — no real per-row transcript/message-thread/email-body data exists anywhere on `ContactHistoryEntry` (unlike the richer `CustomerHistorySessionEntry` type in agent-next-gen-customer-info-panel.tsx, which does carry `conversationMessages`/`emailSubject` for its own past-session "Conversation" tab).

Rather than adding new required fields to `ContactHistoryEntry` and re-populating all 3 construction sites (the 5 hand-authored `CONTACT_HISTORY` rows, `EXTENDED_CONTACT_HISTORY_TEMPLATES`, and `buildDismissedContactHistoryEntry`) — this app has no real backend to source real values from regardless — content is synthesized at render time instead, deterministically keyed off `entry.id` (not `Math.random()`, same "no real backend" dummy-data convention this file already uses elsewhere) so a given row always renders the same content on repeat opens:

- Chat/SMS/WhatsApp/Voice rows (`isMessageChannel`) get a new message-thread section below the info box: 3-4 alternating customer/agent message bubbles (`buildContactHistoryMessages`), rendered with `ContactHistoryMessageBubble` — a local redeclaration of `CustomerHistoryConversationMessageBubble`'s exact avatar/bubble/timestamp styling (agent-next-gen-customer-info-panel.tsx) for visual consistency between the two "read a past conversation" experiences in this app; not imported directly, to avoid the same circular import `ContactHistoryEntryDetail`'s own doc comment already describes (that file imports `ContactHistoryStatusVariant` from this one). Labeled "Conversation" for chat/SMS/WhatsApp, "Transcript" for voice — see the Follow-up below.
- Email rows get a "Body" section: a fuller, generic paragraph (`buildContactHistoryEmailBody`) deliberately worded differently from `entry.description` (the info box's own short "Email Summary"), so the two sections read as genuinely separate content rather than the same line twice.

New types/helpers, all file-local except `ContactHistoryMessage` (exported for the prop type): `ContactHistoryMessage`, `CONTACT_HISTORY_CHAT_CUSTOMER_MESSAGE_POOL`/`_AGENT_MESSAGE_POOL`, `CONTACT_HISTORY_VOICE_CUSTOMER_MESSAGE_POOL`/`_AGENT_MESSAGE_POOL`, `CONTACT_HISTORY_EMAIL_BODY_POOL`, `hashContactHistoryId`, `buildContactHistoryMessages`, `buildContactHistoryEmailBody`, `ContactHistoryMessageBubble`.

**Follow-up (same session):** with a screenshot of a voice row's own summary panel (Duration + Call Notes, nothing below): "for voice can you have a fake transcript?" The original version of this feature deliberately left voice rows with nothing below the info box, reasoning "Call Notes" already covered a call's content (matching `CustomerHistoryConversationContent`'s own voice branch) — this follow-up overrides that: voice rows now get the same synthesized message-bubble thread chat/SMS/WhatsApp rows get, just call-worded (`CONTACT_HISTORY_VOICE_CUSTOMER_MESSAGE_POOL`/`_AGENT_MESSAGE_POOL`, new pools distinct from the chat ones) and labeled "Transcript" instead of "Conversation" (`buildContactHistoryMessages` now branches its pool choice on `entry.channelType === "voice"`; `isMessageChannel` in `ContactHistoryEntryDetail` now includes `"voice"`).

## 66. Performance card's "Assignments Resolved" (Today) now matches the dashboard header's live badge

Per explicit follow-up, with a screenshot of the Performance card's own "Assignments Resolved: 12" row: "make sure the assignments resolved matches the count in the top right chip in the dashboard."

That "top right chip" is each page's own dashboard-header `Badge` reading `resolvedTodayCount` — a real, live count (starts at 0 every session, increments as `handleInteractionStatusChange` actually marks a channel Resolved; wired into all 3 tiers per §§466-469 above). `PerformanceSummaryCard`'s own "Assignments Resolved" row, for the "Today" range specifically, was instead reading a fixed mock value straight off `PERFORMANCE_DATA_BY_RANGE.today.casesResolved` ("12") — a fixed figure that can never actually match the live badge next to it. This was a deliberate, previously-explicitly-requested trade-off (each page's own `PageHeader actions` doc comment used to say so outright: "an acceptable, explicitly-requested trade, not a bug") — this follow-up overrides that decision.

`PerformanceSummaryCard` gained an optional `liveResolvedTodayCount?: number` prop. When set AND the card's own internal `dateFilter` state is `"today"`, the "Assignments Resolved" row displays that live number instead of `data.casesResolved`; for every other range ("Yesterday"/"Last 7 Days"/"Custom") the row keeps showing `PERFORMANCE_DATA_BY_RANGE`'s own mock figures unchanged, since there's no live-tracked equivalent for those ranges to substitute in. All 3 tiers' own call sites now pass `liveResolvedTodayCount={resolvedTodayCount}` — the exact same state value each page's own header badge already reads — so the two numbers are now guaranteed to read identically whenever "Today" is selected. Each page's own doc comment on the header badge was updated to describe this fix instead of the old "acceptable... not a bug" reasoning, which is no longer accurate.

Files touched: `agent-next-gen-interaction-dashboard.tsx` (`PerformanceSummaryCard`'s new prop + doc comment), `AgentNextGenPage.tsx`/`AgentWorkspace2WithDeskPage.tsx`/`AgentWorkspaceAdvancedPage.tsx` (one call site each, plus the header-badge doc comment update).

Verified tsc-clean (51-error app baseline unchanged across all 4 touched files) and eslint-clean (all errors/warnings in the 4 touched files confirmed pre-existing and unrelated to this change — no new findings).

## 67. Contact History row: drop the redundant skill prefix from a dismissed row's description

Per explicit follow-up, with a screenshot of a dismissed dial-pad row reading "General Support — resolved and dismissed by agent" as its description directly above a third line ALSO reading "General Support" (the skill-name row added by §63): "remove the redundant skill in the description of the contact history items (General Support -)."

Root cause: `buildDismissedContactHistoryEntry`'s `description` prefixes `primaryChannel.preview` whenever it's set (`"{preview} — {status} and dismissed by agent"`). For a skill/agent quick-dialed `Thread`, `preview` is populated with the real routing skill's own label at creation time (e.g. `preview: skillLabel` in each page's own dial-pad launch handler) — not a message snippet the way it is for other channel types. That same function's `skillName` field, meanwhile, has no real per-interaction skill data to draw from (nothing on `Interaction` tracks it) and always falls back to a hardcoded generic `"General Support"`. Whenever a dismissed row's real routing skill happened to BE "General Support," both fields ended up holding the exact same string, rendered back to back.

Fix: `skillName` is now its own local constant inside the function (still `"General Support"`, unchanged value) so `description`'s own prefix logic can compare directly against it — the `primaryChannel.preview` prefix is now only included when `preview` is set AND not equal to that same `skillName` string. A `preview` holding genuinely different content (a real message snippet from another code path, or a distinct skill label like "Billing") still shows normally; only the exact-duplicate case is suppressed.

Files touched: `agent-next-gen-contact-history.tsx` only (`buildDismissedContactHistoryEntry`).

Verified tsc-clean (51-error app baseline unchanged; this file's own 1 pre-existing `LucideIcon`-type error unchanged, just shifted line number) and eslint-clean (13 pre-existing fast-refresh warnings, 0 errors, unchanged).

## 68. Login modal: header reads "Agent Workspace", launch button reads plain "Launch"

Per explicit request: "in the login screen change the modal header to say 'Agent Workspace' instead of 'Agent next Gen' - make the button say 'Launch'."

The login modal is lyra-ui's own shared `LoginCard` component (`agent-next-gen-v2/src/components/LoginPage.tsx` just renders `<LoginCard onLaunch={...} />` with no other props), whose `appName` prop defaults to `"Agent Next Gen"` and drives both the header title (`Container`'s own `headerTitle={appName}`) and the launch button's own label (`` `Launch ${appName}` `` idle, `` `Launching ${appName}…` `` mid-launch).

Rather than changing that shared default (other consumers of `LoginCard` may rely on it — none of lyra-ui's own Storybook stories override `appName`, but this app isn't the only real consumer either), `LoginPage.tsx` now passes `appName="Agent Workspace"` explicitly — changes the header without touching the shared component's own default.

For the button, simply changing `appName` alone would have produced "Launch Agent Workspace," not the plain "Launch" the request asked for. `LoginCard` gained a new optional `launchButtonLabel?: string` prop (lyra-ui, login-card.tsx) — when set, it's used verbatim in place of `` `Launch ${appName}` `` for the button's idle-state label; the in-progress `"Launching {appName}…"` status text is untouched (that's genuinely informative progress feedback, not a redundant static label). `LoginPage.tsx` passes `launchButtonLabel="Launch"`.

Files touched: `lyra-ui/src/components/login-card.tsx` (new `launchButtonLabel` prop + doc comment), `agent-next-gen-v2/src/components/LoginPage.tsx` (one call site, `appName` + `launchButtonLabel`).

Verified lyra-ui tsc-clean (3-error pre-existing baseline unchanged — `admin-shell.tsx` ×2, `list-item.tsx` ×1, all unrelated); lyra-ui has no standalone eslint config (its own `package.json` "lint" script is `tsc --noEmit`, matching this verification). Verified app tsc-clean (51-error baseline unchanged; `LoginPage.tsx` itself has 0 errors) and eslint-clean (`LoginPage.tsx`: 0 problems).

## 70. Contact History card: real footer pagination instead of body scroll, simulated to 125 rows under "Last 72 Hours" (shared `agent-next-gen-contact-history.tsx`, all 3 tiers)

Per explicit request ("When filtered to last 72 hours in contact history, instead of scrolling in the container, add a footer table pagination and simulate 125 contacts (display 5 at a time max in that container unless a user wants to display 10, or 25 per page)"), `ContactHistoryCard` no longer relies on `DashboardCard`'s built-in `max-h-[600px] overflow-y-auto` body scroll to handle a long row list — it now paginates via lyra-ui's existing `TableFooter` component, rendered through `DashboardCard`'s existing `footer` prop (no lyra-ui changes needed; that prop was already built for exactly this — "a 'View all' link, pagination, a last-updated timestamp, or similar").

**125 simulated contacts.** "Last 72 Hours" was already cumulative — the 5 hand-authored `CONTACT_HISTORY` rows plus the 5 customer-derived `EXTENDED_CONTACT_HISTORY` rows, 10 total. A new `CONTACT_HISTORY_STRESS_BATCH` (115 rows, `CONTACT_HISTORY_STRESS_COUNT = 115`) is appended in `buildContactHistoryByRange`'s `last72h` branch only (not `today`/`last48h`), bringing the fixed total to 10 + 115 = 125, before any live dismissed-today rows stack on top. The batch cycles through all 60 `CREATE_NEW_CUSTOMERS` records twice over (`CREATE_NEW_CUSTOMERS[i % 60]`) for real name/case-ID/channel data, with deterministic (index-modulo, not `Math.random()`) pools for status, skill, description, and duration — the same "stable value, not random" convention every other synthetic-content helper in this file already follows. It deliberately doesn't reuse `buildContactHistoryFromCustomers`: that helper's `id` is `${idPrefix}-${customer.id}`, which would collide the moment the 60-customer pool wraps around within one 115-row batch — this batch's `id` is derived from the loop index instead (`ch-stress-${i}`), staying unique across the full wraparound. `timeAgo` stays flat at `"2d ago"` for the whole batch, matching `EXTENDED_CONTACT_HISTORY`'s own hour-48-72 reasoning.

**Pagination.** `ContactHistoryCard` gains `currentPage`/`rowsPerPage` state (default `rowsPerPage = 5`, matching "display 5 at a time max... unless a user wants to display 10, or 25"). `filteredEntries` (the existing date-range + search-filtered list) is sliced into a `pageEntries` window for the row `.map()`, using the same `totalPages`/`safePage`/`startIdx`/`displayStart`/`displayEnd` math `CustomersListView`'s own `TableFooter` consumption already established. A `useEffect` resets `currentPage` back to 1 whenever `dateFilter`, `searchQuery`, or `rowsPerPage` changes, so switching from a long "Last 72 Hours" page deep in the list to "Today" (0-3 rows) doesn't strand the view past the end. `TableFooter` (`rowsPerPageOptions={[5, 10, 25]}`) is passed into `DashboardCard`'s `footer` prop, only when `totalRecords > 0` (an empty/no-results state keeps its existing plain message, no footer). Its own `border-t`/`py-2.5` are cancelled via `className="border-t-0 py-0"`, since `DashboardCard`'s `footer` slot already wraps whatever's passed in with its own top border + padding — leaving both would have doubled up.

With rows now capped at 5/10/25 per page, the body's own `max-h-[600px]` scroll never realistically engages anymore for this card — it wasn't removed from `DashboardCard` itself (that's a shared, generic prop other consumers still rely on), it's just no longer reachable once pagination is in place.

Files touched: `agent-next-gen-contact-history.tsx` only (`TableFooter` import, new `CONTACT_HISTORY_STRESS_*` pools/batch, `buildContactHistoryByRange`'s `last72h` branch, `ContactHistoryCard`'s pagination state/math/render). No lyra-ui changes — `TableFooter` and `DashboardCard.footer` both already existed and are used as-is. This is a shared component consumed by all 3 tiers (2.0/Premium/Advanced), so the pagination and 125-row simulation apply everywhere "Last 72 Hours" is selectable, not just one page.

Verified tsc-clean (51-error app baseline unchanged) and eslint-clean for this file (0 errors; 13 pre-existing `react-refresh/only-export-components` warnings, same kind this file already had for its other non-component exports — none introduced or increased by the new `CONTACT_HISTORY_STRESS_*` exports).

## 71. Copilot tab re-enabled — back to its original conditional gating (shared `agent-next-gen-customer-info-panel.tsx`, all 3 tiers)

Per explicit follow-up request ("copilot as one of the tabs - can you re-add that?"), the Copilot tab — hidden entirely everywhere per a prior session's follow-up request (§46-52's original build, hidden by a later, undocumented-in-this-log change before this session) — is un-hidden again, restored to its ORIGINAL conditional visibility rather than always-on: `copilotAvailable = !startedFresh || channels.some((c) => c.lastCustomerMessageTick !== undefined)`, computed identically in both `CustomerInformationSidePanel` (the docked panel) and `CustomerInfoHoverPreview` (its closed-state hover flyout) — the only two consumers that ever had this hardcoded to `false`. A `startedFresh` (blank-slate, agent-initiated) interaction hasn't had the customer say anything yet, so Copilot has nothing to summarize; anything else — a notification-opened case, a reopened history entry, an interaction opened from the Interactions list, the page's own initially-seeded active call, or a `startedFresh` interaction whose customer HAS since replied (`Thread.lastCustomerMessageTick` gets set by `handleSendMessage`'s simulated reply) — makes Copilot available immediately. `startedFresh` is destructured back into both components' params (it had stayed in each type signature, unused, specifically so this revert needed no call-site changes across any of the 3 page files — confirmed true here).

Deliberately NOT re-added: the old "jump to Copilot automatically the moment the customer replies" behavior (the since-fully-removed `copilotFocusSignal`/`copilotFocusRequest` prop and its effect, previously wired through `CustomerInformationSidePanel` and all 3 page files). That auto-jump — not the tab's mere existence — is what got reported as annoying in the original hide request, and it stays gone; the tab is available once `copilotAvailable` flips true, but the agent has to click it themselves.

Two other, unrelated consumers keep their own PERMANENT, unconditional exclusion of "Copilot" — `CustomerRowInfoPanel` (Customers-table row info panel: "a Customers-table row is read-only reference info about a customer, never the seat of an actual conversation, so Copilot never has anything to summarize here") and `CustomerFullScreenTabContent` (its own doc comment: "Same unconditional 'Copilot' strip as `CustomerRowInfoPanel`"). Both predate and are independent of the "hidden everywhere" toggle this section reverts — neither was touched.

Not touched: the lyra-ui Storybook demo (`lyra-ui/src/components/__stories__/customer-information-demo.tsx`) that `CustomerInformationPanel.stories.tsx`/`PageHeader.stories.tsx`/the `AgentNextGenTemplate` "Active Interaction" story all share — its own `CUSTOMER_PANEL_TABS` never included "Copilot" at all (no matching commit in `lyra-ui`'s history), and its doc comment ("Copilot is currently hidden everywhere in v2, so it's omitted from the tab list here too") is now stale relative to this fix. Left as-is since replicating the real conditional (`startedFresh`/`lastCustomerMessageTick`) would need new state this simplified demo doesn't model at all — flagged here rather than guessed at silently.

Files touched: `agent-next-gen-customer-info-panel.tsx` only (`CustomerInfoHoverPreview` and `CustomerInformationSidePanel`'s `startedFresh` destructuring + `copilotAvailable` computation + related doc comments; `CUSTOMER_PANEL_TABS`/`AGENT_WORKSPACE_CUSTOMER_PANEL_TABS`'s own top-of-file doc comments).

Verified tsc-clean (51-error app baseline unchanged) and eslint-clean for this file (0 errors; 40 pre-existing warnings — the same `react-refresh/only-export-components` family this large file already had throughout, plus pre-existing `react-hooks/exhaustive-deps` warnings on unrelated hooks — none introduced by this change).

## 72. "Interactions" renamed to "Contacts" — Search panel sub-tab and Customer Information panel tab (all 3 tiers); Desk-tab label too, though currently unreachable

Per explicit request ("overall - rename Interactions 'Contacts' in all 3", clarified via follow-up to cover the Search panel sub-tab, the Customer Information panel's own tab, AND the (currently unreachable) Desk-tab label): every user-facing "Interactions" label this app has becomes "Contacts". Three genuinely separate label surfaces existed under that same word — none of their underlying data, components, or internal identifiers were renamed, only the display text and (where the label doubled as a literal type value) its matching comparisons:

1. **Search panel sub-tab** (`SEARCH_PANEL_TAB_LABELS.interactions`, `agent-next-gen-search-panel.tsx`) — the internal `SearchPanelTabKey` value stays `"interactions"`, the `InteractionsListView` component it renders is untouched; only the map's display string changed. Shared by all 3 tiers via `useSearchPanelContent`, so this single-line change covers 2.0, Premium, and Advanced at once.
2. **Customer Information panel tab** (`CUSTOMER_PANEL_TABS`, `agent-next-gen-customer-info-panel.tsx`) — unlike the Search panel's tab, this one has no separate internal key from its display label: the array entry itself IS the `CustomerPanelTabLabel` literal type value, so every comparison against it (`CUSTOMER_PANEL_TABS.indexOf("Interactions")`, `tabs.includes("Interactions")`, `visibleTabs.includes("Interactions")` — across `CustomerInformationSidePanel`, `CustomerInfoHoverPreview`, `CustomerRowInfoPanel`, and `CustomerFullScreenTabContent`) changed to `"Contacts"` together as one atomic rename — TypeScript's own literal-union checking caught every call site via compile errors until all were updated consistently. The "View All Interactions" button (Latest Interaction accordion, Overview tab) that jumps to this tab now reads "View All Contacts" to match. `AGENT_WORKSPACE_CUSTOMER_PANEL_TABS` (2.0's reduced tab set) doesn't include this tab at all, so 2.0 has no visible "Contacts" tab here — only Premium/Advanced do, unchanged from before.
3. **Desk-tab label** (`DESK_TAB_LABELS.interactions`, `agent-next-gen-interaction-dashboard.tsx`) — renamed for consistency even though currently unreachable in the live UI: Premium's own `deskTabOrder` no longer includes `"interactions"` (moved into Search per §70 above), and 2.0/Advanced dropped their whole Desk-tab row entirely — so this label map entry has no current render site anywhere, but stays renamed in case a `deskTabOrder` ever reintroduces it.

Two OTHER, unrelated "Interactions" exclusions were explicitly left alone, per the scoped clarification: `CustomerRowInfoPanel` (Customers-table row panel) and `CustomerFullScreenTabContent` both unconditionally strip "Copilot" from their tabs — that's an unrelated, permanent design decision (nothing to do with this rename) and wasn't touched. Historical/explanatory doc-comment mentions of "when 'Interactions' was a separate top-level tab" were left as accurate history rather than rewritten, since they describe a genuinely bygone state, not the current tab's name.

Files touched: `agent-next-gen-search-panel.tsx` (`SEARCH_PANEL_TAB_LABELS`, one doc comment), `agent-next-gen-customer-info-panel.tsx` (`CUSTOMER_PANEL_TABS` + every literal comparison against it across its 4 consumers, "View All Contacts" button text, related doc comments), `agent-next-gen-interaction-dashboard.tsx` (`DESK_TAB_LABELS`), `AgentWorkspace2WithDeskPage.tsx` and `AgentWorkspaceAdvancedPage.tsx` (doc-comment accuracy only, no functional code — both already consume the renamed shared constants).

Verified tsc-clean (51-error app baseline unchanged, and critically: zero NEW errors in either renamed file — confirming every `CustomerPanelTabLabel` literal comparison was updated consistently, since a stray old `"Interactions"` comparison would have failed to typecheck against the now-`"Contacts"`-inclusive union) and eslint-clean across all 5 touched files (0 new errors; `AgentWorkspace2WithDeskPage.tsx`/`AgentWorkspaceAdvancedPage.tsx` each show their own same 2 pre-existing errors — unused `PanelLeftClose` import, a hand-rolled-button rule violation — documented repeatedly earlier in this log, unchanged by this pass).

## 73. Copilot moved to the first tab position (Premium only, by structural consequence) — `CUSTOMER_PANEL_TABS` reorder

Per explicit follow-up request ("copilot be the first tab displayed and should not display until the customer responds/initiates a conversation"), `CUSTOMER_PANEL_TABS` is reordered from `["Overview", "Copilot", "Contacts", ...]` to `["Copilot", "Overview", "Contacts", "Detail", "Directory", "Tasks", "Notes", "Accounts", "Tickets"]` — Copilot is now the leftmost tab button whenever it's showing at all.

**The visibility-gating half of this request needed no change.** "Should not display until the customer responds/initiates a conversation" is exactly what `copilotAvailable` (restored in §71) already does: `!startedFresh || channels.some((c) => c.lastCustomerMessageTick !== undefined)`. A still-unavailable Copilot is filtered out of `visibleTabs` the same as always, regardless of this reorder — it doesn't jump the queue by becoming first in the underlying array; it simply isn't in `visibleTabs` at all until available.

**Array order alone wasn't enough — the panel now also opens ON Copilot by default whenever it's available.** The first pass here just reordered the array and pointed all four `useState(0)` initializers (`CustomerInfoHoverPreview`, `CustomerInformationSidePanel`, `CustomerRowInfoPanel`, `CustomerFullScreenTabContent`) at `CUSTOMER_PANEL_TABS.indexOf("Overview")` — reasoning that "first tab displayed" meant array position only, and the panel should keep landing on Overview by default. Live testing (screenshot: Alex Miller, an active WhatsApp interaction where the customer had already replied) showed this wasn't enough: lyra-ui's `TabList overflowMenu` collapses to "active tab + N More" the moment the panel is too narrow to fit every tab (the normal case here), and that collapsed row is keyed off whichever tab has `active` set, NOT array index 0. With `activeTab` still defaulting to Overview, Overview stayed the one visible pinned tab and Copilot stayed buried inside "N More" — from the agent's perspective, indistinguishable from Copilot not having moved at all.

Fixed in two passes. First, the two consumers that actually compute `copilotAvailable` (`CustomerInfoHoverPreview`, `CustomerInformationSidePanel`) were changed to land on "Copilot" by default whenever it's available at MOUNT time, falling back to "Overview" only when it isn't: `useState(() => CUSTOMER_PANEL_TABS.indexOf(copilotAvailable ? "Copilot" : "Overview"))`. `CustomerRowInfoPanel`/`CustomerFullScreenTabContent` kept their plain `CUSTOMER_PANEL_TABS.indexOf("Overview")` initializer — both strip "Copilot" unconditionally regardless of availability, so there's nothing to default to there.

That alone still wasn't enough — confirmed live via a second screenshot (Alex Miller, a genuinely `startedFresh` WhatsApp interaction: agent's greeting sent, Copilot correctly unavailable/absent at that point, THEN the customer replies "Thanks, got it!" — `copilotAvailable` flips `true` mid-session on the SAME already-mounted panel). A `useState` lazy initializer only runs once, at mount — it has no way to react to `copilotAvailable` changing after the fact, so the panel kept sitting on "Overview" even once Copilot had something to show. Fixed with a second, narrowly-scoped `useEffect` in `CustomerInformationSidePanel` (the only consumer that stays mounted across a live reply — `CustomerInfoHoverPreview` fully unmounts/remounts on every hover-off, so its mount-time initializer alone is already correct for it): a `prevCopilotAvailableRef` tracks `copilotAvailable`'s prior render value, seeded from the CURRENT value at declaration (not `false`) specifically so a panel that mounts with Copilot already available doesn't also fire a redundant jump on top of what the `useState` initializer already landed on directly. The effect only calls `setActiveTab(CUSTOMER_PANEL_TABS.indexOf("Copilot"))` on the actual false→true edge of `copilotAvailable`, once per interaction — deliberately narrower than the old `copilotFocusSignal` auto-jump removed in §71 (which fired repeatedly and stole focus mid-conversation): this fires exactly once, at the single moment Copilot first has anything to summarize, not on every subsequent customer message after that. The existing `useEffect` fallback (`if (!copilotAvailable && activeTab === CUSTOMER_PANEL_TABS.indexOf("Copilot")) setActiveTab(CUSTOMER_PANEL_TABS.indexOf("Overview"))`, keyed on `recordId` — handling the opposite direction, landing safely on Overview when switching TO a different interaction where Copilot isn't available) needed no change; the two effects are independent and keyed on different deps (`recordId` vs. `copilotAvailable`).

**Scoped to Premium by structural consequence, not new conditional logic.** `CUSTOMER_PANEL_TABS` (the full array just reordered) is used only by Premium's real-customer render call sites (`CustomerInformationSidePanel`/`CustomerInfoHoverPreview`) and by Premium's Customers-table sub-views (`CustomerRowInfoPanel`/`CustomerFullScreenTabContent`, which always strip "Copilot" unconditionally regardless of this reorder). 2.0 and Advanced both use the entirely separate `AGENT_WORKSPACE_CUSTOMER_PANEL_TABS` array (`["Overview", "Copilot", "Detail", "Notes"]`, unchanged), so this reorder has zero effect on either tier — matching the explicit "only in premium" scoping from the prior clarifying request, with no extra tier-specific plumbing needed.

Files touched: `agent-next-gen-customer-info-panel.tsx` only (`CUSTOMER_PANEL_TABS` array + its doc comment; four `useState` initializers).

Verified tsc-clean (51-error app baseline unchanged) and eslint-clean for this file (0 errors; same 40 pre-existing warnings as §71/§72 — no new warnings introduced).

## 74. Copilot tab content animates in sequentially on open

Per explicit follow-up request ("animate in the content in copilot sequentially after the copilot window opens"), `CopilotTabContent`'s two blocks — the reason-for-contact info box, then the "Journey Summary" card — now reveal one after another instead of popping in together. Both use the same `animate-in fade-in-0 slide-in-from-bottom-1` entrance `CustomerRecordSaveFooter` already established elsewhere in this file (its own `slide-in-from-bottom-2 fade-in-0` footer entrance), staggered via `tailwindcss-animate`'s `delay-*` utilities — a plain Tailwind `transitionDelay` scale that plugin also wires up as `animation-delay` for anything under `animate-in`, so no custom CSS or JS timers were needed. The info box has no `delay-*` (goes first); the Journey Summary card is delayed behind it (goes second).

This works as an "on open" reveal for free because `CopilotTabContent` is only ever mounted while the Copilot tab is actually active — its call site is a plain `activeTab === CUSTOMER_PANEL_TABS.indexOf("Copilot") && <CopilotTabContent ... />` guard, so every time the tab becomes active (clicked manually, or landed on automatically per §73's "default to Copilot when available" / "jump to Copilot on first availability" behavior) is a fresh mount, and CSS entrance animations replay on every fresh mount with nothing to manually reset in between.

**Follow-up fix: flash on open, and pacing too fast.** Reported live right after the first pass — the content briefly flashed fully visible before animating. Root cause: `animate-in`'s default `animation-fill-mode` is `none`, so for the ENTIRE `delay-*` window (and even the sub-frame gap before a non-delayed animation's first paint), the browser renders the element in its plain resting style — fully visible — and only jumps to the "from" keyframe (invisible/offset) once the animation actually starts, then animates back to visible. That "briefly visible → sudden disappear → fade back in" read as a flash, worst on the delayed Journey Summary card since its whole delay window was spent fully visible first. Fixed by adding `fill-mode-backwards` (a utility this same `tailwindcss-animate` plugin already provides — `matchUtilities({"fill-mode": ...})`, mapped straight to CSS `animation-fill-mode`) to both blocks: it holds the "from" keyframe's styles for the entire time before each block's animation starts, so nothing is visible before its intended reveal moment. Pacing was also slowed per the same request — `duration-300`/`delay-150` became `duration-500`/`delay-300`, a more clearly readable half-second fade+slide each with a real pause between the two, not a rushed back-to-back flicker.

Files touched: `agent-next-gen-customer-info-panel.tsx` only (`CopilotTabContent`'s two block `className`s + its own doc comment).

Verified tsc-clean (51-error app baseline unchanged) and eslint-clean for this file (0 errors; same 40 pre-existing warnings — no new warnings introduced).

## 75. Closed Customer Information panel auto-opens — but only the FIRST time Copilot has something new (not every reply)

Per explicit follow-up request ("if the customer information is closed and the agent gets a response from the customer, open the customer information panel with copilot"), a customer's (simulated) reply reveals the docked Customer Information panel if it was closed. A closely-following clarification ("only open the customer information automatically if a NEW message appears in the copilot window") narrowed this from firing on every single reply down to firing only the first time Copilot actually has something new — the sub-sections below describe both the original (broader) pass and the fix.

**First pass (too broad).** `handleSendMessage`'s simulated customer-reply `setTimeout` (in all 3 page files) called `setSidePanelOpen(true)` after EVERY reply landed on the interaction the agent was still viewing (scoped via a render-assigned `activeInteractionIdRef`, avoiding the same stale-closure issue `clockTickRef` already solves for `clockTick` in this function). This correctly revealed the panel the first time, but kept re-triggering on every later reply in an already-open, already-`copilotAvailable` conversation too — not what "a NEW message appears in the copilot window" was asking for, since Copilot's own tab content (`reasonForContact`/`journeySummary`) is a static per-customer summary, not a running message feed that gets something new on every reply.

**Fix: piggyback on the SAME false→true edge Copilot's own tab-switch already uses.** Reverted the `handleSendMessage`/`activeInteractionIdRef` changes in all 3 pages entirely. Instead, `CustomerInformationSidePanel` gained a new optional `onCopilotFirstAvailable?: () => void` prop, invoked from inside its EXISTING `copilotAvailable` false→true `useEffect` (§73 — the same one that already calls `setActiveTab(CUSTOMER_PANEL_TABS.indexOf("Copilot"))`, guarded by `prevCopilotAvailableRef` so it only fires on the actual availability edge, never on a later reply within an already-available conversation). All 3 page files wire `onCopilotFirstAvailable={() => setSidePanelOpen(true)}` at their `CustomerInformationSidePanel` render call site. This needed no separate "which interaction is the agent still looking at" tracking at all: `<div key={`side-panel-${activeInteraction.id}`}>` (wrapping every page's own render call site) already force-remounts `CustomerInformationSidePanel` on every interaction switch, which resets `prevCopilotAvailableRef` fresh from that new interaction's own `copilotAvailable` value — so the effect (and this callback) is inherently scoped to whichever interaction is currently displayed, with no extra plumbing.

Files touched: `agent-next-gen-customer-info-panel.tsx` (`onCopilotFirstAvailable` prop + its invocation inside the existing effect), `AgentNextGenPage.tsx`, `AgentWorkspace2WithDeskPage.tsx`, `AgentWorkspaceAdvancedPage.tsx` (each: removed the `activeInteractionIdRef`/`handleSendMessage` addition from the first pass, added one `onCopilotFirstAvailable={() => setSidePanelOpen(true)}` line at their own `CustomerInformationSidePanel` call site).

Verified tsc-clean (51-error app baseline unchanged) and eslint-clean across all 4 touched files (0 new errors — the 3 page files each show the same 2 pre-existing errors already documented earlier in this log: unused `PanelLeftClose` import, a hand-rolled-`<button>` rule violation; `agent-next-gen-customer-info-panel.tsx` shows its same 40 pre-existing warnings, 0 errors).

## 76. Fixed: Copilot content leaking into the Customers-table row panel (a real, live bug — not stale docs)

Reported live via screenshot ("don't display copilot in the customer tab - it is only an in-interaction feature") alongside a second message ("you are missing the overview in customer table"): opening a Customers-table row (`CustomerRowInfoPanel`) showed "Overview" as the selected tab label, but the actual body content underneath was Copilot's info box + Journey Summary card — Overview's own real content (the Customer Overview accordion, Latest Interaction, Latest Note) never rendered at all.

Root cause: a leftover per-row reset `useEffect` — `useEffect(() => { if (row) setActiveTab(0); }, [row?.contactNumber]);` — that predates §73's `CUSTOMER_PANEL_TABS` reorder. Before that reorder, global index `0` WAS "Overview", so this hardcoded reset happened to be correct by coincidence. Once "Copilot" moved to index `0`, this same reset started forcing `activeTab` onto Copilot's content on every row open instead — even though `CustomerRowInfoPanel`'s own `visibleTabs` already unconditionally strips "Copilot" from the tab row itself (see that `tabs` prop's own doc comment: "a Customers-table row is read-only reference info about a customer, never the seat of an actual conversation"), so no tab button ever visibly highlighted as "Copilot" — the mismatch between the (correct, Overview-labeled) tab row and the (wrong, Copilot) body content is exactly what both bug reports describe.

This was the ONLY leftover hardcoded numeric `setActiveTab` call in the file — confirmed via a repo-wide `setActiveTab\(\d\)` search turning up nothing else. (The four `useState(0)` mount-time initializers were already fixed correctly back in §73 itself, including this same `CustomerRowInfoPanel`'s own initializer — this bug was specifically in a SEPARATE, later-added reset effect that §73's fix didn't touch, since it wasn't part of that original grep for `useState(0)`.)

Fixed the same way as every other site in this file: `setActiveTab(CUSTOMER_PANEL_TABS.indexOf("Overview"))` instead of the hardcoded `0`.

Files touched: `agent-next-gen-customer-info-panel.tsx` only (`CustomerRowInfoPanel`'s per-row reset effect).
