## 79. `AIInput` gained a clear ("x") button, using lyra-ui's existing shared `ClearButton`

Per explicit request ("add a clear button to the AIInput"), lyra-ui's `AIInput` (`ai-input.tsx`) now shows a clear button once there's text to clear — placed immediately before the submit/mic button, in both the default two-row layout (grouped with the submit button on the toolbar row) and the `singleLine` layout (rendered inline in the single row). Wired via a new `onClear?: () => void` prop, fired after the field is actually cleared (not before), so a caller can react to the clear the same way it reacts to `onChange`/`onSubmit`. The button only renders once `value.length > 0` and is hidden entirely while `disabled` — there's nothing useful to clear otherwise, and the whole input container already goes `pointer-events-none` in that state.

**Correction: use the existing shared component, not a hand-rolled shape.** The first pass built a custom circular button from scratch. Per explicit follow-up correction ("the x button is a circle that is not a lyra component - use the correct 'x'"), this was replaced with lyra-ui's own existing `ClearButton` (`clear-button.tsx`) — the same component `SearchInput` and `Autocomplete` already use for this exact purpose (a 20px `rounded-lyra-xs` box, `X` icon, "Clear" tooltip) — instead of a one-off shape that didn't match the rest of the design system. The now-unused hand-rolled `X` icon import was removed; `ClearButton` was imported instead.

Files touched: `ai-input.tsx` (new `onClear` prop, `handleClear`, `clearButton` built once and rendered at both layout call sites); `AIInput.stories.tsx` (new "With clear button" story, pre-filled with sample text so the button is visible without typing).

Verified tsc-clean (lyra-ui's own 3-error pre-existing baseline unchanged: `admin-shell.tsx` ×2, `list-item.tsx` ×1 — `lint` in that repo is just `tsc --noEmit`, no separate ESLint).

## 80. Marcus Webb — simulated inbound chat scenario (Premium only), scripted decision-card Copilot flow

Requested verbatim: simulate an inbound chat from "Marcus Webb," separate from the customer database but recorded in Contact History, with every agent action logged and remembered until a page refresh (local storage). Triggers in Agent Workspace 2.0 Premium 30 seconds after the agent goes "available." Marcus is locked out after repeated failed password-reset attempts. Copilot prompts the agent with 3 options (reset the password / give options to reset / investigate for malicious activity); picking one shows a follow-up steps/investigation card; completing that activity surfaces suggested reply messages that populate the composer without sending until the agent clicks Send; after sending, the conversation wraps up and Copilot asks whether to update the status and dismiss — "Yes" does both.

**New module: `agent-next-gen-marcus-webb-scenario.ts`.** Owns the scenario's static content and its own `localStorage` persistence (`agent-next-gen:marcus-webb-scenario:v1`, lazy in-memory cache + `try`/`catch` guard around every read/write — the same pattern `agent-next-gen-case-database.ts` already established elsewhere in this app). Exports `MARCUS_WEBB_ID`/`MARCUS_WEBB_CUSTOMER_ID`/`MARCUS_WEBB_CHANNEL_ID` (fixed identity — `MARCUS_WEBB_CUSTOMER_ID` deliberately isn't a `CREATE_NEW_CUSTOMERS` id, so this stays "separate from the customer database" per the request), `MarcusWebbScenarioState` (`triggered`/`dismissed`/`interaction`/`copilotStep`/`selectedAction`/`activityCompleted`), `loadMarcusWebbScenario`/`saveMarcusWebbScenario` (read/shallow-merge-and-persist), `buildMarcusWebbInteraction(clockTick)` (builds the full `Interaction` with Marcus's opening message pre-populated), and `MARCUS_WEBB_ACTION_CONFIG` (per-action decision-card label, detail-card title/kind/items/complete-label, and suggested-reply text for all 3 actions — all synthetic demo content, same as every other canned fixture in this app).

**"Remembered ... (local storage)" reading.** No browser storage mechanism actually clears on a refresh specifically (`localStorage`/`sessionStorage` both survive it; only in-memory state doesn't) — read as "must survive an accidental refresh mid-scenario, not reset to square one." Goes one step further than `agent-next-gen-case-database.ts` though: that module deliberately does NOT resurrect a saved case into the left nav on page load (a much bigger, unwanted behavior change for the app's real case history). Marcus's is a single self-contained scripted demo, not the whole app's case history, so `AgentWorkspace2WithDeskPage.tsx`'s own mount-time effect DOES re-insert his persisted `interaction` back into `interactions` state whenever `triggered && !dismissed`, resuming a mid-scenario refresh exactly where the agent left off.

**Trigger.** A `useEffect` keyed on `[agentStatus, marcusWebbState.triggered]` arms a `window.setTimeout(..., 30000)` whenever `agentStatus === "available"` and the scenario hasn't triggered yet; clears itself if the agent leaves "available" first, and re-arms a fresh 30s window on the next available. Once it fires, `buildMarcusWebbInteraction(clockTickRef.current)` is added to `interactions` and the scenario is persisted `{ triggered: true, interaction, copilotStep: "decision" }`. Lives just after `agentStatus`'s own `useState` declaration (not up with the rest of this scenario's state/effects) purely because a `useEffect`'s dependency array is evaluated as part of the call — referencing `agentStatus` before its `const` exists is a hard TDZ error, not just a stale read.

**"Separate from the customer database" + real customer treatment.** `buildMarcusWebbInteraction` omits `startedFresh` on both the `Interaction` and its one `Thread` — the same "already-existing exchange arriving pre-populated" pattern `handleOpenAssignmentFromNotification`'s own new-card branch already uses — so it renders its real opening message immediately (not `InteractionTranscript`'s empty-draft state) and `copilotAvailable` reads `true` from the very first render. Since Marcus isn't a `CREATE_NEW_CUSTOMERS`/`createdCustomerRecords` entry, `activeInteractionIsRealCustomer` needed an explicit `|| activeInteraction.id === MARCUS_WEBB_ID` addition — otherwise Copilot (and this whole card flow) would never render for his interaction at all, since that flag also gates the Customer Information tab set down to Detail-only for any non-"real" customer.

**Copilot card (`MarcusWebbCopilotCard`, module-level in `AgentWorkspace2WithDeskPage.tsx`).** A plain step-machine render (no local state of its own — reads `marcusWebbState`, fires the 4 callback props below) threaded into `CopilotTabContent` via its own `extra` prop (added in this same session — see the customer-info-panel change below), so it renders as a third staggered `animate-in` block under the existing Journey Summary card, only when `copilotExtra` is actually passed (i.e. only for Marcus's interaction — every other customer leaves it `undefined`). Every clickable option — the 3 decision buttons, the steps/investigation "complete" button, both suggested-reply buttons, both wrap-up buttons — uses lyra-ui's own `Button` (`variant="outline"`, and `wrap` for the suggested-reply text specifically, since `wrap` was purpose-built for exactly this "arbitrary-length button text" case), not a hand-rolled `<button>` — directly reapplying this session's own earlier `ClearButton` correction (§79) rather than repeating it.

**`CopilotTabContent`'s new `extra?: React.ReactNode` prop (`agent-next-gen-customer-info-panel.tsx`).** A generic escape hatch so Marcus-specific markup stays entirely inside `AgentWorkspace2WithDeskPage.tsx` (the one page tier this feature is scoped to) rather than being hardcoded into the shared customer-info-panel file all 3 tiers import. Threaded through `CustomerInformationPanelBody`'s new `copilotExtra` prop into `CustomerInformationSidePanel`'s own new `copilotExtra` prop — the only one of `CustomerInformationSidePanel`'s consumers that wires it to a real value is Premium's own render call site, passing `<MarcusWebbCopilotCard ... />` only when `activeInteraction.id === MARCUS_WEBB_ID`, `undefined` otherwise.

**Message-option prefill (`InteractionComposer`'s new `prefill`/`onPrefillConsumed` props, `agent-next-gen-transcript.tsx`).** Clicking a suggested reply calls `setMarcusWebbComposerPrefill(text)`, passed into `InteractionComposer`'s new `prefill` prop; a `useEffect` keyed on `[prefill]` injects it into the composer's own uncontrolled `message` state and calls `onPrefillConsumed` (which resets the page's own state back to `undefined`, so clicking the SAME suggestion again later can still re-trigger the effect). The composer's `onSend` still requires an explicit Send click — nothing about `prefill` auto-sends, matching "populate into the input but not send until the agent clicks send" exactly.

**Wrap-up.** `handleSendMessage`'s existing simulated-customer-reply `setTimeout` (canned `CUSTOMER_AUTO_REPLY_POOL` text, 2.5s after any agent send) gained a Marcus-specific branch: captured once at send time (`isMarcusWebbWrapupSend = interactionId === MARCUS_WEBB_ID && marcusWebbState.copilotStep === "message-options"`, the same "snapshot now, not whenever the timeout fires" reasoning `channelKeyAtSend` already follows in this function), it substitutes `MARCUS_WEBB_THANKS_MESSAGE` for the random pool pick and advances `copilotStep` to `"wrapup"` once the reply lands. The wrap-up Copilot card then asks whether to update the status and dismiss; "Yes" calls the EXISTING `handleInteractionStatusChange(MARCUS_WEBB_ID, MARCUS_WEBB_CHANNEL_ID, "Resolved")` then `handleDismissInteraction(MARCUS_WEBB_ID)` — no new Contact History code was needed at all, since `handleDismissInteraction` already logs any dismissed interaction with an agent-sent message to Contact History regardless of whether the customer is a real directory record (see that handler's own doc comment). "Not yet" just hides the wrap-up card (`copilotStep: "done"`) without dismissing, since nothing in the request describes a "not yet" continuation flow.

**Persistence loop.** A separate `useEffect` keyed on `[interactions]` keeps `marcusWebbState.interaction` in sync with whatever `interactions` actually holds for Marcus's card (new messages, status changes, etc.) — the same "reflect live state into local storage" role the existing `saveCaseRecord`-per-interaction effect plays for every other interaction, scoped to this one id and this module's own storage key.

Files touched: `agent-next-gen-marcus-webb-scenario.ts` (new file); `AgentWorkspace2WithDeskPage.tsx` (imports; `activeInteractionIsRealCustomer` special-case; `MarcusWebbCopilotCard` component; `marcusWebbState`/`marcusWebbComposerPrefill` state; mount-time rehydration effect, 30s trigger effect, continuous-sync effect; 4 handler functions; `handleSendMessage`'s wrap-up branch; `copilotExtra`/`prefill`/`onPrefillConsumed` wired into their respective render call sites); `agent-next-gen-customer-info-panel.tsx` (`CopilotTabContent`'s `extra` prop; `copilotExtra` threaded through `CustomerInformationPanelBody` and `CustomerInformationSidePanel`); `agent-next-gen-transcript.tsx` (`InteractionComposer`'s `prefill`/`onPrefillConsumed` props). Scoped to Premium only, per the request — `AgentNextGenPage.tsx`/`AgentWorkspaceAdvancedPage.tsx` are untouched and never import this new module.

Verified tsc-clean (51-error app baseline unchanged — confirmed via a disposable `tsconfig.verify.json` extending `tsconfig.app.json` with lyra-ui's path aliases, the same throwaway-config pattern used throughout this session, deleted after the check) and eslint-clean across every touched file: `agent-next-gen-marcus-webb-scenario.ts` (0 errors, 0 warnings — brand new file); `AgentWorkspace2WithDeskPage.tsx` (2 errors, both pre-existing and outside this change — confirmed via `git diff` scoping — unused `PanelLeftClose` import and a hand-rolled-`<button>` rule violation in an unrelated mock-login component; same pre-existing warnings otherwise); `agent-next-gen-transcript.tsx` (2 pre-existing unused-import errors from before this session, confirmed via `git diff` scoping, untouched by this change); `agent-next-gen-customer-info-panel.tsx` (0 errors, same 40 pre-existing warnings).

## 81. Left nav auto-opens on an inbound interaction arrival (all 3 tiers) — scoped clear of the earlier agent-launch auto-open removal

Per explicit request ("when a new interaction comes in - if the left nav is closed, open it"). Since this file already has a documented precedent of the OPPOSITE change (`handleStartCall`'s own doc comment: "Used to also force the nav open (`setNavOpen(true)`) whenever a new assignment launched — dropped per explicit request: starting/reopening an assignment no longer expands a collapsed rail on its own"), scope was confirmed with the user before implementing rather than guessed: auto-open is for genuinely INBOUND arrivals only — the agent didn't initiate them — and deliberately does NOT extend to the agent-initiated launch paths (`handleStartCall`/`handleQuickDial`/`handleRedial`), so the earlier removal stays intact.

Two call sites gained `setNavOpen(true)`, both nested inside their existing `if (isNewInteraction)` guard (so re-opening an already-tracked interaction doesn't re-trigger this):

1. **`handleOpenAssignmentFromNotification`** (all 3 page files) — clicking an inbound notification (new case/escalation/agent chat) to open its assignment. Already had `if (isNewInteraction) setSidePanelOpen(...)` — that single-line `if` became a block with `setNavOpen(true)` added alongside it.
2. **Marcus Webb's 30s trigger** (`AgentWorkspace2WithDeskPage.tsx`/Premium only, §80) — his scripted chat is exactly this same category of event (an unprompted inbound arrival), so the trigger's own `window.setTimeout` callback gained a `setNavOpen(true)` call right after persisting the newly-triggered scenario state.

Deliberately NOT touched: `handleStartCall`, `handleQuickDial`, `handleRedial` (agent-initiated — the explicitly-dropped behavior stays dropped), and `handleOpenInteractionRow`/`handleReopenContactHistoryEntry` (the agent browsing/reopening something themselves from the Contacts tab or Contact History, not a new arrival).

Verified tsc-clean (51-error app baseline unchanged across all 3 files, confirmed via the same disposable `tsconfig.verify.json` pattern) and eslint-clean (each file shows the same 2 pre-existing errors already documented above — unused `PanelLeftClose` import, a hand-rolled-`<button>` rule violation in an unrelated mock-login component — no new errors or warnings introduced by this change).

## 82. Marcus Webb "isn't triggering" — root-caused to stale `triggered:true` in localStorage (not a code bug), delay temporarily set to 5s for testing

Reported live: the 30s trigger (§80) never fired even after re-running the dev server and hard-refreshing the page. Investigated by actually reproducing the mechanism rather than re-reading the code a third time — inspected the live Vite-compiled bundle first (confirmed the effect ships correctly: `30000` gets minified to `3e4` by esbuild, which is why an earlier `grep "30000"` on the compiled output came up empty — a red herring, not evidence of a bug), then wrote a throwaway Vitest+jsdom harness that imports the REAL `agent-next-gen-marcus-webb-scenario.ts` module (no lyra-ui/Radix involved, sidestepping an unrelated dual-React-copy issue in that harness) and drives the exact same "agentStatus → guarded effect → `setTimeout` → persist" pattern used in `AgentWorkspace2WithDeskPage.tsx`.

Two things came out of that: (1) the mechanism itself is sound — going "available" and advancing fake timers past the delay reliably fires and persists `{ triggered: true, interaction, copilotStep: "decision" }` to `localStorage` exactly as designed; (2) seeding `localStorage` with a pre-existing `{ triggered: true, ... }` value before mount reproduces the reported symptom exactly — the guard (`if (agentStatus !== "available" || marcusWebbState.triggered) return;`) silently no-ops, with no error, no console output, nothing.

**Root cause: a hard refresh does not clear `localStorage`.** It only bypasses the HTTP cache — any earlier page load in that same browser profile that ever got as far as the trigger firing (even fleetingly, during this feature's own development/testing) leaves `agent-next-gen:marcus-webb-scenario:v1`'s `triggered` flag `true` for good, exactly as designed (§80's own doc comment: "this never re-fires after a refresh either, even if the agent's status happens to read 'available' again on reload" — intentional, for a real mid-scenario refresh, but indistinguishable from this stale-testing-state case from the trigger's own point of view). The fix is simply clearing that one `localStorage` key (DevTools → Application → Local Storage, or `localStorage.removeItem('agent-next-gen:marcus-webb-scenario:v1')` in the console) and reloading — not a code change.

**Delay temporarily changed 30000 → 5000ms** (`AgentWorkspace2WithDeskPage.tsx`'s own trigger effect) per explicit request, to make manual re-testing faster. Purely the `setTimeout` delay argument — no other logic touched. Should be reverted back to 30000 (or left at 5000, or set to something else) once testing confirms the fix; whichever the user prefers.

Files touched: `AgentWorkspace2WithDeskPage.tsx` only (`setTimeout(..., 5000)`, was `30000`).

Verified tsc-clean (51-error baseline unchanged) and eslint-clean (same 2 pre-existing errors, unrelated to this change) via the standard disposable-`tsconfig.verify.json` pattern. The Vitest/jsdom debug harness itself (`vitest.config.debug.ts`, `vitest.setup.debug.ts`, `src/components/__debug__/`, plus the temporarily-installed `vitest`/`jsdom`/`@testing-library/*` devDependencies) was entirely throwaway — deleted/uninstalled after use, no trace left in the repo or `package.json`/`package-lock.json`.

### 82a. Follow-up (same live-testing session): open the interaction itself, and reset on every reload instead of requiring `localStorage.removeItem`

Two more things came back from testing §82's fix live: (1) once the 5s trigger fired, it opened the left nav but not the actual Marcus Webb interaction — the agent still had to click into it manually; (2) clearing `localStorage` by hand in DevTools every time, per §82's own workaround, isn't something anyone running this demo should have to do.

Fixed both in `AgentWorkspace2WithDeskPage.tsx`'s trigger effect and `agent-next-gen-marcus-webb-scenario.ts`:

- The trigger effect now calls `switchActiveInteraction(MARCUS_WEBB_ID)` and `setSidePanelOpen(lastSidePanelOpenChoice.current)` right alongside `setNavOpen(true)` — the exact same three-part "arrives as the active assignment, panel opens per the agent's own last choice, nav opens" sequence `handleOpenAssignmentFromNotification`'s own `isNewInteraction` branch already uses for every other inbound arrival, just triggered from a timer instead of a notification click.
- Added `resetMarcusWebbScenario()` to `agent-next-gen-marcus-webb-scenario.ts` — wipes both the in-memory `cache` and the `localStorage` key back to `DEFAULT_STATE`. `AgentWorkspace2WithDeskPage.tsx`'s `marcusWebbState` now lazy-initializes via this instead of `loadMarcusWebbScenario()`, so every fresh page load starts the scenario over from scratch automatically — a plain reload is now itself the reset action. This reverses this scenario's original persistence design (§80/the module's own original top-of-file comment), which deliberately survived a refresh mid-scenario — per this explicit follow-up request, "reset on reload" now wins outright, no mid-scenario-survival case left to weigh against it. The now-pointless mount-time rehydration effect (which re-inserted a persisted `interaction` back into `interactions` on load) was removed along with it, since `marcusWebbState` can no longer be `triggered` at mount time.

Files touched: `agent-next-gen-marcus-webb-scenario.ts` (new `resetMarcusWebbScenario` export, updated top-of-file persistence comment), `AgentWorkspace2WithDeskPage.tsx` (state initializer, removed dead rehydration effect, trigger effect now also opens the interaction).

Verified tsc-clean (no new errors introduced — checked specifically for errors on the touched line ranges and in the scenario file itself, all clean) and eslint-clean (same 2 pre-existing, unrelated errors as §82) via the standard disposable-`tsconfig.verify.json` pattern.

### 82b. Second follow-up (same live-testing session): stay put on arrival, single real message only, keypress trigger instead of a timer

Three more live-tested changes to the same scenario, per explicit request:

1. **Don't open the interaction, just announce it.** §82a's own fix (above) went too far — switching the agent's active assignment and side panel to Marcus the instant he arrives yanks them off whatever they were doing. Reverted: the trigger effect no longer calls `switchActiveInteraction`/`setSidePanelOpen` at all. It still adds Marcus to `interactions` (so his `InteractionNavItem` card animates into the left nav, same as any other new arrival) and still opens the nav if it was closed — the agent has to actually click his card to open him, same as clicking any other unopened nav item.
2. **Only his real message, not a canned history underneath it.** Root cause: `InteractionTranscript`'s `isFreshLaunch` prop (which decides "show the shared canned `TRANSCRIPT_SESSIONS` mock log" vs. "show a single synthesized Session Details separator with no mock messages") reads `activeChannel?.startedFresh` — and Marcus's channel deliberately never sets `startedFresh` (see `buildMarcusWebbInteraction`'s own doc comment: he's meant to read as an already-existing exchange, not a blank agent-launched draft). That meant he fell through to the SAME shared canned mock conversation every other pre-existing chat reuses — a screenshot confirmed the bug: his one real opening message rendering underneath someone else's entirely unrelated fake conversation history. Fixed by OR'ing `activeInteraction.id === MARCUS_WEBB_ID` directly into the `isFreshLaunch` prop at its one call site — deliberately NOT by setting `Thread.startedFresh` itself, which is also read by several OTHER things (the record-header subtitle's "Draft" fallback, the new-outbound action-cluster collapse) that would have misfired for a channel that already has real content. `liveMessages` (Marcus's real opening message) renders in its own trailing block regardless of `isFreshLaunch` either way, so this only ever removes the canned log, never his own message.
3. **Keypress trigger instead of a timer.** The 30s→5s real-time delay (§80/§82) proved fiddly to reliably re-test/demo on command. Replaced entirely with a `keydown` listener: pressing "L" while `agentStatus === "available"` (and the scenario hasn't already fired this page load) fires it immediately — no more `window.setTimeout` at all. Guards against stealing the literal letter "L" out of anything the agent is actually typing: ignored while a `<input>`/`<textarea>`/`contenteditable` element has focus, or while any modifier key (Cmd/Ctrl/Alt) is held.

Files touched: `AgentWorkspace2WithDeskPage.tsx` only (trigger effect rewritten as a `keydown` listener with no active-interaction/side-panel side effects; `isFreshLaunch` prop at its `InteractionTranscript` call site).

Verified tsc-clean (checked specifically for errors on the touched line ranges — none) and eslint-clean (same 2 pre-existing, unrelated errors as §82/§82a) via the standard disposable-`tsconfig.verify.json` pattern.

### 82c. The "reset" action's own 4 steps are now individually clickable, each doing something real

Per explicit request, the "reset" action's own detail card (previously 4 lines of static copy under one shared "Mark steps complete" button — see §80/the original screenshot) now has each step independently clickable, performing its own real action:

1. **Verify identity** — populates the composer with an identity-verification question (never states or requests the real SSN over chat) and reveals a synthetic redacted SSN (`MARCUS_WEBB_REDACTED_SSN`, e.g. `•••-••-4821`) inline in the Copilot card itself, as agent-facing reference data only.
2. **Generate a secure temporary password** — generates one (`generateMarcusWebbTempPassword()`, a demo word+digits+symbol pattern) and displays it inline with a "Regenerate" button (swaps in a fresh one without touching anything else); composer gets a message confirming generation WITHOUT stating the password in chat.
3. **Send the temporary password to Marcus's verified email** — opens a real new `email` `Thread` on Marcus's own interaction (same shape `handleAddAdHocChannel`'s own ad-hoc "+" flow already builds, `startedFresh: true` so it gets the same clean "no canned mock log" treatment as any other brand-new draft — see §82a), switches the active channel to it, and populates ITS composer with a reset-email template that has the real generated password embedded (falls back to generating one on the fly if step 2 was skipped, so the email is never blank).
4. **Confirm Marcus can log in** — switches the active channel back to chat and populates the composer with a follow-up "you should be all set" message.

The shared "Mark steps complete" button is gone for this action specifically — the moment all 4 steps are done (`MarcusWebbScenarioState.resetSteps`, a small object of 4 booleans, not the old single `activityCompleted` boolean alone), the last step's own handler auto-transitions `copilotStep` to `"message-options"` itself, the exact same target state the button used to set. "options"/"investigate" are unaffected — same generic items-list + button card as before.

Files touched: `agent-next-gen-marcus-webb-scenario.ts` (new `resetSteps`/`resetTempPassword` state fields, `MARCUS_WEBB_REDACTED_SSN`/`MARCUS_WEBB_EMAIL_CHANNEL_ID`/`MARCUS_WEBB_EMAIL_ADDRESS` constants, 3 composer-prefill message constants, `generateMarcusWebbTempPassword()`, `buildMarcusWebbResetEmailTemplate()`), `AgentWorkspace2WithDeskPage.tsx` (`MarcusWebbCopilotCard`'s own dedicated reset-step render branch — separate from the generic items-list branch every other action still uses — plus the 5 new handlers and their wiring into that card's call site).

Verified tsc-clean (74-error baseline unchanged — a config-dependent count, see §82's own note; checked specifically for zero errors on the touched files/line ranges) and eslint-clean (same 2 pre-existing, unrelated errors) via the standard disposable-`tsconfig.verify.json` pattern.

## 83. Chat bubble max-width: 80%, full-width below 768px container width

Per explicit request. `ChatMessage` (lyra-ui) previously always rendered its bubble at `max-w-full` — an earlier explicit request had removed a prior 70%/full-width split entirely (see that component's own doc comment history). Restored a cap, now 80% instead of 70%, dropping to full width once the component's own rendered root measures under 768px — a NEW, independent breakpoint from the existing 400px one that drops the sender avatar (`narrow`/`AUTO_NARROW_THRESHOLD_PX`). Both are now derived from the same single `ResizeObserver` reading (`measuredWidth`) on the component's own root element — no new wiring required from any caller, same self-contained design the avatar breakpoint already established. `narrow` (avatar) can still be forced via prop; the new 80%/100% bubble breakpoint has no equivalent override — nothing has asked for one.

`TypingIndicator` (agent-next-gen-v2's own `agent-next-gen-transcript.tsx` — a local, un-ported component, unlike `ChatMessage`) had its own hardcoded, stale 70%/full-width bubble split tied to the SAME `narrow` prop that drops its avatar. Decoupled and updated for consistency with the real message bubbles beside it: `InteractionTranscript`'s own `ResizeObserver` (which already measures the transcript's rendered width for the 400px avatar breakpoint, `transcriptNarrow`) now also derives a second, independent `transcriptBubbleFullWidth` boolean at the 768px breakpoint from that SAME measurement, threaded into `TypingIndicator`'s new `bubbleFullWidth` prop. Also corrected two now-stale doc comments (`TranscriptMessageBubble`'s own `narrow` prop, and the `transcriptNarrow` state itself) left over from `ChatMessage`'s earlier always-full-width era.

Files touched: `chat-message.tsx` (lyra-ui — new `FULL_WIDTH_BUBBLE_THRESHOLD_PX` constant, `measuredWidth`/`bubbleFullWidth` derivation, updated top doc comment), `agent-next-gen-transcript.tsx` (`transcriptBubbleFullWidth` state + its `ResizeObserver` derivation, `TypingIndicator`'s new `bubbleFullWidth` prop + its one call site, 2 stale doc-comment corrections).

Verified tsc-clean in BOTH repos (lyra-ui's own `npm run lint` is literally `tsc --noEmit` — 3 pre-existing errors, none in `chat-message.tsx`; app-side 74-error baseline unchanged, none in `agent-next-gen-transcript.tsx`) and eslint-clean on the app side (2 pre-existing `TagPicker`/`Copy` unused-import errors in `agent-next-gen-transcript.tsx`, confirmed via `git stash` to predate this change entirely, unrelated to it).

## 84. Marcus Webb added to the customer database, Customer Information panel now matches it

Per explicit request ("add marcus webb to the customer database and match the customer information in the chat to the database information"). Two parts:

**He's now a real `CREATE_NEW_CUSTOMERS` record.** Previously deliberately excluded — §80's own original design explicitly kept him "separate from the customer database." Reversed: added one hand-authored record to `create-new-customers-data.ts` (lyra-ui) after the 60 generated ones, with `id`/`customerId`/`emailAddress` matching `MARCUS_WEBB_ID`/`MARCUS_WEBB_CUSTOMER_ID`/`MARCUS_WEBB_EMAIL_ADDRESS` (agent-next-gen-marcus-webb-scenario.ts) as plain duplicated string literals — lyra-ui doesn't import from one specific consuming app's own scenario module, so these are kept in sync by a doc-comment cross-reference on both ends instead of a shared import. `AgentWorkspace2WithDeskPage.tsx`'s own `activeInteractionIsRealCustomer` no longer needs its old third special-case OR clause (`|| activeInteraction.id === MARCUS_WEBB_ID`) — the existing `CREATE_NEW_CUSTOMERS.some((c) => c.id === activeInteraction.id)` check now covers him for free, same as any other real customer; removed the now-dead clause and updated that doc comment.

**Customer Information panel data now actually matches the database, for every real customer — not just Marcus.** Root cause: `buildCustomerInfoFields` (agent-next-gen-customer-info-panel.tsx), which builds the panel's Phone/Contact #/Email/Balance/Address/City/State/Zip rows for whatever interaction is open, had ALWAYS synthesized those values independently from a `recordId`-seeded hash — it never once read `CREATE_NEW_CUSTOMERS`'s own `firstPhone`/`emailAddress`/`address1`/`city`/`state`/`postalCode`/`paymentBalance` fields, even though every real customer's `Interaction.customerId` already carries the exact value (`customer.customerId`) needed to look its own record right back up. A stale doc comment on this function claimed CREATE_NEW_CUSTOMERS "only carries id/name/customerId/channels, nothing address- or billing-shaped" — true when originally written, no longer true since those fields were added later for the Customers list view's own columns/filters, but nobody had gone back and wired the panel to actually use them. Fixed: `buildCustomerInfoFields` now looks up `CREATE_NEW_CUSTOMERS.find((c) => c.customerId === recordId)` and prefers that record's real fields — falling back to a real, currently-open voice/email channel's own address first (unchanged, still the most authoritative source when one exists), then the database record, then the old hash-synthesis only as a last resort for ad-hoc/unknown contacts with no database record at all. Since this is the one shared function every "Customer Information" consumer across all 3 page tiers already calls, this fix — and Marcus's own now-matching panel — both came from the same small, general change rather than a Marcus-only special case.

Files touched: `create-new-customers-data.ts` (lyra-ui — new hand-authored record), `agent-next-gen-customer-info-panel.tsx` (real `CREATE_NEW_CUSTOMERS` import, `buildCustomerInfoFields` database-lookup + doc comment rewrite), `AgentWorkspace2WithDeskPage.tsx` (`activeInteractionIsRealCustomer`'s dead special case removed + doc comment updated).

Eslint-clean on every touched file (no new errors).

### 84c. Follow-up: sequential card animation + bordered warning style

Per explicit follow-up, with a screenshot of §84b's two-block investigate detail card and a second screenshot of a bordered pale-yellow box labeled "warning-solid": "I would like these to animate in sequentially (all copilot cards should) - also - the second screenshot is the container I was looking for (border variant)."

**Sequential entrance, generalized to every `MarcusWebbCopilotCard` branch.** Previously only `CopilotTabContent`'s own info-box/Journey-Summary pair (and, as of §84b, nothing in `MarcusWebbCopilotCard` itself) had the staggered `animate-in fade-in-0 slide-in-from-bottom-1 fill-mode-backwards duration-500` entrance. Every one of `MarcusWebbCopilotCard`'s 5 return branches (decision, the dedicated reset-steps card, the generic options/investigate detail card, message-options, wrapup) now gets that same entrance on its own root element — reusing `CopilotTabContent`'s own established pacing/fill-mode reasoning rather than inventing a new one. The two-block detail branch (main card + `recommendation` warning callout, when present) keeps the `delay-300` stagger between them from §84b.

Getting this to actually replay per step required more than adding the classes: `MarcusWebbCopilotCard` is a single long-lived component instance (mounted once, its `state.copilotStep` prop just changes over time) rendering a different root `<div>` per branch at the SAME position in the tree — without a `key`, React just patches the existing DOM node's children/classes in place rather than creating a fresh element, so a className that stays textually identical between renders never gets the browser to replay its CSS animation. Fixed by giving each branch's root an explicit, step-derived `key` ("decision", "reset-detail", `` detail-${selectedAction} ``, `` message-options-${selectedAction} ``, "wrapup"), forcing React to unmount/remount a brand-new node on every step transition. Deliberately did NOT key on anything inside the reset-steps branch's own `resetSteps` flags — those flip while `copilotStep`/`selectedAction` both stay "detail"/"reset", so the same key correctly keeps that one card mounted throughout (clicking through the 4 reset steps doesn't replay the card's entrance each time; only its one-time arrival does).

**Warning callout restyled to the bordered "warning-solid" look.** The screenshot matched `Container`'s (container.tsx) own `warning-solid` variant exactly: `bg-lyra-status-warning-subtle border border-lyra-status-warning-strong` — a Storybook swatch labeled with that literal variant name. Rather than switching to `Container` (which has no built-in slot for a trailing action like `InlineNotification`'s new `action` prop from §84b) or widening `InlineNotification`'s own "warning" variant everywhere it's used in this file (which would also restyle the unrelated SLA-breach banner), added the matching border directly via `className="... border border-lyra-status-warning-strong"` on this one `InlineNotification` call site only — `cn()`'s tailwind-merge already handles the class addition safely alongside the variant's own classes.

Files touched: `AgentWorkspace2WithDeskPage.tsx` only (`MarcusWebbCopilotCard`'s 5 branches + its own top-of-function doc comment).

Verified tsc-clean (75-error baseline unchanged, none in this file) and eslint-clean (the 2 pre-existing `PanelLeftClose`/hand-rolled-`<button>` errors are unrelated to this change, confirmed present before it too).

### 84d. Follow-up: decision card stays (checkmark + disabled) and stacks like a chat; Copilot's "ask anything" input hidden

Per explicit follow-up, with a screenshot of the "How would you like to help Marcus?" decision card: "when an option is clicked in the how would you like to help, add a green checkmark and disable the menu item but keep the card (shift the copilot down like a normal chat) - also hide the ask anything input in copilot for now."

**Decision card persists as a resolved/read-only card instead of being replaced.** Previously `MarcusWebbCopilotCard` was a strict mutually-exclusive step machine — each `copilotStep` transition (`decision` → `detail` → `message-options`/`wrapup`) returned a completely different card, so picking an option made the decision card vanish outright. Restructured so `decisionCard` is now built unconditionally near the top of the function (instead of only inside the `copilotStep === "decision"` branch) and keeps rendering for every step from "decision" onward. `decisionMade` (`state.selectedAction != null`) flips it into its resolved state: every option `Button` gets `disabled`, and the one actually picked additionally renders a green `CheckCircle2` (`text-lyra-status-success-strong`, the same "done" color `resetSteps`' own step icons already use) ahead of its label. The rest of the old mutually-exclusive `if`/`return` chain (reset-detail / generic detail / message-options / wrapup) is now an `if`/`else if` chain that assigns to a `nextCard` variable instead of returning directly, and the function's final return wraps `decisionCard` + `nextCard` in one `flex flex-col gap-3` — so the current step's card renders BELOW the now-permanent decision card rather than replacing it, "shifting everything below it down" for free through normal document flow, exactly like a chat transcript where an earlier turn stays in place and the next one appends underneath. `decisionCard` keeps the same `key="decision"` from §84c's animation work, so it does NOT remount/replay when later steps change — it animates in once, on arrival, then just re-renders in place as its buttons flip to disabled.

**Copilot's own "Ask anything" `AIInput` footer hidden.** It was previously deliberately left ungated by `SHOW_CUSTOMER_INFO_AI_INPUT` (the flag §previously added to temporarily hide the Overview tab's own `AIInput`) — see that flag's own doc comment before this change, which called it out as "a distinct, always-on feature." Reversed per this explicit request: both call sites that render Copilot's singleLine `AIInput` (`CustomerInformationSidePanel`'s own `footer` prop, and `CustomerInfoHoverPreview`'s content-parity copy of the same footer) now also check `SHOW_CUSTOMER_INFO_AI_INPUT` (currently `false`), so flipping that one flag back to `true` restores both the Overview AND Copilot inputs together, per its own doc comment's "flip back to `true` to restore both" language, updated accordingly.

Files touched: `AgentWorkspace2WithDeskPage.tsx` (`MarcusWebbCopilotCard` restructure + doc comment), `agent-next-gen-customer-info-panel.tsx` (`SHOW_CUSTOMER_INFO_AI_INPUT` now also gates the 2 Copilot `AIInput` call sites, doc comments updated).

Verified tsc-clean (75-error baseline unchanged, none in either touched file) and eslint-clean (same 2 pre-existing `AgentWorkspace2WithDeskPage.tsx` errors as §84c, confirmed unrelated; `agent-next-gen-customer-info-panel.tsx` itself has zero eslint errors, only its usual pre-existing `react-refresh`/`exhaustive-deps` warnings).

### 84e. Follow-up: only disable the clicked option; auto-scroll to the latest card

Per explicit follow-up, with the same decision-card screenshot showing all 3 options grayed out: "1) only disable the clicked option 2) scroll to the latest card in copilot."

**Only the picked option disables now.** §84d's `disabled={decisionMade}` disabled all 3 buttons the instant any one was picked. Changed to `disabled={state.selectedAction === action}` — only the actually-chosen option disables (with its green checkmark, unchanged); the other two stay clickable, so the agent can still change their mind mid-flow. Clicking a different option just calls `onSelectAction` again, which naturally moves both the checkmark and the disabled state over since they're both derived from the same per-button `state.selectedAction === action` check — nothing extra to reset. The now-unused `decisionMade` local was removed.

**Copilot auto-scrolls to whichever card is newest.** A zero-height sentinel `<div ref={latestCardRef} />` was added at the very end of `MarcusWebbCopilotCard`'s returned stack (after `decisionCard` and `nextCard`), paired with a `useEffect` keyed on `state.copilotStep`/`state.selectedAction` — the same two values that determine which card is currently "latest" — that calls `latestCardRef.current?.scrollIntoView({ behavior: "smooth", block: "end" })` whenever either changes. `scrollIntoView` walks up to its own nearest scrollable ancestor automatically (the Copilot tab's `PanelContent` scroll region in agent-next-gen-customer-info-panel.tsx), so no ref or knowledge of that container was needed here at all. The hook had to be added ABOVE the pre-existing `copilotStep === "done"` early return, not below it — this component stays mounted (same call site, same instance, no remount) across a transition INTO "done", so a hook that only sometimes ran would violate the rules of hooks the moment that happened; called unconditionally on every render instead.

Files touched: `AgentWorkspace2WithDeskPage.tsx` only (`MarcusWebbCopilotCard`'s decision-button `disabled` condition, `decisionMade` removal, new `latestCardRef`/`useEffect`/sentinel, and its own top-of-function doc comment).

Verified tsc-clean (75-error baseline unchanged, none in this file) and eslint-clean (same 2 pre-existing `PanelLeftClose`/hand-rolled-`<button>` errors as every prior §84 sub-entry, confirmed unrelated — no new hook-order or unused-variable errors from this change).

### 84f. Follow-up: keep a completed option disabled even after picking a different one

Per explicit follow-up, with a screenshot showing "Give options to reset" checkmarked/disabled while "Investigate for malicious activity" and "Reset the password" are enabled: "once an item is completed keep it disabled."

**The bug.** §84e's `disabled={state.selectedAction === action}` check only ever looked at the single CURRENT pick. `selectedAction` holds exactly one value at a time, so the moment the agent selected a different option, the previously-picked option's `state.selectedAction === action` check flipped back to `false` — silently discarding its checkmark/disabled ("completed") appearance, even though that action had already been carried out.

**The fix.** Added a new `selectedActions: MarcusWebbAction[]` field to `MarcusWebbScenarioState` (`agent-next-gen-marcus-webb-scenario.ts`, defaulting to `[]` in `DEFAULT_STATE`) — a monotonically-growing, deduplicated history of every action ever picked, distinct from `selectedAction` (current pick only, unchanged, still drives which `nextCard` renders). `handleMarcusWebbSelectAction` now appends the newly-picked action into `selectedActions` (via an `includes` dedup check) in the same `saveMarcusWebbScenario` call that updates `selectedAction`/`copilotStep`. The decision card's button loop now computes `const picked = state.selectedActions.includes(action)` and drives both `disabled` and the green-checkmark render off `picked` instead of `state.selectedAction === action` — so once an option has ever been picked, it stays checkmarked/disabled for the rest of the scenario, while options never yet picked stay clickable, regardless of how many times the agent changes their mind afterward.

Files touched: `agent-next-gen-marcus-webb-scenario.ts` (`selectedActions` field + doc comment on `MarcusWebbScenarioState`, `DEFAULT_STATE`), `AgentWorkspace2WithDeskPage.tsx` (`handleMarcusWebbSelectAction` now appends to `selectedActions`, decision card's button loop reads `picked` off `selectedActions.includes(action)` instead of `state.selectedAction === action`, top-of-function doc comment updated to describe the new persistent-history behavior).

Verified tsc-clean (75 total errors / 72 app-side / 3 lyra-ui-side, unchanged; zero errors in either touched file related to this change — the 11 pre-existing `AgentWorkspace2WithDeskPage.tsx` errors listed are all unrelated module-resolution/implicit-any/ReactNode/LucideIcon issues predating this change) and eslint-clean (same 2 pre-existing `PanelLeftClose`/hand-rolled-`<button>` errors, confirmed unrelated; zero errors in `agent-next-gen-marcus-webb-scenario.ts`).

### 84g. Follow-up: "Not yet" on wrap-up should only dismiss that card

Per explicit follow-up, with a screenshot showing the resolved decision card (one option checkmarked/disabled, per §84f) stacked above the "Update status and dismiss?" wrap-up card: "if the user selects not yet, just dismiss that card do not remove the How would you like to help Marcus card."

**The bug.** `handleMarcusWebbWrapUp(false)` (the "Not yet" button) set `copilotStep: "done"` — the same terminal step used for a real dismiss. `MarcusWebbCopilotCard` early-returns `null` the instant `copilotStep === "done"` (see that check's own doc comment, added back in §84d's decision-card-persists work), which takes the ENTIRE card stack down, including the now-permanently-resolved decision card built above it — not just the wrap-up prompt the agent actually declined.

**The fix.** Added a new `"idle"` value to `MarcusWebbCopilotStep` (`agent-next-gen-marcus-webb-scenario.ts`), distinct from `"done"`: `"done"` still nulls the whole card (real dismiss, via "Yes, update & dismiss"), while `"idle"` deliberately has no matching branch in `MarcusWebbCopilotCard`'s `nextCard` if/else chain, so `nextCard` stays `null` while the decision card — built unconditionally, independent of `copilotStep`, per §84d — keeps rendering exactly as it does for any other step. `handleMarcusWebbWrapUp(false)` now sets `copilotStep: "idle"` instead of `"done"`. Nothing in the scenario ever transitions back out of `"idle"`, since the wrap-up prompt is scripted to fire only once (after the wrapup-send simulated reply).

Files touched: `agent-next-gen-marcus-webb-scenario.ts` (`"idle"` added to `MarcusWebbCopilotStep` union + doc comment), `AgentWorkspace2WithDeskPage.tsx` (`handleMarcusWebbWrapUp`'s `false` branch now sets `copilotStep: "idle"`, doc comment updated).

Verified tsc-clean (75 total errors, unchanged baseline; zero errors in either touched file) and eslint-clean (same 2 pre-existing `AgentWorkspace2WithDeskPage.tsx` errors, confirmed unrelated; zero errors in `agent-next-gen-marcus-webb-scenario.ts`).

### 84h. Follow-up: reset steps as checkboxes instead of buttons

Per explicit follow-up, with a screenshot showing reset-step text cut off with a horizontal scrollbar: "the resetting steps do not wrap - maybe don't have the entire item a button but have a checkbox next to the item that reveals the content and populates the chat when checked and once completed it disables."

**The wrapping bug.** Each reset step was a full-width `variant="ghost"` `Button` (icon + step text as children). `buttonVariants`'s base class (`button.tsx`, lyra-ui) always includes `whitespace-nowrap` — there's an opt-in `wrap` prop (`Button.stories.tsx`'s own size scale) that overrides it, but the reset-step buttons never set it — so long step text (e.g. "Verify Marcus's identity (date of birth or last 4 of SSN)") overflowed instead of wrapping, producing the horizontal scrollbar in the screenshot.

**The redesign.** Rather than opt into `wrap` on the same `Button`, replaced each step's `Button` with a `Checkbox` (lyra-ui) + a hand-laid-out `<span className="flex-1 min-w-0">` for the step text — NOT `Checkbox`'s own built-in `label` prop, since that renders inside an `inline-flex` wrapper sized to its content rather than one free to wrap within the card. `checked` and `disabled` are both driven off the same `steps.*` boolean (`identityVerified`/`passwordGenerated`/`emailSent`/`loginConfirmed`) — checking a step calls its existing one-shot `onReset*` handler (unchanged; each already idempotent/no-arg, still reveals the SSN/generated-password inline content and still populates the chat composer via `setMarcusWebbComposerPrefill`, exactly as the old button-click did), and once checked the checkbox is `disabled`, so — per the request's "once completed it disables" — there's no way to uncheck a completed step. The `!steps.*` guard in each `onCheckedChange` callback is a defensive no-op on top of that (Radix's Checkbox won't even fire the callback once `disabled`). Completed step text also gets a muted/line-through treatment (`text-lyra-fg-secondary line-through`) as a checklist-style "done" cue, replacing the old green-vs-gray `CheckCircle2` icon color distinction (the checkbox's own checked-fill now carries that signal instead).

Files touched: `AgentWorkspace2WithDeskPage.tsx` only (`Checkbox` added to the `@nicecxone/lyra-ui` import list, the 4 reset-step rows in `MarcusWebbCopilotCard`'s reset-detail branch rebuilt from `Button` to `Checkbox`+`span`, that branch's own doc comment updated).

Verified tsc-clean (75-error baseline unchanged, none in this file) and eslint-clean (same 2 pre-existing `PanelLeftClose`/hand-rolled-`<button>` errors, confirmed unrelated — no new errors from the `Checkbox` import or JSX change).

### 84i. Follow-up: defer decision-option disable until the reply is actually sent

Per explicit follow-up, with a screenshot showing "Investigate for malicious activity" AND "Give options to reset" both already checked/disabled on the top decision card while the "Reset options for Marcus" detail card below was still mid-progress (unchecked items, "Mark steps complete" not yet clicked): "don't fully disable steps until they are complete (ie. the agent has sent a reply to the customer)."

**The bug.** Since §84f, `handleMarcusWebbSelectAction` appended the clicked option into `selectedActions` (the permanent checked/disabled history) the INSTANT it was picked — before the detail steps were finished, before "Mark steps complete," before any suggested reply was even sent. So an option read as "completed" on the decision card the moment the agent started it, not when they actually finished it — which is what produced the screenshot's inconsistent-looking state (an option shown as done while its own detail card was plainly still in progress).

**The fix.** `handleMarcusWebbSelectAction` no longer touches `selectedActions` at all — it only ever sets `selectedAction` (still drives which `nextCard` shows, unchanged). The actual "this option is done" signal moved to `handleSendMessage`'s existing `isMarcusWebbWrapupSend` check (`interactionId === MARCUS_WEBB_ID && copilotStep === "message-options"` — already the established "the agent just sent Marcus's suggested reply" signal used to trigger the customer's thank-you reply and the wrap-up card, per the scenario's original design): when that's true, `marcusWebbState.selectedAction` gets appended into `selectedActions` (deduplicated), fired synchronously at send time — not deferred into the 2500ms simulated-customer-reply `setTimeout` — since the completion signal per the request is the AGENT's send, not the customer's reply landing afterward. Renamed the decision card's local `picked` variable to `completed` to match the corrected semantics, and updated the doc comments on `selectedActions` (agent-next-gen-marcus-webb-scenario.ts), `MarcusWebbCopilotCard`'s top-of-function comment, and `handleMarcusWebbSelectAction`/`handleSendMessage` accordingly. Net effect: an option stays enabled and re-pickable for as long as it's merely in progress (picked but not yet sent), and only locks in as permanently checked/disabled once the agent has actually sent that option's reply to the customer.

Files touched: `AgentWorkspace2WithDeskPage.tsx` (`handleMarcusWebbSelectAction` no longer appends to `selectedActions`; `handleSendMessage` now does, in the `isMarcusWebbWrapupSend` branch; decision-card `picked`→`completed` rename; 3 doc comments updated), `agent-next-gen-marcus-webb-scenario.ts` (`selectedActions`' own doc comment updated to describe the send-time completion signal).

Verified tsc-clean (75 total errors, unchanged baseline; zero errors in either touched file) and eslint-clean (same 2 pre-existing `AgentWorkspace2WithDeskPage.tsx` errors, confirmed unrelated; zero errors in `agent-next-gen-marcus-webb-scenario.ts`).

### 84j. Follow-up: show the in-progress option in its own "active" state

Per explicit follow-up, with a screenshot of the decision card mid-flow (one option checkmarked/disabled, one plain outline, and the actually-in-progress one with no visual distinction from the untouched one): "show the active option in an active state (no checkmark and disabled until completed)."

Added a third visual state to the decision card's per-button logic, alongside the existing `completed` (checkmark + `disabled`, §84f/§84i) and plain-untouched states: `active = !completed && state.selectedAction === action` — the option currently picked but not yet sent, explicitly with NO checkmark and NOT `disabled` (matching the request's parenthetical), consistent with §84i's "stays enabled/re-pickable until the reply is actually sent" behavior.

**First attempt (superseded below).** Gave `active` a filled `bg-lyra-bg-surface-sunken` background via a `cn(...)` class merge — same token the reset-detail card's own SSN/password reveal boxes use elsewhere in this file.

Swapped to the design system's own established selected/pressed-toggle combo instead of another one-off tint — `bg-lyra-bg-active-subtle border-lyra-border-active text-lyra-fg-active-strong`, the exact same 3-class treatment `ToggleGroup`'s own `selected` state uses (toggle-group.tsx) — for a clearly "on/pressed" look consistent with the rest of the system's active-state pattern.

### 84k. Follow-up: defer the reset-step checkboxes' "completed" look until the reply is sent

Per explicit follow-up, with a screenshot of the reset-detail card's first step already showing full checkmark + strikethrough right after being clicked: "don't set the checked as completed until after the message is sent."

Same principle as §84i (the top-level decision options), now applied to the 4 individual reset-step checkboxes (§84h): clicking a step still fires its one-shot `onReset*` handler immediately (still reveals the SSN/generated-password boxes, still populates the composer — unchanged), but the checkbox's own "completed" look — full checkmark, strikethrough text, `disabled` — no longer appears the instant that handler runs. It now waits on the same `state.selectedActions.includes("reset")` signal §84i introduced (appended in `handleSendMessage`'s `isMarcusWebbWrapupSend` branch once the agent actually sends the reply).

In between — the step's action has run, but the reply hasn't been sent yet — each checkbox reads `"indeterminate"` rather than `true`, via a new `checkboxState(done)` helper mapping `false → false`, `true` (not yet sent) → `"indeterminate"`, `true` (sent) → `true`. Radix's `Checkbox` already renders `"indeterminate"` with the same filled/on background as `true` but a dash (`Minus`) instead of a checkmark (checkbox.tsx, unchanged) — a natural three-state fit with no new lyra-ui work needed, reusing the exact same "active but not yet locked in" idea as the decision card's own pressed state (§84j) without inventing a fourth custom style. `disabled` and the text's strikethrough styling both gate on `done && messageSent` now, not `done` alone, so a clicked-but-unsent step stays enabled/re-clickable (harmless — the underlying `onReset*` handlers are idempotent) and its text stays normal weight until the reply actually goes out.

Files touched: `AgentWorkspace2WithDeskPage.tsx` only (`MarcusWebbCopilotCard`'s reset-detail branch: new `messageSent`/`checkboxState` locals, all 4 step `Checkbox`/`disabled`/text-class call sites, updated doc comment).

Verified tsc-clean (75-error baseline unchanged, none in this file) and eslint-clean (same 2 pre-existing `PanelLeftClose`/hand-rolled-`<button>` errors, confirmed unrelated).

*(Superseded by §84l below: the "agent send" completion signal used here was corrected to "customer response.")*

### 84l. Follow-up: completion means the customer responds, not the agent sends

Per explicit follow-up, with a screenshot of a reset step already showing its `"indeterminate"` dash: "ok good but once the step is completed (ie. the customer responds) then make it checked and disabled."

§84i had deliberately fired the `selectedActions` append synchronously at the agent's send moment, reasoning at the time that "the completion signal is the AGENT's send, not the customer's simulated thank-you landing afterward." This explicit follow-up corrects that reasoning: completion means the CUSTOMER responding, not the agent sending. Moved the `selectedActions` append out of the synchronous part of `handleSendMessage` and into its existing 2500ms simulated-customer-reply `setTimeout` callback — the same place `copilotStep: "wrapup"` already gets set once Marcus's thank-you message lands — merged into that same `saveMarcusWebbScenario(...)` call. `marcusWebbState.selectedAction` is snapshotted into a new `marcusWebbCompletedAction` local right alongside `isMarcusWebbWrapupSend` (same "capture now, read inside the timeout later" reasoning that const already followed), since 2.5 real seconds pass before the callback fires and a live read could theoretically be stale.

Net effect: both the decision card's chosen option (§84f/§84i/§84j) and, for "reset," its own 4 step checkboxes (§84h/§84k) now show their filled/`"indeterminate"` "in progress" look for the FULL 2.5s between the agent's send and the customer's simulated reply, and only flip to checked+strikethrough+`disabled` once that reply actually lands — matching a real "the customer confirmed/acknowledged this" completion signal rather than merely "the agent tried." Updated every doc comment across both files that previously described the "agent send" signal (§84i/§84j/§84k's own reasoning blocks, `handleMarcusWebbSelectAction`, the reset-detail branch, and `selectedActions`' own field doc comment in agent-next-gen-marcus-webb-scenario.ts) to describe this corrected "customer response" signal instead.

Files touched: `AgentWorkspace2WithDeskPage.tsx` (`handleSendMessage`: removed the synchronous append, added `marcusWebbCompletedAction` snapshot, merged the append into the existing wrap-up `setTimeout`'s `saveMarcusWebbScenario` call; several doc comments updated), `agent-next-gen-marcus-webb-scenario.ts` (`selectedActions`' own doc comment updated).

Verified tsc-clean (75-error baseline unchanged, zero errors in either touched file) and eslint-clean (same 2 pre-existing `AgentWorkspace2WithDeskPage.tsx` errors, confirmed unrelated; zero errors in `agent-next-gen-marcus-webb-scenario.ts`).

### 84m. Follow-up: the reset flow's own 4 checkboxes need their own per-step completion signal, not the scenario-wide one

Per explicit follow-up, with a screenshot of a real agent→customer→reply exchange for "Verify Marcus's identity" where the checkbox still showed its `"indeterminate"` dash instead of flipping to checked+disabled: "it's not updating in the copilot card to checked and disabled."

§84l's fix only covers `selectedActions`, which is appended exactly ONCE — inside `isMarcusWebbWrapupSend`'s branch of the customer-reply `setTimeout`, which only fires for the single final suggested-reply send that resolves the whole scenario (`copilotStep === "message-options"`). It never fires for any of the reset action's own 4 step-message/reply round trips, which all happen earlier while `copilotStep` is still `"detail"` — so those checkboxes had no completion signal to read at all and were stuck showing `"indeterminate"` forever (§84l's "Net effect" paragraph describing the reset checkboxes flipping via `selectedActions` was wrong for this case).

Added a parallel, per-step signal instead of trying to overload `selectedActions`:
- New `resetStepsConfirmed: MarcusWebbResetStepsProgress` field on `MarcusWebbScenarioState` (`agent-next-gen-marcus-webb-scenario.ts`) — same shape as `resetSteps` (`identityVerified`/`passwordGenerated`/`emailSent`/`loginConfirmed`), but tracking CUSTOMER confirmation of each step rather than the agent having clicked it. Defaults to all-`false`, added to `DEFAULT_STATE`.
- New exported `MARCUS_WEBB_RESET_STEP_ORDER` constant (same file) — the canonical step order, used to figure out which step a given reply is confirming.
- New `isMarcusWebbResetStepSend` const in `handleSendMessage` (`AgentWorkspace2WithDeskPage.tsx`): true for any send while `selectedAction === "reset"` and `copilotStep === "detail"` (i.e., every reset-flow send that ISN'T the final wrap-up one — mutually exclusive with `isMarcusWebbWrapupSend`, since one requires `copilotStep === "detail"` and the other `"message-options"`). Inside the same 2500ms customer-reply `setTimeout`, when true, finds the first step that's done (`resetSteps[key]`) but not yet confirmed (`!resetStepsConfirmed[key]`) via `MARCUS_WEBB_RESET_STEP_ORDER.find(...)` and flips its `resetStepsConfirmed` entry — advancing one step at a time in order as replies land, without needing exact message-to-reply pairing.
- `MarcusWebbCopilotCard`'s reset-detail branch now reads BOTH `resetSteps` and `resetStepsConfirmed` per checkbox: unclicked → `false`, clicked but not yet confirmed → `"indeterminate"`, confirmed → `true` + `disabled` + strikethrough. Same 3-state Radix Checkbox pattern as before, just keyed off the new per-step field instead of the scenario-wide one.

Files touched: `agent-next-gen-marcus-webb-scenario.ts` (`resetStepsConfirmed` field + doc comment, `MARCUS_WEBB_RESET_STEP_ORDER` constant, `DEFAULT_STATE` update), `AgentWorkspace2WithDeskPage.tsx` (`MARCUS_WEBB_RESET_STEP_ORDER` import, `isMarcusWebbResetStepSend` const + its confirmation-advancing logic in `handleSendMessage`'s customer-reply `setTimeout`, reset-detail branch's `checkboxState`/`stepTextClassName` reworked to take a step key and read both `resetSteps`/`resetStepsConfirmed`, all 4 `Checkbox` call sites updated).

Verified tsc-clean (75-error baseline unchanged, zero errors in either touched file) and eslint-clean (same 2 pre-existing `AgentWorkspace2WithDeskPage.tsx` errors, confirmed unrelated; zero errors in `agent-next-gen-marcus-webb-scenario.ts`).

Its `allStepsDone` check now reads `identityVerified && passwordGenerated && loginConfirmed` (was 4 flags, now 3). The remaining checkbox for `loginConfirmed` now reads `config.items[2]` (was `items[3]`) to match the shortened items array.

## 89. Agent leg connection status now survives switching between Agent Workspace tiers, without the toast re-firing on that switch

Per explicit request: "the agent leg disconnected toast should only fire after the login - keep the state of the agent leg consistent if the user goes from premium to advanced to basic."

Root cause: "Agent Workspace 2.0 Premium" (`AgentWorkspace2WithDeskPage.tsx`), "Agent Workspace 2.0 Advanced" (`AgentWorkspaceAdvancedPage.tsx`), and the plain "agent" page (`AgentNextGenPage.tsx`) are each their own separate top-level route/component (`App.tsx`) — switching between them via the app-picker dropdown fully unmounts one and mounts another, it's not a re-render of one shared page. `AgentProfile` (lyra-ui) has always owned its own `agentLegStatus` as plain internal `useState`, defaulting to `"disconnected"` on every mount with no way for a consumer to seed it — so an agent who'd actually connected the leg on Premium saw it reported disconnected again the moment they switched to Advanced, with nothing about the leg itself having changed.

Fixed with two pieces:
- **lyra-ui**: added an `initialAgentLegStatus?: "disconnected" | "connected"` prop to `AgentProfile` — seeds the internal `useState` instead of hardcoding `"disconnected"`. Deliberately excludes `"connecting"` (a transient in-flight state with no sensible meaning as a starting point), and deliberately NOT a controlled prop — `AgentProfile` still owns the value after mount exactly as before. `isFirstAgentLegRender`'s existing mount-skip guard applies to whatever this seeds too, same as it always did for the plain `"disconnected"` default — hydrating a previous page's value is not itself a new connect/disconnect event, so `onAgentLegStatusChange` (and therefore a consumer's own toast) never fires just from seeding it. This is exactly the mechanism behind the "should only fire after the login" half of the request: carrying state across a tier switch must stay silent, never re-announce a status that hasn't actually changed.
- **agent-next-gen-v2**: new shared module `agent-next-gen-agent-leg-state.ts`, same in-memory-cache-backed-by-`localStorage` pattern `agent-next-gen-case-database.ts` already established — `readAgentLegStatus()`/`saveAgentLegStatus()`, storing only the two settled states (never `"connecting"`). Each of the 3 pages now: seeds `AgentProfile`'s `initialAgentLegStatus` from `readAgentLegStatus()` at mount (`useState(() => readAgentLegStatus())`, matching this file's existing read-once-at-mount seed pattern), and calls `saveAgentLegStatus(status)` unconditionally at the top of its own `fireAgentLegStatusToast`, ahead of the existing welcome-modal deferral — the underlying status is persisted the instant it actually changes; only the TOAST announcing it is ever deferred behind the welcome modal, not the state a later page mount needs to pick up from.

Files touched: `agent-profile.tsx` (lyra-ui: `initialAgentLegStatus` prop + doc comment, `agentLegStatus`'s `useState` initializer), `agent-next-gen-agent-leg-state.ts` (new file), `AgentNextGenPage.tsx`/`AgentWorkspace2WithDeskPage.tsx`/`AgentWorkspaceAdvancedPage.tsx` (import, `initialAgentLegStatus` seed state, `saveAgentLegStatus` call in `fireAgentLegStatusToast`, `initialAgentLegStatus` prop wired into each page's own `AgentProfile` call site).

Verified tsc-clean (lyra-ui: zero errors in `agent-profile.tsx`; app: 75-error baseline unchanged, same known pre-existing errors in all 3 touched page files, zero in the new module) and eslint-clean (same 2 pre-existing errors per page file, confirmed unrelated; lyra-ui has no separate eslint config — its own `lint` script is `tsc --noEmit`, already covered above).

## 90. Donut chart hover tooltip was getting clipped by its own card

Per explicit request, with a screenshot of the home screen's Activity donut chart showing a hover tooltip ("...le: 17") cut off at the left edge of its card: "on the home page the hover is being cut off on the donut chart - it should not be cut off by the card."

Root cause: `DonutChart` (lyra-ui, built on the shared `Chart`/`echarts-for-react` wrapper) uses ECharts' default tooltip behavior, which renders the tooltip as a plain `position: absolute` DOM node appended as a *child* of the chart's own container div. That makes it subject to clipping by any ancestor with `overflow: hidden` — in this case the home screen's Activity card (a rounded `DashboardCard`), which clips the tooltip the instant it would extend past the card's own bounds (e.g. hovering a slice near the card's left edge).

Fixed by adding `appendToBody: true` to `DonutChart`'s `tooltip` option. This is ECharts' standard fix for exactly this problem — it renders the tooltip as a direct child of `document.body` instead, escaping every ancestor's overflow/stacking context, the same way this library's own portal-based popovers/tooltips already do elsewhere. Scoped to `DonutChart` only (not `bar-chart.tsx`, `gauge-chart.tsx`, `treemap-chart.tsx`, `sparkline.tsx`, or the shared `chart.tsx` wrapper) — no existing convention for this in the codebase, and no other chart component was reported as affected.

Files touched: `donut-chart.tsx` (lyra-ui: `tooltip.appendToBody: true` + doc comment).

Verified tsc-clean: zero errors in `donut-chart.tsx` (lyra-ui `tsc --noEmit`), and zero new errors in its app-side consumer `agent-next-gen-interaction-dashboard.tsx` (only the 2 pre-existing, unrelated `LucideIcon` type errors at lines 1246/1379 — confirmed present before this change too). lyra-ui has no separate eslint config (its own `lint` script is `tsc --noEmit`), already covered above.

## 94. Customer Sentiment card turns Positive after the agent's first reply to Marcus

Per explicit request: "after the agent responds for the first time, update the sentiment to be positive."

Added `agentHasReplied: boolean` to `MarcusWebbScenarioState` (agent-next-gen-marcus-webb-scenario.ts, defaults `false`), set once in `AgentWorkspace2WithDeskPage.tsx`'s `handleSendMessage` — at the synchronous moment the agent's own message is applied to the interaction (not the delayed 2.5s simulated-customer-reply callback), guarded by `interactionId === MARCUS_WEBB_ID && !marcusWebbState.agentHasReplied` so it only ever fires once, on the agent's genuinely first message. Deliberately a standalone signal rather than piggybacking on `copilotStep`/`selectedAction`/`selectedActions`: those all depend on the decision card, which §92 hid behind `SHOW_MARCUS_DECISION_CARD`, so nothing about this scenario's existing step machinery reliably fires anymore — this needed its own "has the agent sent anything yet" check independent of that flow.

## 96. Copilot's hover preview was missing the docked panel's `copilotExtra` content

Fixed by adding `copilotExtra?: React.ReactNode` to `CustomerInfoHoverPreview`'s own props (forwarded to `CustomerInformationPanelBody` exactly like the docked panel already does), then wiring the hover preview's call site in `AgentWorkspace2WithDeskPage.tsx` with the exact same `activeInteraction.id === MARCUS_WEBB_ID ? <MarcusWebbCopilotCard .../> : undefined` expression the docked panel's own `copilotExtra` prop already uses — a second, independent `MarcusWebbCopilotCard` instance (not the same element relocated), reading the same shared `marcusWebbState` and firing the same handlers, same "duplicate the JSX per call site" pattern this hover preview already follows for `matchState`/`tabs`.

## 98. Contact History's "Conversation" section now shows a dismissed row's real transcript when one exists

Per explicit bug report, with a screenshot of Marcus Webb's own Contact History detail panel showing generic wrap-up chatter ("Sorry, one more question before we wrap up." / "Happy to help — have a great rest of your day!" / "Hi, I wanted to follow up on this.") that never actually happened in his real, scripted chat: "contact history of Marcus Webb should reflect the actual conversation in the right conversation area of the interior panel."

Root cause: `ContactHistoryEntryDetail`'s "Conversation"/"Transcript" section has always rendered `buildContactHistoryMessages(entry)` unconditionally — a deterministic (`entry.id`-hashed) pool of 4 generic customer/agent lines, picked because this app has "no real backend/transcript data for any Contact History row" per that builder's own doc comment. That was true for the 5 hand-authored rows and the `CREATE_NEW_CUSTOMERS`-backed rows this feature originally shipped for, but it's no longer true for a row logged from a real, live `Interaction` being dismissed (`buildDismissedContactHistoryEntry`) — that interaction's own `liveMessages` (the exact same data the record view's live transcript reads) is real, scripted content, and Marcus Webb's scenario is the clearest case where it visibly diverged from what the synthesized filler showed.

Fixed generally, not with a Marcus-specific special case (same "prefer real data when it exists" principle §512 already established for `buildCustomerInfoFields`): added `messages?: ContactHistoryMessage[]` to `ContactHistoryEntry` (agent-next-gen-contact-history.tsx), populated by `buildDismissedContactHistoryEntry` from `interaction.liveMessages[primaryChannel.id ?? primaryChannel.type]` — the same thread-key scheme `primaryChannel` itself is already resolved with — mapped into this file's own `ContactHistoryMessage` shape (`sender`/`text` carried straight across, `timestamp` → `timestampDisplay`). Left `undefined` (not `[]`) when the primary channel never actually accumulated real messages (every hand-authored/customer-fixture row, and any dismissed row whose primary channel was voice — this app's `InteractionComposer` typed-message flow, the only thing that ever populates `liveMessages`, doesn't apply there). `ContactHistoryEntryDetail` now reads `entry.messages ?? buildContactHistoryMessages(entry)` — a real transcript wins when one exists, every other row's synthesized filler is completely unchanged.

In its place, one new field, `customerRespondedToFirstMessage: boolean` (agent-next-gen-marcus-webb-scenario.ts), set inside `handleSendMessage`'s existing delayed simulated-customer-reply `window.setTimeout` callback (not the agent's own send) — guarded by `isFirstMarcusWebbAgentMessage`, a local snapshot of `!marcusWebbState.agentHasReplied` taken BEFORE that field flips at send time, so this only ever fires for the reply that lands after the agent's true first message; a "reply" following any later message does nothing here. Both `marcusSummaryCards`' Customer Sentiment card and `passwordResetKbCard` now read this one field instead of their own separate ones.

## 100. Fixed two real console warnings: Tooltip controlled↔uncontrolled, and an invalid `<div>` inside `<tr>`

Per explicit bug report, with a screenshot of the console flooded with dozens of copies of "Tooltip is changing from controlled to uncontrolled. Components should not switch from controlled to uncontrolled (or vice versa)... tooltip.tsx:143": "i keep getting these console warnings. can you investigate and fix?" A second console screenshot, sent mid-investigation, showed a separate, unrelated pair of warnings — "In HTML, `<div>` cannot be a child of `<tr>`. This will cause a hydration error." / "`<tr>` cannot contain a nested `<div>`" — pointing at `agent-next-gen-customers-table.tsx:728` and lyra-ui's `table.tsx:397`. Both are genuine bugs (not demo-data artifacts), fixed at the root:

**Tooltip (lyra-ui, `tooltip.tsx`).** `TooltipPrimitive.Root`'s `open` prop was `disabled || !allowOpen ? false : undefined` — a literal `false` (a CONTROLLED value) for as long as either guard held, then `undefined` (UNCONTROLLED, handing control to Radix's own internal state) the instant `allowOpen` flipped true ~50ms after mount (see `allowOpen`'s own doc comment — a guard against a tooltip firing on mount from a synthesized pointer event). React fixes whether a component started controlled or uncontrolled at its very first render and warns on any later switch — since `allowOpen` starts `false` and flips true on every single mount, this fired on literally every `Tooltip` in the app, ~50ms after it appeared. Fixed by giving the component its own `open`/`setOpen` state (wired through `onOpenChange`, same as any fully-controlled Radix primitive) that stands in for Radix's internal one once neither guard is active — `open` is now a plain boolean for the component's entire lifetime, never `undefined`, so there's no controlled/uncontrolled transition to warn about. The guards work exactly as before (forcing `false` while active); only the mechanism changed.

**Customer table row (`agent-next-gen-customers-table.tsx`).** `CustomerChannelStack` (§407) returns a plain `<div>` — its own `absolute inset-y-0 left-0` hover overlay (§412) — and was rendered directly as a sibling of the row's `<TableCell>`s inside `<TableRow>` (a `<tr>`). The HTML table content model only permits `<td>`/`<th>` as direct children of `<tr>`; a raw `<div>` there is invalid markup, which is exactly what both warnings were reporting (and, per the first warning's own text, a genuine SSR/hydration-mismatch risk, not just a lint nit — browsers' own HTML parsers silently relocate an invalid `<div>` out of a `<tr>` during initial parse, which can diverge from what React's virtual DOM expects). Fixed by wrapping `CustomerChannelStack` in a real `<td role="presentation" style={{ display: "contents" }}>` at its one call site — a real `<td>` element satisfies `<tr>`'s content model (fixing the warnings), while `display: contents` removes that `<td>`'s own box entirely so it contributes no width/gap to the row's flex layout and doesn't become a new CSS positioning ancestor for the overlay `<div>`'s own `absolute` positioning — the row's own `relative` (`leadingChannelStack && "relative"`, unchanged) is still what the div positions against, so the visual result is identical to before this fix. Confirmed via `grep` that `CustomerChannelStack` has exactly one call site in the app, so no other consumer needed the same fix.

Files touched: `tooltip.tsx` (lyra-ui — `open`/`setOpen` state + `onOpenChange` wiring replacing the `false`/`undefined` toggle), `agent-next-gen-customers-table.tsx` (`CustomerChannelStack`'s call site wrapped in a `<td role="presentation" style={{ display: "contents" }}>`).

Verified tsc-clean (app: 51-error baseline unchanged, zero errors in `agent-next-gen-customers-table.tsx`; lyra-ui's own `tsc --noEmit` — its `lint` script — came back with only its 2 pre-existing errors, `admin-shell.tsx`'s "Expected 1 arguments" and `list-item.tsx`'s `ReactNode`-not-assignable, zero in `tooltip.tsx`) and eslint-clean for the app-side file (same pre-existing warnings/errors only — 5 `react-refresh/only-export-components` warnings, 2 hand-rolled-button errors, 1 unused `InteractionsTable` import, none introduced by or near this change).

The current `Plus` icon appears in three "launch new interaction" spots: lyra-ui's `CreateNew`'s main "New Outbound" trigger, `OutboundAddButton`'s default icon, and `AddChannelAdHocButton`'s trigger (the record-header ad-hoc Add Channel popover). Candidates that read clearly as "start a conversation/contact" rather than "add a person":

- **`MessageSquarePlus`** — a chat bubble with a plus, reads as "start a new conversation" across any channel (chat, SMS, email), probably the most channel-agnostic option.

- **`Rocket`** — strongly reads as "launch," but skews playful/marketing rather than matching this app's other utilitarian glyphs (RefreshCw, UserPlus, etc.).

`MessageSquarePlus` is the strongest single-icon fit for "new interaction" generally (channel-agnostic, visually distinct from `UserPlus`'s person silhouette); `PhoneOutgoing` is worth it specifically wherever the trigger is voice-only. No code changed for this part.
