## 103. Agent leg now announces "not connected" on a genuine fresh login, still without re-firing on Premium/Advanced/Basic tier switches

Per explicit request: "the agent leg is automatically connecting - I want it to display a not connected toast but if connected, keep it connected when going to premium, advanced, basic (and likewise keep it disconnected but don't fire the toast again)."

Context: §89 already made the agent leg's CONNECTED/DISCONNECTED status itself survive switching between the 3 Agent Workspace tiers (`readAgentLegStatus`/`saveAgentLegStatus`, agent-next-gen-agent-leg-state.ts), and already suppressed the toast from re-firing on that switch (`AgentProfile`'s `isFirstAgentLegRender` mount-skip guard in lyra-ui, seeded via `initialAgentLegStatus` rather than always defaulting to `"disconnected"`). What §89 never did is announce the disconnected state on an actual fresh login at all — a brand-new browser tab starts genuinely disconnected (correct), but silently, with no toast, since seeding a value is deliberately NOT treated as a connect/disconnect event. What likely read as "automatically connecting" in the report is really this same tab persisting a `"connected"` status from earlier in the same session (§89 working as designed) with no earlier "you're disconnected" toast ever having been shown to make the later "still connected" carry-over legible as a return to a known state, rather than an unannounced default.

Fixed by adding one new export to agent-next-gen-agent-leg-state.ts: `consumeInitialAgentLegAnnouncement()`, backed by an in-memory-only module `let` (deliberately NOT persisted to `localStorage`, unlike this file's existing `cache`) — returns `true` the very first time it's called in a browser tab's lifetime, `false` every time after. This works because `App.tsx`'s top-level `App` component never actually unmounts when switching Agent Workspace tiers — Premium → Advanced → Basic is just a hash-route change that swaps which page component `App` renders (see `App.tsx`'s own `useHashRouter`) — so a plain module-level singleton naturally survives every tier switch within a tab while still resetting on a genuine browser reload (a fresh module instance), which is exactly the "login" vs. "switching tiers" distinction this needed. Deliberately consumed from inside a mount-only `useEffect` in each page, not a `useState` lazy initializer — this app runs under `React.StrictMode` (main.tsx), which double-invokes lazy initializers during render as an impurity check; that would silently burn a "consume once" flag on a throwaway, discarded render. Effects don't have that problem: StrictMode's dev-only effect double-invoke is mount → cleanup → mount, so a second invocation still correctly finds the flag already consumed by the first, and the user only ever sees the toast once either way.

Each of the 3 pages (AgentNextGenPage.tsx, AgentWorkspace2WithDeskPage.tsx, AgentWorkspaceAdvancedPage.tsx) got the same new mount effect, placed right after `fireAgentLegStatusToast`'s own definition:
```tsx
useEffect(() => {
  if (initialAgentLegStatus === "disconnected" && consumeInitialAgentLegAnnouncement()) {
    fireAgentLegStatusToast("disconnected");
  }
  // eslint-disable-next-line react-hooks/exhaustive-deps
}, []);
```
Reuses `fireAgentLegStatusToast` itself (not `showAgentLegToast` directly) so this goes through the exact same welcome-modal deferral every other agent-leg toast already does — if the welcome modal is still open at login, the announcement waits behind it like any other agent-leg toast, per §89's own existing behavior. Only fires for `"disconnected"` — a fresh login that's already carrying a CONNECTED status (e.g. a real browser reload after connecting earlier in the same tab) has nothing to announce; a genuine in-session connect event later is still covered by the ordinary `showAgentLegToast("connected")` success toast, unchanged. Net result: true login → disconnected → toast shown once; connect via the toast's own "Connect" button → stays connected across every later tier switch, no announcement repeats; never connect → stays disconnected across every later tier switch, same — the original disconnected toast (from login) is the only one ever shown, exactly matching "don't fire the toast again."

Files touched: `agent-next-gen-agent-leg-state.ts` (new `consumeInitialAgentLegAnnouncement` export + module-level flag), `AgentNextGenPage.tsx`/`AgentWorkspace2WithDeskPage.tsx`/`AgentWorkspaceAdvancedPage.tsx` (import + one new mount effect each).

Verified tsc-clean (51-error baseline unchanged) and eslint-clean across all 4 touched files (same pre-existing errors/warnings only — `PanelLeftClose` unused import, hand-rolled-button rule, a pre-existing `useMemo` missing-dep warning, and 3 pre-existing unused-eslint-disable-directive warnings elsewhere in each file, all shifted down by this change's own line insertions but not introduced by it; zero errors/warnings in agent-next-gen-agent-leg-state.ts, and the new effect's own `eslint-disable-next-line` was confirmed NOT flagged as unused, meaning it's correctly suppressing a real missing-dependency warning there).

## 104. Fixed §103's own root cause: agent leg still read "connected" on a genuine fresh login

Per explicit follow-up report: "it's still showing connected on initial log in - it should be disconnected."

Root cause: §103's `consumeInitialAgentLegAnnouncement()` correctly re-armed on every real browser reload (a fresh JS module instance), but `readAgentLegStatus()`/`saveAgentLegStatus()` — the functions that track the actual CONNECTED/DISCONNECTED value itself, from §89 — persisted `cache` to `localStorage`. `localStorage` survives a real reload or a brand-new tab, not just an in-app tier switch, so once the leg had ever been connected once (in any earlier session, including earlier testing), every subsequent fresh login kept reading that same stale `"connected"` value straight back — §103's new toast logic never even had a chance to fire, since it's gated on the status actually being `"disconnected"`.

Fixed by removing `localStorage` entirely from `agent-next-gen-agent-leg-state.ts` — `cache` is now a plain in-memory module variable, always starting `"disconnected"`. This still satisfies §89's original request (state survives Premium ↔ Advanced ↔ Basic) because `App.tsx`'s top-level `App` component never unmounts across a tier switch — only a genuine browser reload creates a fresh module instance, which is exactly when the status should reset. Net behavior now: real login → always starts disconnected → §103's toast fires; connect via the toast's "Connect" button → stays connected across every later tier switch this same tab, no toast repeats; a real reload → back to disconnected, toast fires again (a new login).

Files touched: `agent-next-gen-agent-leg-state.ts` (removed `localStorage`/`STORAGE_KEY`/`readCache`, `cache` is now a plain always-`"disconnected"`-by-default module variable; doc comments rewritten to explain why `localStorage` was wrong here).

Verified tsc-clean (51-error baseline unchanged) and eslint-clean (zero errors/warnings in the touched file).

## 105. Experimental InteriorPanel fork (1440px absolute-overlay breakpoint, no auto-full-screen) — agent-next-gen-v2 only, NOT applied to lyra-ui

Per explicit request: "for agent-next-gen-v1 I'd like to test the absolute position happening at 1440px parent container width and remove the full screen breakpoint entirely - if I like it I will apply it to lyra-ui" (corrected mid-conversation to target agent-next-gen-v2, not v1).

Both agent-next-gen-v1 and agent-next-gen-v2's own `vite.config.ts` alias `@nicecxone/lyra-ui` straight to lyra-ui's own `src/index.ts` — editing lyra-ui's real `interior-panel.tsx` directly would have changed behavior for BOTH apps (and anything else consuming lyra-ui) immediately, with no way to trial the idea first, contradicting "if I like it I will apply it to lyra-ui." Confirmed this trade-off with the user before proceeding (a temporary local fork scoped to just this app, vs. editing lyra-ui directly) and built the local-fork option.

New file `agent-next-gen-interior-panel-1440-test.tsx`: a full copy of lyra-ui's `InteriorPanel` (including `usePanelDragResize`, inlined since it's internal-only wiring not exported from lyra-ui's `index.ts`), with exactly two behavioral changes:
1. `isNarrow`'s absolute-overlay threshold: `parentWidth < 1024` → `parentWidth < 1440`.
2. `isAutoFullScreen` (the automatic <400px "no room for anything, go full-screen" breakpoint, added in §57 and lowered from 768px in that same section) removed entirely — every downstream reference (`fullScreenToggle`'s visibility, `dragHandle`'s visibility, `displayWidth`, and the absolute-vs-inline branch condition) had its `isAutoFullScreen` check dropped to match. The user-triggered `allowFullScreen` toggle (the Maximize2/Minimize2 button) is UNCHANGED — only the automatic, width-triggered full-screen behavior was requested to be removed, not the opt-in manual one.

One incidental fix during the copy: the original's full-screen toggle hand-rolls a `<button>`, which is fine in lyra-ui itself but tripped CLAUDE.md's "Rule zero" no-hand-rolled-button lint rule once copied into agent-next-gen-v2's own linted `src/` — swapped for lyra-ui's `ActionIconButton` atom (visually/behaviorally equivalent) so this experimental file stays lint-clean like everything else in the app.

Wired into the app by changing exactly the `InteriorPanel` import at all 4 real render sites — `AgentNextGenPage.tsx`, `AgentWorkspace2WithDeskPage.tsx`, `AgentWorkspaceAdvancedPage.tsx`, `agent-next-gen-customer-info-panel.tsx` (2 usages) — from `@nicecxone/lyra-ui` to `@/components/agent-next-gen-interior-panel-1440-test`, each with a comment explaining this is experimental and how to revert. `AgentNextGenPage.pre-split-backup.tsx` (a stale, non-live pre-refactor backup) was deliberately left untouched.

To make this permanent: port the two behavioral changes above into lyra-ui's own `interior-panel.tsx`, then delete the local fork file and revert the 4 import lines back to `@nicecxone/lyra-ui`. To abandon it: same revert, discard the fork file.

Verified tsc-clean (51-error baseline unchanged, zero errors attributable to the new file) and eslint-clean across all 5 touched files (same pre-existing errors/warnings only in the 4 page/panel files; zero in the new fork file after the `ActionIconButton` fix).

## 106. Added a visible "View Details" link to the session row, and a `compactHeader` mode that hides everything but it below 768px — so the right-hand action cluster stops wrapping

Per explicit request: "add an html link to the left of the chevron and right of the date in the session details that says 'View Details' and when the parent container drops below 768px just display the 'View Details' and the chevron (hide the rest of the copy) - my goal is to keep the action buttons in the top right and not wrap to the next line when the container is smaller."

**"View Details" link.** The session row's left-hand toggle (`TranscriptSessionSeparator`, agent-next-gen-transcript.tsx) already carried this exact string, but only as a hover `Tooltip` — never visible text. Added a real `<span>` reading "View Details" between the date and the chevron, styled `text-lyra-fg-link hover:underline` (the same inline-text-link token `DesktopDesignsPage.tsx`'s own link cell already uses, for consistency). The now-redundant `Tooltip` wrapper was removed — once the same string is always on-screen, a hover tooltip repeating it added nothing.

**`compactHeader` (the 768px behavior).** The "{n} Messages | # contactId · date" portion was pulled into its own inner `<span className="inline-flex items-center gap-1.5">` (preserving its internal spacing now that it's one flex item instead of several direct ones) and given a new `compactHeader` prop that adds `hidden` to it — leaving just "View Details" + the chevron once true. Per the stated goal ("keep the action buttons ... not wrap"): this row already uses `flex-wrap` (§14's own doc comment) specifically so the right-hand Consult/Transfer + Outcome + status tag + Unassign & Dismiss cluster drops to its own line rather than clipping when the row runs out of space — shrinking the LEFT side down to its shortest form frees up enough room that the right cluster usually doesn't need to wrap at all anymore.

Rather than adding a second `ResizeObserver` to measure this row's own width, `compactHeader` is fed from `InteractionTranscript`'s existing `transcriptBubbleFullWidth` state — the SAME `<768px` reading already measured off the shared scroll container (via one `ResizeObserver`, established in the "chat bubble full-width" breakpoint work) that this row already lives inside. One shared measurement, two consumers now.

Files touched: `agent-next-gen-transcript.tsx` (`TranscriptSessionSeparator`: new `compactHeader` prop + doc comment, row markup restructured into an inner hideable span + always-visible "View Details" link, `Tooltip` wrapper removed; `InteractionTranscript`'s own call site: `compactHeader={transcriptBubbleFullWidth}`).

Tied to the same flag as the date cluster itself rather than its own condition — once the date cluster hides, there's nothing left for a leading divider to separate "View Details" from. ## 115. "Personal Queue" chip: success/warning/critical color tiers + SLA-breach "!" icon

Per explicit request, with a screenshot of the "Personal Queue: Empty" chip: "make the personal queue chip success when empty and warn when there are assignments, make it red when an sla is breached in an assignment and add a ! icon."

**New shared helper.** Added `interactionHasBreachedSla(interaction, clockTick)` to `agent-next-gen-interaction-dashboard.tsx` (exported, alongside `Interaction`/`Thread`) — walks an Interaction's own threads and asks the same three questions each `InteractionNavItem` card's own inline per-channel severity computation already asks in the LeftNav render loop (closed-or-interaction-closed, Resolved, "has the customer actually written something"), then feeds the resulting wait time through the existing `getAwaitingSeverity` (agent-next-gen-shared-utils.ts) and checks for `"critical"` — i.e. genuinely breached, not just nearing breach. This is a small, deliberate re-derivation of that inline per-card logic rather than a shared extraction, since the card's own version is built inline per-card closing over card-local state; a header chip has no per-card render loop of its own and needs to ask this across the WHOLE `interactions` array at once.

**Wiring (all 3 pages).** Added a memoized `hasBreachedSlaAssignment = interactions.some(i => interactionHasBreachedSla(i, clockTick))` alongside each page's own `clockTick` state, so it re-evaluates on the same per-second tick every other elapsed/awaiting reading already uses.

**Chip color.** The chip's fixed "info" blue (`bg-lyra-status-info-subtle`/`text-lyra-status-info-strong`) is now conditional, three-tier, matching the same success/warning/critical language `getAwaitingSeverity` and the record-header channel tab's own escalation already use: `bg-lyra-status-success-subtle`/`text-lyra-status-success-strong` once the queue is empty, `bg-lyra-status-warning-subtle`/`text-lyra-status-warning-strong` once it holds any assignment at all, escalating to `bg-lyra-status-critical-subtle`/`text-lyra-status-critical-strong` the moment `hasBreachedSlaAssignment` is true — regardless of how many assignments are in the queue at that point.

**"!" icon.** A `CircleAlert` (lucide-react) renders next to the label only when `hasBreachedSlaAssignment` is true — the exact same icon `ChannelTab` (lyra-ui, channel-row.tsx) already reserves for a channel that's genuinely breached SLA (as opposed to `TriangleAlert` for merely nearing it), reused here rather than inventing a second "!" glyph for the same meaning.

Files touched: `agent-next-gen-interaction-dashboard.tsx` (new exported `interactionHasBreachedSla` helper + `getAwaitingSeverity` import); `AgentNextGenPage.tsx`/`AgentWorkspace2WithDeskPage.tsx`/`AgentWorkspaceAdvancedPage.tsx` (import `interactionHasBreachedSla` + `CircleAlert`; `hasBreachedSlaAssignment` memo; chip `className` and icon).

Verified tsc-clean (51-error baseline unchanged) and eslint-clean on the 3 page files (same pre-existing errors/warnings as prior sections; zero new). `agent-next-gen-interaction-dashboard.tsx` picked up one additional pre-existing-category `react-refresh/only-export-components` warning (23 → 24) — expected, not a regression: this file already mixes component and non-component exports throughout (the same warning already fires for `sortAssignments` and others), and the new `interactionHasBreachedSla` export is one more non-component export in that same already-established pattern.

Per explicit request, with a screenshot of a right-docked "Interaction Details" panel: "do not force interior panels to full width in agent-next-gen-v2 but they should shrink with the width of the parent container."

Scoped to the local experimental fork only (`agent-next-gen-interior-panel-1440-test.tsx`, §57/§105/§602 — not lyra-ui's own `interior-panel.tsx`, per that file's own "local copy, port to lyra-ui only if it sticks" convention). Previously `displayWidth` was either the raw drag/cookie-remembered width (`currentWidth`, default 350-425px) or, in the user-triggered full-screen state, a literal `"100%"` — neither was clamped to the panel's actual measured `parentWidth`, so a panel nested inside another narrow container (e.g. this one, opened from inside the docked Customer Information panel) could render wider than the space available and get visually cut off at the container edge. `displayWidth` is now `isFullScreen ? parentWidth : Math.min(currentWidth, parentWidth)` — both cases are bounded by the live `ResizeObserver`-measured parent width, so the panel shrinks to fit a narrow container instead of overflowing it, while keeping its ordinary size whenever the container has room.

Files touched: `agent-next-gen-interior-panel-1440-test.tsx` (`displayWidth` calc + doc comment, this app only).

Verified tsc-clean (277-error baseline unchanged; the file's own single pre-existing `@nicecxone/lyra-ui` module-resolution error is untouched, no new errors).

## 116. Home dashboard header switched to the identity/"Connect Agent Leg" treatment (2.0 only)

Per explicit request: "apply the header with greeting false to the agent-next-gen-v2." This brings lyra-ui's "Agent Home Dashboard" Storybook story's `greeting=false` header treatment (agent-dashboard.tsx's `resolveGreetingContent`, built across several earlier sessions in that repo — see its PROJECT_SUMMARY.md) into this app's own home dashboard header, which previously always showed the time-of-day "Good {Morning/Afternoon/Evening}, {name}" greeting + date subhead unconditionally.

**Title/subhead.** The dashboard's `PageHeader` (rendered in the scrollable body under `showPageHeader`, §sundry above) now reads `Agent John Smith` as its title (was `formatHeaderGreeting(CURRENT_AGENT_FIRST_NAME)`), and its subhead is `Agent ID: johnsmith329202` followed by one of three states depending on the agent leg's real connection status: a "Connect Agent Leg" link while disconnected, "Connecting..." during the transition, or "Connection Lag Time: 00:32" once connected — the exact same three-state pattern (and literal `CURRENT_AGENT_ID`/lag-time values) lyra-ui's own story uses. `CURRENT_AGENT_ID`/`CURRENT_AGENT_CONNECTION_LAG_TIME` are new constants in `agent-next-gen-shared-utils.ts`, next to `CURRENT_AGENT_NAME`. `formatHeaderGreeting`/`formatHeaderDate` are no longer imported into `AgentNextGenPage.tsx` (this was their only call site in this file) — both remain exported from shared-utils.ts and still used by Premium/Advanced, which this request didn't touch.

**Real state, not a demo toggle.** Unlike the lyra-ui story (which has an explicit `greeting` Storybook control switching between both states), this app has no such control — the header always shows the identity treatment now. What genuinely varies is the agent-leg status, and this app already had a full real connect/disconnect implementation (`connectAgentLegSignal`/`initialAgentLegStatus`/`fireAgentLegStatusToast`, wired to the real `AgentProfile` — §36-39 and later sections) that the lyra-ui story's own demo state only approximates. A new `agentLegStatus` tri-state (`useState<"disconnected"|"connecting"|"connected">`, seeded from the same persisted `initialAgentLegStatus`) now mirrors `AgentProfile`'s real state genuinely: `handleConnectAgentLeg` sets it to `"connecting"` the instant the header's link is clicked (right before bumping `connectAgentLegSignal`, the same imperative trigger that already drove `AgentProfile`'s own connect flow), and `fireAgentLegStatusToast` — already the callback `AgentProfile`'s `onAgentLegStatusChange` fires on every real settle event — now also calls `setAgentLegStatus(status)` unconditionally, right alongside its existing `saveAgentLegStatus(status)` call (both must run immediately regardless of `showWelcomeModal`; only the toast itself, further down that same function, is deferred while the welcome modal is open).

**Rule zero fix.** The "Connect Agent Leg" link (a hand-rolled `<button>` in lyra-ui's own agent-dashboard.tsx, which has no such restriction) had to become a `Button` here — this app's `eslint.config.js` hard-errors on any bare `<button>` JSX element. Used the exact same "`Button variant="ghost"` stripped to `h-auto p-0 hover:bg-transparent`, reading as a plain underlined text link" pattern the record-header's own "View Details" link (`agent-next-gen-transcript.tsx`) already established, rather than inventing a new link-button treatment.

Files touched: `agent-next-gen-shared-utils.ts` (`CURRENT_AGENT_ID`/`CURRENT_AGENT_CONNECTION_LAG_TIME` constants + exports); `AgentNextGenPage.tsx` (import swap, `agentLegStatus` state, `handleConnectAgentLeg`/`fireAgentLegStatusToast` updates, `PageHeader` title/subtitle JSX). Scoped to Agent Workspace 2.0 only, per the literal request — Premium (`AgentWorkspace2WithDeskPage.tsx`) and Advanced (`AgentWorkspaceAdvancedPage.tsx`) still show the time-of-day greeting header, untouched.

Verified tsc-clean (`tsc -b` 277-error baseline unchanged, all pre-existing `@nicecxone/lyra-ui` module-resolution errors) and a full `vite build` (the meaningful check for this repo, since `tsc -b` alone can't resolve that Vite-only alias) — clean, no new errors. eslint on both touched files: the one new error the first pass introduced (hand-rolled `<button>`, Rule zero) was fixed as described above; the 3 remaining errors/warnings (`PanelLeftClose` unused import, one unrelated pre-existing hand-rolled `<button>` elsewhere in the file, one unused `_omit` in shared-utils.ts) are all pre-existing baseline, unchanged.

## 117. Identity header made a genuine fixed/pinned page header, rolled out to Premium and Advanced too

Per explicit follow-up (after seeing a screenshot of §116's result): "i want it to be a page header and applied to advanced and premium too" — two combined asks, both addressed here: (a) the header should be a real fixed/non-scrolling page header rather than living inside the scrollable dashboard body, and (b) the same identity/"Connect Agent Leg" treatment §116 built for Agent Workspace 2.0 only should also apply to Premium (`AgentWorkspace2WithDeskPage.tsx`) and Advanced (`AgentWorkspaceAdvancedPage.tsx`).

**Fixed vs. scrollable, disambiguated.** §116 had (per an earlier, separate follow-up already in place before this request) put the `PageHeader` inside the scrollable dashboard body, `-mx-6 mb-6`-adjusted to align with the cards below — a deliberate earlier choice, not an oversight. Rather than guess which behavior "page header" meant, asked the user directly: "Fixed, non-scrolling" vs. "keep it in the scrollable body." User chose fixed/non-scrolling. All three tiers' identity headers now render in each page's dedicated non-scrolling header slot (immediately inside the top-level `dashboard` div, before the "Body row" content), not inside the scrollable body div — the old scrollable-body copies (title `formatHeaderGreeting(...)`, `bordered={false}`, `titleSize="2xl"`, the "Personal Queue" chip in `actions`) were removed from all three files, superseding the position §116 had described for 2.0.

**Rolled out identically to Premium and Advanced.** Both tiers gained the same `CURRENT_AGENT_ID`/`CURRENT_AGENT_CONNECTION_LAG_TIME` import swap, the same tri-state `agentLegStatus` (`useState<"disconnected"|"connecting"|"connected">`, seeded from `initialAgentLegStatus`), the same `handleConnectAgentLeg` (`setAgentLegStatus("connecting")` before bumping `connectAgentLegSignal`) and `fireAgentLegStatusToast` (`setAgentLegStatus(status)` unconditionally alongside `saveAgentLegStatus`) changes §116 made for 2.0, and the same title/subtitle JSX (`Agent {name}` / `Agent ID: {id}` + tri-state Connect Agent Leg link / "Connecting..." / "Connection Lag Time" — built with the same Rule-zero-safe `Button`-as-text-link pattern §116 established) and "Personal Queue" chip `actions`.

**Per-tier `bordered`/`titleSize` differences.** 2.0 and Advanced have no other bordered element sharing their fixed header slot, so both now use `PageHeader`'s own default `bordered={true}`. Premium is different: its fixed header slot also holds a `TabList` (desk tabs + full-screen customer tabs) immediately below the header, which draws its own `border-b` — so Premium's header explicitly keeps `bordered={false}` to avoid a doubled border line, and both `PageHeader` and `TabList` are wrapped together in one fragment, restoring the header's own documented original position (directly above the tab row) from before it had been moved into the scrollable body. None of the three tiers override `titleSize` anymore (all read at `PageHeader`'s own default `lg`) — the earlier `titleSize="2xl"` bump was specific to the scrollable-body greeting variant this follow-up removed.

Files touched: `AgentNextGenPage.tsx`, `AgentWorkspace2WithDeskPage.tsx`, `AgentWorkspaceAdvancedPage.tsx` — each: fixed-slot `PageHeader` added (Premium: paired with its `TabList` in one fragment), old scrollable-body `PageHeader` copy removed. No changes to `agent-next-gen-shared-utils.ts` this round (§116 already added the constants both this and that request share).

Verified `npx eslint` on all three files: identical 6-error/12-warning baseline across all three (2 pre-existing errors each — `PanelLeftClose` unused import, one unrelated hand-rolled `<button>` elsewhere in the file — plus pre-existing `react-hooks/exhaustive-deps` warnings), no new violations introduced by this change in any of the three. `npx vite build` — clean, no new errors (only the same pre-existing Tailwind ambiguous-class and >500kB chunk-size warnings, unrelated to this change).

## 124. Search panel's Customers sub-tab row-click now opens the docked panel, pop-out icon removed (Advanced only)

Per explicit request ("in the customer tab in search in advanced have the customer info panel open in the right panel when the rows are clicked (like in premium). But remove the pop out icon to make it a tab"): Advanced's Search panel Customers sub-tab (the only tier whose Search panel has a Customers tab at all — see `useSearchPanelContent`'s own doc comment) now opens `CustomerRowInfoPanel` docked on the right when a row is clicked, and that panel instance no longer shows any expand/full-screen affordance.

**Row click re-enabled.** `AgentWorkspaceAdvancedPage.tsx`'s `useSearchPanelContent({...})` call's `customers.onRowClick` was a no-op (per an earlier session's explicit request); it now sets `selectedCustomerRow`, toggle-open/close on the same row — the same pattern Premium's own desk-tab Customers table `onRowClick` already uses. The desk-tab Customers table's own `onRowClick` (a separate, still-intentional no-op) is untouched — this change is scoped to "the customer tab in search" only, per the request's own wording.

**Pop-out icon removed via a new `hideFullScreenToggle` prop, not `onOpenFullScreenTab`.** The visible "pop out" icon here turned out to be `InteriorPanel`'s own built-in full-screen-toggle button (controlled by `CustomerRowInfoPanel`'s `allowFullScreen={!onOpenFullScreenTab}`), not the separate Premium-only "Open Tab" button (`onOpenFullScreenTab`-gated, `SquareArrowOutUpRight` icon) — Advanced has no desk-tab full-screen-tab mechanism to pop into, so wiring `onOpenFullScreenTab` here would've been the wrong fix (it would swap in a button with nowhere to go). Instead, `CustomerRowInfoPanel` (agent-next-gen-customer-info-panel.tsx) gained a new optional `hideFullScreenToggle?: boolean` prop (default `false`) that forces `allowFullScreen={false}` on the underlying `InteriorPanel` regardless of `onOpenFullScreenTab` — `allowFullScreen={!onOpenFullScreenTab && !hideFullScreenToggle}`. This prop is passed only from the Search panel's own Customers-tab `CustomerRowInfoPanel` call site (agent-next-gen-search-panel.tsx's `useSearchPanelContent` hook, the Advanced-only `"customers"` branch) — every other `CustomerRowInfoPanel` consumer (both desk-tab Customers tables, Premium and Advanced) is untouched and keeps its existing `allowFullScreen={!onOpenFullScreenTab}` behavior.

Files touched: `agent-next-gen-customer-info-panel.tsx` (new `hideFullScreenToggle` prop + `allowFullScreen` logic), `agent-next-gen-search-panel.tsx` (passes `hideFullScreenToggle` at the Advanced-only Customers-tab call site), `AgentWorkspaceAdvancedPage.tsx` (`customers.onRowClick` re-enabled). No changes to Premium or 2.0 — this request was Advanced-only ("in advanced").

Verified `npx eslint` on all three touched files: identical pre-existing baseline (`AgentWorkspaceAdvancedPage.tsx`'s own 2 pre-existing errors — `PanelLeftClose` unused import, one unrelated hand-rolled `<button>` — plus pre-existing `react-hooks/exhaustive-deps`/`react-refresh/only-export-components` warnings across all three), no new violations. `npx vite build` — clean, no new errors (only the same pre-existing Tailwind ambiguous-class and >500kB chunk-size warnings).

## 126. Raise CustomerRowInfoPanel's z-index above the Customers table row's launch-channel button overlay (Premium/Advanced)

Per explicit request ("make the customer info panel z-index higher than the z-index of the launch channel buttons in the rows of the customer tables for both premium and advanced"): the docked Customer Information panel (`CustomerRowInfoPanel`, agent-next-gen-customer-info-panel.tsx) was rendering BELOW the Customers table row's own hover-reveal launch-channel buttons (Call/Email/WhatsApp — the `leadingChannelStack` overlay built in §407/§412, `z-10` in agent-next-gen-customers-table.tsx), so hovering a row underneath the open panel bled that overlay visibly on top of it (per the attached screenshot).

**Fix.** `CustomerRowInfoPanel`'s `InteriorPanel` render gained `className="z-20"`. `InteriorPanel`'s own built-in outer wrapper is hardcoded `z-[5]` (interior-panel.tsx) — `className` is merged in via `cn`/`tailwind-merge`, which resolves same-property Tailwind class conflicts by keeping the last one, so `z-20` cleanly overrides the built-in `z-[5]` without needing to touch `InteriorPanel` itself, and comfortably clears the row overlay's `z-10`. This is a single change to the shared `CustomerRowInfoPanel` component, so it applies everywhere the component renders: both desk-tab Customers tables (Premium, Advanced) and Advanced's Search panel Customers sub-tab instance alike — no per-call-site wiring needed, unlike §124/§125's opt-in props.

Files touched: `agent-next-gen-customer-info-panel.tsx` only.

Verified `npx eslint` — 0 errors, same pre-existing warning baseline. `npx vite build` — clean; confirmed `z-20` present in the compiled CSS output.

## 127. Swap CustomerRowInfoPanel's close button to a "close panel" icon (`PanelRightClose`), keep plain X for Premium's open-as-tab variant

Per explicit request ("for the customer table interior customer information panels use the close panel icon instead of close icons (keep them as close icons when they are open as tabs in premium)"): the Customer Information panel's top-right close button — rendered by `InteriorPanel`'s/`ContainerHeader`'s own built-in `onClose` close button, which was always lucide-react's plain `X` with no way to override it — now uses `PanelRightClose` instead, matching the "closing a docked panel" glyph already used elsewhere in this file (the Customer Information side-panel's own pin/close button).

**lyra-ui change.** `ContainerHeader` (container-header.tsx) gained a new optional `closeIcon?: React.ReactNode` prop, defaulting to the exact same `<X className="h-5 w-5" .../>` it always rendered — every existing consumer is unaffected. `InteriorPanel` (interior-panel.tsx) gained a matching `closeIcon?: React.ReactNode` pass-through prop, forwarded to its internal `PanelHeader` call alongside `onClose`.

**App change.** `CustomerRowInfoPanel`'s `InteriorPanel` call (agent-next-gen-customer-info-panel.tsx) now passes `closeIcon={<PanelRightClose className="h-5 w-5" strokeWidth={1.5} aria-hidden="true" />}`. Since `CustomerRowInfoPanel` is the one shared component behind every "customer table interior" instance (both desk-tab Customers tables in Premium/Advanced, plus Advanced's Search panel Customers sub-tab), this single change covers all of them — no per-call-site wiring needed.

**Premium's "open as tab" variant is untouched, per the request's own explicit carve-out.** `CustomerFullScreenTabContent` (the component Premium's desk-tab full-screen customer tabs render) has its own hand-rolled close button — a plain `ActionIconButton` wrapping a literal `<X>` in its `actions` slot — that was never routed through `onClose`/`closeIcon` at all, so it keeps reading as "close this tab" exactly as before.

## 128. Raise DraggablePanel's own resize-handle z-index above docked panel content (fixes a regression from §126)

Per explicit request ("the interior panel is overlaying on top of the drag icon so the dragged containers cannot be resized when the interior panel is open"): §126's fix (raising `CustomerRowInfoPanel`'s own `InteriorPanel` to `z-20`, to clear the Customers table row's `z-10` hover-overlay) had an unintended side effect — the Search panel itself is hosted inside a `DraggablePanel` (draggable.tsx), whose own left-edge resize handle (`docked` variant) and bottom-right corner resize handle (`float` variant) were BOTH `z-10` — a full stacking level BELOW the newly-raised `z-20` panel now rendering inside `children`. Since those resize handles are plain siblings of `contentNode` within the same stacking context (not nested inside a separately-isolated one), the elevated docked customer panel started painting over them, silently swallowing their mouse events — exactly the "can't resize the container anymore" symptom in the attached screenshot.

**Fix.** Both `DraggablePanel` resize-handle elements (`edge-resize`, the docked variant's left-edge strip; `corner-resize`, the float variant's bottom-right corner) raised from `z-10` to `z-30` — one level above the highest panel-content z-index currently in play (`z-20`), so these handles stay interactive above whatever arbitrary content a consumer renders inside `children`, rather than needing to be re-tuned every time some inner panel's own z-index changes.

Files touched: `lyra-ui/src/components/draggable.tsx` only.

Verified `npx tsc --noEmit` in lyra-ui — same pre-existing 2-error baseline (admin-shell.tsx, list-item.tsx), no new errors. `npx vite build` (agent-next-gen-v2) — clean, no new errors.

## 131. Move the customer-record channel launch buttons into the Customer Overview card, next to Edit

Per explicit request ("move the contact icon buttons into the customer overview and put them next to the edit button in a row above the other information") — `CustomerAddChannelButton`'s wide, one-button-per-channel shape (mail/WhatsApp/chat as outline icons, Voice as a solid-primary "Call" button) used to render in the panel/tab header's own action cluster (`CustomerRowInfoPanel`'s `headerActions`, `CustomerFullScreenTabContent`'s `PanelHeader actions`) — now it renders inside the shared Customer Overview accordion (`CustomerInformationPanelBody`, agent-next-gen-customer-info-panel.tsx) instead, in a new top row shared with the existing Edit button: channel buttons left, Edit right (`justify-between`), sitting above the Phone #/Contact #/Email/etc. field list rather than Edit alone sitting below the last field row.

`CustomerInformationPanelBody` gained two new optional props, `row` (`CustomerListRecord | null`) and `onStartInteraction`, mirroring `CustomerAddChannelButton`'s own signature — when `row` is set, its wide-mode `CustomerAddChannelButton` renders in the new top row; when it's `undefined` (every consumer with no such row to launch a channel from), only Edit renders there, same as before. The new row is gated `!overviewEditing`, same reasoning the Edit button itself already had: once editing starts, Save/Cancel in the footer are the only way back out, and launching a new channel mid-edit would be a confusing detour.

Wired at both real call sites that had a `row` to give it: `CustomerRowInfoPanel` (docked panel opened from a Customers-table row) and `CustomerFullScreenTabContent` (Premium's "open as desk tab" / Advanced's Search-panel Customers full-record view — see §130) — both removed their own header's `CustomerAddChannelButton` and now pass `row`/`onStartInteraction` straight through to `CustomerInformationPanelBody` instead. Always the "wide" shape now (not `isNarrowActions`-dependent like `CustomerRowInfoPanel`'s old header placement was) — the Customer Overview card has its own fixed width regardless of whether the panel itself is docked or full screen. `CustomerInformationSidePanel` (an already-open interaction's record header — never had these buttons at all) and `CustomerInfoHoverPreview` (never offered `allowOverviewEdit` either) are unaffected — they simply don't pass `row`, so the new top row renders with no channel buttons in it.

## 135. "All Contacts" restyled as a title badge; detached into its own standalone breadcrumb view (supersedes §134's panel-based mechanism)

Per explicit follow-up request (a "My Contact History" screenshot showing "All Contacts" as a small pill immediately after the title, plus: "detach the contacts table from the search panel and have it exist on its own - have the breadcrumbs at the top say 'Dashboard / All Contacts' and when Dashboard is clicked navigate back to the Dashboard view") — two changes, both applied to all 3 tiers.

**1. Button restyle.** The "All Contacts" `Button` moved out of `ContactHistoryCard`'s `headerActions` (far-right of the header row) into a new `headerTitleBadge` prop on the `DashboardCard` call — the slot `ContainerHeader` renders inline immediately after the title `<h2>`, in the same flex row (`container-header.tsx`: `<div className="flex items-center gap-2 min-w-0">{title}{titleBadge}</div>`). `DashboardCardProps` doesn't redeclare `headerTitleBadge` in its own interface, but `DashboardCard` spreads `...containerProps` (`Omit<ContainerProps, "children">`) straight through to `Container`, so the prop works regardless. `headerActions` now holds only the `SearchInput` and `ContactHistoryDateFilterChip`.

**2. Full detachment from the shared Search panel.** §134's approach — opening the shared right-side panel's Contacts tab and full-screening it — is replaced entirely. `handleOpenAllContacts` on each page now just does `setShowAllContacts(true)`, a new top-level boolean state alongside `showSettings`. This makes 4 mutually-exclusive top-level views instead of 3: Settings, All Contacts, an active interaction, and the Home dashboard (default) — enforced the same way `showSettings` already was: an effect that clears both `showSettings` and `showAllContacts` when `activeInteractionId` is set, and the two `buildNavItems(...)` click handlers (Home, Settings) each now also clear `showAllContacts`. `mainRegionTabLabel` (the narrow-viewport combined-panel switcher's label) gained a `showAllContacts ? "All Contacts" :` branch between Settings and the active-interaction branch.

The new `showAllContacts` branch in the Content column's top-level ternary mounts `InteractionsListView` (agent-next-gen-interactions-table.tsx) directly — reimported in each page file alongside `type InteractionHistoryRecord` — with no wrapper `overflow-y-auto`/padding div, since the component already manages its own internal scrolling and fills its parent (`flex flex-col flex-1 min-w-0 min-h-0 overflow-hidden` on its own root), the same way it's mounted with zero wrapper inside the Search panel's own Contacts tab body. Above it, gated behind `showPageHeader` (true in the real app via `App.tsx`'s route — confirmed via grep, not just Storybook's bare `<AgentNextGenPage />` story), a `PageHeader` renders the breadcrumb: `title="All Contacts"` with `breadcrumb={{ label: "Dashboard", onClick: () => setShowAllContacts(false) }}`. This is `PageHeader`'s existing `breadcrumb` prop (page-header.tsx — `{ label: string; onClick?: () => void }`, internally composed from the real `Breadcrumb`/`BreadcrumbList`/`BreadcrumbItem`/`BreadcrumbLink`/`BreadcrumbSeparator` parts), confirmed via grep to have never been used anywhere in this app before this change — its first real usage. Clicking "Dashboard" returns to the Home dashboard view; the "All Contacts" segment itself is the non-interactive current page.

The Search panel's own Contacts sub-tab (§134's original destination) is untouched and still renders `InteractionsListView` internally via `useSearchPanelContent` — this is now a second, independent render of the same component for a different entry point, not a shared instance.

## 136. All Contacts row click opens a summary `InteriorPanel` instead of jumping straight into a live assignment

Per explicit follow-up request ("in the dashboard / contacts table - when one of the rows is clicked, open an interior panel like the ones in My Contact History") — the standalone All Contacts view (§135) previously wired `InteractionsListView`'s `onOpenInteraction` straight to `handleOpenInteractionRow`, so a row click immediately opened it as a live assignment in the left nav. That's now a two-step flow, matching how a My Contact History row already behaves: clicking a row opens a summary panel first (Duration/notes box + a synthesized Conversation/Transcript/Body section), and only clicking that panel's own "Re-open" (or "Redial", for voice rows) footer button actually calls `handleOpenInteractionRow`.

New state, `selectedAllContactsRecord: InteractionHistoryRecord | null`, tracks the selected row — a second, independent pair from the dashboard's own `selectedContactHistoryEntry`/`InteriorPanel`, since the All Contacts view is a fully standalone container (§135) with no dashboard content mounted alongside it to share that docked slot with. Cleared alongside `showAllContacts` everywhere that already got cleared (the `activeInteractionId` effect, both `buildNavItems` click handlers, and the breadcrumb's "Dashboard" `onClick`), so a fresh visit to All Contacts never opens with a stale panel already showing.

`InteractionHistoryRecord` (agent-next-gen-interactions-table.tsx) has no `ContactHistoryEntry`-shaped summary of its own, so a new exported adapter, `buildContactHistoryEntryFromInteractionRecord`, maps one on the fly — `statusLabel`/`caseId`/`skillName` map straight across (`record.status`/`caseId`/`skill`), `description` uses `record.context`, `duration` uses `record.resolutionTime`, `redial` is true only for `voice` rows (matching every other `ContactHistoryEntry` consumer's "Redial is voice-only" convention), and `timeAgo` is computed fresh from the row's real `createDateValue` via a new `formatInteractionRecordTimeAgo` helper (relative "Nm ago"/"Nh ago"/"Nd ago" — this table's rows carry a real `Date`, unlike `ContactHistoryEntry`'s hand-authored `timeAgo` strings). `messages`/`email`/`phone`/`whatsappHandle` are left undefined, so `ContactHistoryEntryDetail` falls back to its own synthesized message/body builders — the same fallback every hand-authored Contact History row without a real transcript already relies on. This reuses `ContactHistoryEntryDetail` completely unchanged, rather than building a second, parallel detail view for data that's already 90% the same information under different field names.

Each of the 3 tiers' All Contacts branch now wraps `InteractionsListView` and a second `InteriorPanel` (gated behind `showInteriorPanel`, same as the dashboard's own) in a `relative flex flex-1 min-h-0 overflow-hidden` row — the same "Body row: main content + interior panel" shape the dashboard branch already uses further down in each file.

## 137. All Contacts (and its selected-row panel) now survives navigating Home, instead of resetting every time

Per explicit follow-up request ("if the agent has all contacts open and navigates away from home, keep home page (all contacts) at the last state before navigating away") — `showAllContacts`/`selectedAllContactsRecord` (§135/§136) were previously reset to `false`/`null` at 3 transition points: the `activeInteractionId` effect, and both the Home and Settings click handlers passed into `buildNavItems(...)`. That meant leaving All Contacts for Settings or an interaction — even briefly — silently discarded it, so "Home" always landed back on the plain dashboard.

All 3 of those reset calls are now removed. `showAllContacts`/`selectedAllContactsRecord` are no longer touched by starting an interaction, opening Settings, or clicking Home — they're only ever cleared by the All Contacts view's own "Dashboard" breadcrumb (still `setShowAllContacts(false); setSelectedAllContactsRecord(null);`), which remains the one deliberate "leave All Contacts" action. The `activeInteractionId` effect now only closes Settings (`setShowSettings(false)`).

Since `showAllContacts` is no longer forced false when an interaction starts, the content-column ternary's `showAllContacts` branch condition and the narrow-viewport `mainRegionTabLabel` computation's equivalent branch each gained a `&& !activeInteraction` guard, so an active interaction still visually takes priority over All Contacts while it's open — mutual exclusion is now enforced by that guard at render time instead of by clearing the underlying flag. Once the interaction ends (or Settings closes), whichever view was showing before — plain dashboard or All Contacts, complete with any selected-row panel — reappears exactly as it was left, since nothing ever discarded that state to begin with.

## 139. All Contacts row click: selected row shows as active, re-clicking it toggles the panel closed

Per explicit follow-up request ("when the contact row is selected show it as active and allow it to close the panel on toggle") — two related fixes to the All Contacts row-click flow (§136).

**1. Active row highlight.** `InteractionsListView` (agent-next-gen-interactions-table.tsx) gained a new optional prop, `activeRecordId?: string | null`, so the CALLER (each page's own `selectedAllContactsRecord`) can tell the table which row currently has its summary panel open — the table itself has no way to know this on its own, since the panel lives outside the component (§136). The row's existing `<TableRow data-state={selectedIds.has(record.id) ? "selected" : undefined}>` condition (where `selectedIds` is the bulk-actions checkbox selection) now also checks `activeRecordId === record.id`, reusing the exact same `data-state="selected"` highlight style rather than inventing a second visual treatment. The two conditions are deliberately kept independent — a row can be checkbox-selected for a bulk action while a completely different row's panel is open, and both states can be true at once without colliding.

**2. Toggle-to-close.** Each of the 3 pages' `onOpenInteraction` handler (passed to `InteractionsListView`) changed from unconditionally opening the clicked row (`setSelectedAllContactsRecord(record)`) to a toggle: `setSelectedAllContactsRecord((prev) => (prev?.id === record.id ? null : record))`. Clicking the row that's already open now closes the panel; clicking any other row still swaps straight to it, same as before. `activeRecordId={selectedAllContactsRecord?.id ?? null}` is passed alongside so the newly-active (or newly-cleared) row's highlight stays in sync automatically — no separate state to keep in sync by hand.

## 141. Premium and Advanced's Search panel replaced with a simplified customer search bar (search → results list → full customer profile)

Per explicit request ("in advanced and premium, clear the search panel of content and just have a search bar - when the agent types into it - add a 'search' icon button into the input then when clicked display results like screenshot 2 - when the agent clicks on the customer display their customer information content"), with two reference screenshots: a pill search input mid-typing with a blue circular submit button at its trailing edge, and a plain "Search Customers" input above a flat list of customer rows (name + `CST-XXXXX` ID + trailing chevron).

Both tiers' Search panel previously rendered the shared `useSearchPanelContent` hook's tabbed `Contacts`/`Messages`/`Customers`/`Threads` view (§97/§98). That tabbed view is now bypassed entirely for these two tiers via a new opt-in `simpleCustomerSearch?: boolean` option on the hook — when true (and a `customers` bag is supplied), the hook takes an early-return branch rendering a bare search bar in place of the whole `title`/`headerContent`/`TabList` structure. 2.0's own Search app is unaffected either way (it's fully hidden per §139, so this branch is unreachable there regardless of the option).

**New `SimpleCustomerSearchBody` component** (agent-next-gen-search-panel.tsx) owns the actual flow: a `lyra-ui` `SearchInput` (now passed the new `onSubmit` prop — see `lyra-ui/PROJECT_SUMMARY.md` for that component-level change) bound to the same shared, already-reactive `customers.searchQuery`/`onSearchChange`/`sortedRows` each tier's own main desk-tab Customers view already owns (the established convention for this prop bag — not a second independent copy of customer data). A local `submitted` boolean (deliberately NOT derived from query non-emptiness, since `sortedRows` is already live-filtered as the agent types) gates whether results are shown at all — typing alone never reveals anything; only clicking the new submit button (or pressing Enter) does. Clearing the query back to empty resets `submitted` to `false`. Once submitted, `customers.sortedRows` renders as a flat list of `lyra-ui` `ListItem`s (title = full name, subtitle = contact number, trailing `ChevronRight`) — deliberately the plain list component from the reference screenshot, not the full `CustomersListView` data table (sorting/filters/bulk-select) the old tabbed "Customers" view used. An empty result set shows a centered `Inbox` icon + "No matching customers" message instead of an empty list.

Clicking a result row calls the existing `customers.onRowClick`, which both tiers already wire to their own shared selected-row state. When a row is selected, the hook's `simpleCustomerSearch` branch swaps the whole panel body to `CustomerFullScreenTabContent` (agent-next-gen-customer-info-panel.tsx) — a pre-existing component originally built for Premium's "open customer as desk tab" feature (§376), reused here as-is for "customer information content" rather than building a second profile view. The panel's breadcrumb title becomes `Search > {customer name}`, with the "Search" segment clickable to close the row and return to the bare search bar.

**`panelTabs` generalization.** `CustomerFullScreenTabContent`'s `tabs` prop was previously hard-coded inside the hook to `AGENT_WORKSPACE_CUSTOMER_PANEL_TABS` (the reduced Overview/Copilot/Detail/Notes set) at its one existing call site (Advanced's old "Customers" tab branch) — safe only because Advanced was the only tier that ever rendered that branch. That's no longer true now that Premium's `simpleCustomerSearch` flow renders the same component too, and Premium's own main desk-tab Customers view uses the fuller `CUSTOMER_PANEL_TABS` set (Copilot/Overview/Contacts/Detail/Directory/Tasks/Notes/Accounts/Tickets), not the reduced one. Fixed by adding a new required field, `panelTabs: readonly CustomerPanelTabLabel[]`, to `SearchPanelCustomersProps`, threaded through explicitly at every `customers` bag construction site instead of hard-coded in the hook: Premium passes `CUSTOMER_PANEL_TABS` (matching its own main Customers view), Advanced passes `AGENT_WORKSPACE_CUSTOMER_PANEL_TABS` (ditto), and 2.0's own now-fully-unreachable `customers` bag (its Search app is hidden entirely, §139) also gained `panelTabs: AGENT_WORKSPACE_CUSTOMER_PANEL_TABS` purely to keep that dead code type-correct — caught via a manual scan of `npx tsc -b`'s output (the project's real `npm run build` script is `tsc -b && vite build`, a genuine type-check this session's habitual `npx vite build`-only checks skip; `tsc -b`'s bulk output itself is unusable as a pass/fail signal here, since `@nicecxone/lyra-ui` resolves entirely through a vite-only alias invisible to `tsc -b`, surfacing dozens of unrelated pre-existing `TS2307` errors — but scanning past that noise for issues touching this session's own changes still caught this one real gap).

Files touched: `agent-next-gen-search-panel.tsx` (hook + new component + `panelTabs` field), `AgentWorkspace2WithDeskPage.tsx` (Premium: `simpleCustomerSearch: true` + new `customers` bag), `AgentWorkspaceAdvancedPage.tsx` (Advanced: `simpleCustomerSearch: true` + `panelTabs` added to its existing `customers` bag), `AgentNextGenPage.tsx` (2.0: `panelTabs` added to its dead `customers` bag only, for type-correctness), plus `lyra-ui/src/components/search-input.tsx` (new `onSubmit` prop — see that repo's `PROJECT_SUMMARY.md`).

Verified: `npx eslint` on all 4 touched app files — each matched its own known pre-existing baseline exactly (2 errors + 4 warnings per page file; `agent-next-gen-search-panel.tsx`: 0 errors, 1 pre-existing warning), no new issues. `npx vite build` from `agent-next-gen-v2` — clean (only the same pre-existing Tailwind ambiguous-class and >500kB chunk-size warnings).

## 142. Launching an interaction closes whichever app panel is open (all 3 tiers)

Per explicit request ("when an interaction is launched, close the app panel") — the right-docked "app panel" (Search/Notifications/Schedule/WEM/etc., gated by the `panelOpen` boolean, `activePanelKey`'s own visibility source of truth) previously stayed open across an interaction launch, leaving the agent's screen split between an unrelated panel and the interaction that just arrived.

`setPanelOpen(false)` was added as the first statement in every "launch/reopen an interaction" handler, in all 3 page files: `handleStartCall` (New Outbound / customer channel buttons — including the new `simpleCustomerSearch` flow from §141, which calls this same handler via `onStartInteraction`), `handleQuickDial` (dial pad), `handleRedial` and `handleReopenContactHistoryEntry` (Contact History row actions), and `handleOpenInteractionRow` (Interactions/search-result row open). In `handleStartCall` specifically, the call is placed AFTER the existing agent+chat early-return branch (which opens the "Agent Chat" panel rather than starting a normal interaction) — that branch explicitly isn't "launching an interaction," so it's exempt and left free to open its own panel undisturbed.

This is a pure state-setter addition — no other logic in any of these 5 handlers changed. The panel's own close animation (an existing `useEffect` keyed on `panelOpen`) handles the visual transition automatically; no direct manipulation of `activePanelKey`, `panelMounted`, or `panelState` was needed.

Files touched: `AgentNextGenPage.tsx`, `AgentWorkspace2WithDeskPage.tsx`, `AgentWorkspaceAdvancedPage.tsx` — 5 call sites each, 15 total.

Verified `npx eslint` on all 3 files — each matched its own known pre-existing baseline exactly (2 errors + 4 warnings per file), no new issues. `npx vite build` — clean (only the same pre-existing Tailwind ambiguous-class and >500kB chunk-size warnings).

## 143. Fixed a real regression: Performance/Productivity's date filter chip permanently stuck collapsed

Per explicit follow-up report (screenshot: "Performance" dashboard card's date filter chip rendering as a bare `⋮` icon instead of "Date: Today ▾") — "the responsive query you added to the contact card affected the performance and productivity widgets as well - the filters are always collapsed on them - I want the query to be not on the widget itself but the parent container."

Root cause: §137's `ContactHistoryCard` fix (raising `lyra-tokens.css`'s `@container` collapse threshold from 480px to 768px) lived in a class, `.lyra-container-header-actions-wrap`, that EVERY `headerActionsWrap` consumer shared automatically — including `AgentDashboard`'s Performance/Productivity cards (lyra-ui) and this app's own equivalent cards (`agent-next-gen-interaction-dashboard.tsx`), which had only turned `headerActionsWrap` on to get their `DateFilterChip`'s icon-collapse fallback. Those cards are wider than 480px but narrower than 768px, so raising the shared threshold flipped them from "always full label" to "always the collapsed icon" — a regression with no changes of their own.

Fixed at the design-system level: `lyra-ui`'s `.lyra-container-header-actions-wrap` no longer establishes the CSS container-query boundary itself — that's now a separate, explicitly opt-in class, `.lyra-container-header-query-boundary`, which the caller applies to whichever ancestor's width should decide the collapse (see that repo's `PROJECT_SUMMARY.md` for the full writeup). `ContactHistoryCard` (agent-next-gen-contact-history.tsx) now passes `className="lyra-container-header-query-boundary"` on its own `DashboardCard` call — no other app-side code changed. Performance/Productivity's own cards (`agent-next-gen-interaction-dashboard.tsx`, unchanged) no longer inherit any container-query boundary at all, so their `DateFilterChip` now stays in full/expanded label mode unconditionally, which is the correct outcome for a card that never legitimately needed the icon-collapse fallback.

Verified: `npx eslint` on `agent-next-gen-contact-history.tsx` — matched its own known 13-warning/0-error baseline, no new issues. `npx vite build` — clean; grepped the built CSS to confirm `.lyra-container-header-query-boundary{container-type:inline-size}` is present in the output.

## 149. Search-panel results now survive navigating away and back (Premium + Advanced)

Per explicit follow-up request, with a screenshot of the simplified Customers search body (§709's `SimpleCustomerSearchBody`) showing a submitted query ("da") but a blank body underneath: "keep the latest search results after a search is run if navigated away from the search panel."

Root cause: `SimpleCustomerSearchBody`'s "should the results list actually be shown" flag (`submitted`) was a local `useState` owned inside that component itself — unlike `searchQuery`, which was already lifted page-level state per this file's own established convention (see its own top-of-file doc comment on `SearchPanelCustomersProps`, "lifted state... has to stay in the page itself"). `searchQuery` survived the Search panel being unmounted and remounted (switching to another app-header icon, e.g. Notifications, then back to Search); `submitted` didn't, resetting to `false` on every remount — so the query text stayed visible in the input but the matching results underneath vanished, exactly as the screenshot showed.

Fixed by lifting `submitted` the same way `searchQuery` already is: added `searchSubmitted`/`onSearchSubmittedChange` to `SearchPanelCustomersProps` (agent-next-gen-search-panel.tsx), converted `SimpleCustomerSearchBody` to read/write those instead of its own `useState`, and added `customerSearchSubmitted`/`setCustomerSearchSubmitted` page-level state in both Premium and Advanced (right alongside their existing `customerSearchQuery` state), wired into the `customers` prop bag each page already builds for `useSearchPanelContent`. Agent Workspace 2.0 never opts into `simpleCustomerSearch` (its own Search app-header icon is hidden entirely per §139, so `SimpleCustomerSearchBody` is unreachable there regardless), but its own `customers` bag still had to satisfy the same now-non-optional type fields — given inert stubs (`searchSubmitted: false, onSearchSubmittedChange: () => {}`) with a doc comment explaining why, rather than making the fields optional and weakening the type for the two consumers that actually need them.

Files touched: `agent-next-gen-search-panel.tsx` (new type fields + `SimpleCustomerSearchBody` converted off local state), `AgentWorkspace2WithDeskPage.tsx`, `AgentWorkspaceAdvancedPage.tsx` (new lifted state + wiring), `AgentNextGenPage.tsx` (inert stub fields only, to satisfy the shared type).

Verified: `npx eslint` on all 4 touched files — the 3 page files each matched their own known pre-existing baseline exactly (2 errors + 4 warnings per file); `agent-next-gen-search-panel.tsx` matched its own known pre-existing 1-warning baseline (`react-refresh/only-export-components` on its own shared-types-plus-component export shape), no new issues. `npx vite build` — clean (only the same pre-existing Tailwind ambiguous-class and >500kB chunk-size warnings), confirming the new non-optional type fields didn't break any consumer.

## 153. Customer Information panel now always starts closed for a newly opened assignment

Per explicit follow-up request: "keep the customer information panel closed when a new assignment is opened."

Reverses an earlier feature (the `lastSidePanelOpenChoice` ref) that had every freshly started/quick-dialed/redialed/reopened interaction's Customer Information panel open in whichever open/closed state the agent last explicitly picked (via the panel's own close button or the header toggle icon), rather than hardcoded open every time. That mechanism is now removed entirely: every "new interaction" launch call site across all 3 tiers (`handleStartCall`, `handleQuickDial`, `handleRedial`, `handleReopenContactHistoryEntry`, `handleOpenAssignmentFromNotification`, and Premium's Marcus-Webb-trigger/search-launch equivalents) hardcodes `setSidePanelOpen(false)` instead of reading the ref, so a brand-new assignment's panel now always starts closed, full stop — regardless of what the agent did on any other assignment.

Switching between two ALREADY-active assignments is a separate mechanism (`sidePanelStateByAssignmentId`, the per-assignment open/full-screen memory `Map`) and is untouched by this change — an assignment the agent already opened the panel on still shows it open again when switched back to. Only genuinely new launches are affected.

Since every read site was hardcoded to `false`, the `lastSidePanelOpenChoice` ref itself (and its two write sites, in `handleSidePanelClose` and `handleSidePanelIconToggle`) became dead code and was removed along with it, rather than left as unused/misleading machinery. Several nearby doc comments that referenced the old ref were also updated to describe the new always-closed behavior. The top-of-file Conditional Logic Reference was updated to match (the "WHEN a launched interaction is genuinely new" bullet under "## 2. Interactions & assignments", and the "Header close" / "Icon toggle click" bullets under the SidePanel behavior section).

Files touched: `AgentNextGenPage.tsx`, `AgentWorkspace2WithDeskPage.tsx`, `AgentWorkspaceAdvancedPage.tsx` — all 3 tiers identically.

Verified: `npx eslint` on each of the 3 page files matched that file's own known pre-existing baseline (2 errors, 4 warnings each) exactly, zero new issues. `npx vite build` — clean (only the same pre-existing Tailwind ambiguous-class and >500kB chunk-size warnings).

## 155. Empty-assignments-queue message + icon in the left nav

Per explicit request, with a screenshot of the collapsed-to-Home-and-Settings left nav ("Assignments (0)"): "add a message and icon into the left nav when there are no assignments indicating that the agent's assignment queue is empty."

Reuses lyra-ui's existing `EmptyState` component (icon + message + optional description, the same "nothing to show here" placeholder already used for `DraggablePanel`'s default body/`AiPanel`'s history view) rather than hand-rolling a new one. Renders right below `AssignmentsSectionCaption` and above the (empty) `.map()` of assignment cards, in all 3 tiers: `<EmptyState icon={<Inbox .../>} message="Your assignment queue is empty" description="New assignments will appear here." />`, gated on `interactions.length === 0 && navOpen`.

The `navOpen` half of that gate matters: `EmptyState`'s icon+message content only makes sense in the expanded rail — there's no room for text in the icon-only collapsed rail — which matches `AssignmentsSectionCaption`'s own established behavior of returning `null` in that same collapsed-and-nothing-to-show state, right above this. `EmptyState`'s own default classes (`h-full w-full ... py-8`, sized for a bounded box) are overridden to `h-auto w-full py-6` here, since this renders inline in `LeftNav`'s normal-flow `header` scroll region rather than a fixed-height container — unmodified, `h-full` would resolve to zero height and the whole block would collapse invisibly.

## 156. Left nav's Home/Settings rail pinned to the bottom — implemented, then reverted

Per explicit request: "let's fix the home and settings buttons to the bottom by default in the left nav (I may move this back if I don't like it so don't update lyra-ui)."

Confirmed with the user first that genuinely pinning the rail to the bottom (regardless of assignment-card-list length) requires an internal `mt-auto` tweak inside lyra-ui's `LeftNav` — no app-only workaround exists, since the wrapping divs involved aren't exposed to app files. The user approved the small, easily-revertible change despite the "don't update lyra-ui" note, so `left-nav.tsx`'s `!itemsFirst` rail wrapper gained `mt-auto` in both the overlay-mode and default render branches.

On the very next turn: "I changed my mind — revert it back." Fully reverted — `left-nav.tsx` is back to byte-for-byte its prior state, doc comments included. No app files were ever touched for this feature (LeftNav's default config already matched what all 3 tiers use), so there's nothing to revert on this side beyond this log entry.

Worth flagging for any future repeat of this request: lyra-ui's own `PROJECT_SUMMARY.md` now documents that this exact "pin to the bottom regardless of content" behavior has been tried and reverted TWICE — once in an earlier session (a different implementation, same underlying tradeoff), and now this one. The tradeoff is geometrically inherent: pinning the rail to the true bottom always opens a gap between the last assignment card and the rail when the list is short, and that's been rejected both times it's been tried.

Files touched: none, net of the revert. See lyra-ui's own `PROJECT_SUMMARY.md` for the corresponding entry there.

## 157. Left nav restructured: Home above Assignments, Settings genuinely pinned to the bottom, empty-state centered — now ALL 3 TIERS

Per explicit request, originally scoped to 2.0 only ("for agent left-nav only"): "fix the settings to the bottom of the left nav and move the home above the assignments section - vertically center align the 'no assignments' message." Unlike §153-§156 (and almost every other left-nav change in this file), this one deliberately did NOT mirror to `AgentWorkspace2WithDeskPage.tsx` (Premium) or `AgentWorkspaceAdvancedPage.tsx` (Advanced) at first — both kept the combined Home+Settings `items` rail exactly as `buildNavItems` already builds it, unaffected.

**Follow-up, explicit request**: "match the left nav in premium and advanced to 2.0" — mirrored the exact same restructuring (below) to both tiers, byte-for-byte matching 2.0's own call site (`items`/`itemsFirst`/`headerFillsHeight`/`footer` all identical; comments trimmed slightly on the mirrors to point back at 2.0's own copy for the fuller history rather than duplicating every word three times). Also updated lyra-ui's own "Agent Next Gen Left Nav" story (`LeftNav.stories.tsx`) to match this same arrangement — see lyra-ui's own `PROJECT_SUMMARY.md` for that entry. `AgentNextGenTemplate.stories.tsx`'s own separate `<LeftNav>` (a different, larger story) was deliberately NOT restructured — only its stale "mirrors ... exactly" comment was corrected to note the divergence, since matching that story wasn't part of this request.

Previously (§156 and earlier), Home and Settings were one `buildNavItems(...)` array passed straight through as `LeftNav`'s `items` prop, rendered together as a single two-item rail — always both in the same place, whichever place that was (`itemsFirst`'s sticky-top, or the default sticky-bottom). This request splits them into two different places for the first time:

- **Home** — now the sole entry in `items`, with `itemsFirst` set. Renders above `header` (the "Assignments (N)" caption + empty-state/cards), `sticky top-0`, directly under `pinnedHeader` ("New Outbound") — the same `itemsFirst` mechanism §153-era comments describe this app briefly using once before (when it held BOTH items), landed on again here for Home alone.
- **Settings** — now rendered via a new `footer` prop instead of `items`. `footer` (`left-nav.tsx`) renders as a sibling AFTER the entire scrollable region, genuinely pinned to the aside's true bottom edge regardless of how much (or little) content is above it — no `sticky`/`mt-auto` trick needed, since it was never part of the scrollable flow to begin with. This directly supersedes the `mt-auto` approach tried and reverted twice before (§156 and an earlier session) for the OLD combined rail — `footer` sidesteps that whole problem class because it isn't inside the scroll region at all.

**lyra-ui changes required** (both scoped as opt-in, off by default, so every other consumer — Premium, Advanced, AdminShell, every `LeftNav`/`TreeMenu` story — is unaffected):
- New exported `NavRail` component (`left-nav.tsx`) — renders a `NavItem[]` exactly the way `LeftNav`'s own item rail does (full `TreeMenu` expanded, icon-only buttons with tooltips + active-bar collapsed), but as a standalone piece a consumer can drop into `footer` (or anywhere else) instead of `items`. Used here for the single Settings item. Deliberately its OWN copy of the icon-only rendering logic, not a shared extraction of `LeftNav`'s internal one — zero risk to `LeftNav`'s existing rendering for any other consumer.
- New `headerFillsHeight` prop on `LeftNav` — lets `header`'s own wrapper grow (`flex-1 min-h-0`) to fill whatever height is left in the scroll region, instead of shrink-wrapping to content (the default, unchanged for everyone else). Needed so the empty-state centering (next) has actual slack to center within — without it, `header` only ever takes the height its content needs, leaving no leftover space to distribute even if the message asked to center inside it.

**Empty-state centering**: the "Your assignment queue is empty" `EmptyState` (§155) is now wrapped in a `<div className="flex flex-1 min-h-0 items-center justify-center">`. Combined with `headerFillsHeight` above, this centers the message vertically within whatever space `header` isn't using for the caption — previously it just sat flush under the caption with empty space below it, uncentered, in normal flow.

`buildNavItems` itself (`agent-next-gen-interaction-dashboard.tsx`) is untouched — still returns `[Home, Settings]` as a pair. Each of the 3 pages' own `LeftNav` call site destructures `[homeNavItem]` for `items` and `[, settingsNavItem]` for `footer`'s `NavRail` from two separate calls to that same shared builder, rather than forking its logic — avoids duplicating the icon/active-state/handler wiring anywhere.

**Follow-up fix, caught from a screenshot** (icon-only Settings button with no label, nav fully expanded): `left-nav.tsx`'s `footer` prop only auto-injects `expanded` via `injectExpanded` in the OVERLAY-mode render branch — the default/inline-mode branch renders `{footer}` directly with no injection at all (pre-existing asymmetry in `left-nav.tsx`, not introduced by this change; `footer` simply had no other consumer whose `expanded` prop mattered until now). `NavRail`'s `expanded` prop was silently stuck on its own `false` default as a result, always rendering the collapsed icon-only button regardless of `navOpen`.
