**Second follow-up, ALL 3 TIERS this time** (not scoped to 2.0 — this is a pre-existing color bug in a component all 3 tiers already share, unrelated to the 2.0-only restructuring above): per explicit request, with a screenshot comparing the "Your assignment queue is empty" message (left nav) against Contact History's own "Nothing to Display" placeholder side by side — the left-nav one read visibly dimmer/lower-contrast. Root cause: lyra-ui's `EmptyState` (§155) hardcodes `text-lyra-fg-disabled` for its icon/message/description; Contact History's own hand-rolled "nothing to show" block (`agent-next-gen-contact-history.tsx`) uses the one-step-brighter `text-lyra-fg-secondary` instead — same shape, different token, so they never matched. Added an opt-in `tone?: "disabled" | "secondary"` prop to `EmptyState` (default `"disabled"`, byte-for-byte unchanged for every other consumer — `DraggablePanel`'s default body, `AiPanel`'s history view, the `EmptyState` stories); `tone="secondary"` now passed at all 3 tiers' "Your assignment queue is empty" call site to match Contact History's token exactly. Files touched: `empty-state.tsx` (lyra-ui); `AgentNextGenPage.tsx`, `AgentWorkspace2WithDeskPage.tsx`, `AgentWorkspaceAdvancedPage.tsx` (all 3 — one line each, `tone="secondary"`). Verified: lyra-ui `npm run lint`, and `npx eslint`/`npx vite build` across all 3 page files — all matched known baselines exactly, no new issues.

**Third follow-up, ALL 3 TIERS again**: per explicit request — "I can see the text growing/shrinking in the empty message of the left nav - can you have it display: none and then display: inline after the panel opens/closes so it is more elegant? maybe have it fade in?" Root cause: the empty-state block mounts the instant `navOpen` flips true, but `left-nav.tsx`'s `<aside>` is simultaneously mid-`transition-all duration-200` on its own width (`w-[60px]` → `w-[256px]`), so the message sat inside a still-widening box for that whole 200ms, visibly rewrapping as more horizontal room opened up — the reported "growing/shrinking."

Fixed by adding `animate-in fade-in-0 duration-150 delay-200 fill-mode-backwards` to each empty-state wrapper `<div>`, at all 3 tiers. `delay-200` matches the aside's own width-transition duration exactly, so the message stays invisible (held at its animation's first frame, opacity 0, via `fill-mode-backwards`) through the entire transition, only beginning its `duration-150` fade-in once the width has settled. This is the same delayed-reveal technique this file already uses elsewhere (the interaction-switch fade, `key={`interaction-${activeInteraction.id}`}` a few hundred lines below this one in `AgentNextGenPage.tsx` — see that block's own doc comment for the fullest writeup of the pattern), applied here for the first time to a `LeftNav`/`EmptyState` consumer.

Asked for literally as `display: none` → `display: inline`; implemented as an opacity fade instead, since `display` itself isn't something a CSS transition can animate — an abrupt toggle, not the "maybe have it fade in" also asked for in the same request — and `opacity: 0` during the delay hides the reflow just as completely (nothing visibly growing or shrinking if nothing is visible yet) without losing the smooth fade. No equivalent treatment was needed on the close side: the mount condition (`navOpen`) flips false and the block unmounts in the same tick, before the aside's own collapse transition has moved at all, so there was nothing to reflow into view there in the first place.

**lyra-ui side**: mirrored the identical wrapper-class change onto the `AgentNextGen` ("Agent Next Gen Left Nav") story's own empty-state block in `LeftNav.stories.tsx`, so the story stays a faithful match of the app. Also added a brand-new, dedicated `EmptyStateExample` ("Empty State") story to the same file — per the request's second half ("add a story to the left nav that shows an empty state like this") — showing the empty state directly on load (fixed `count={0}`, no interaction-seeding/dismissal logic, no `pinnedHeader`) rather than only being reachable by dismissing every card in the `AgentNextGen` story first; toggling its collapse/expand button replays the fade-in on demand. See lyra-ui's own `PROJECT_SUMMARY.md` for that entry.

Files touched: `AgentNextGenPage.tsx`, `AgentWorkspace2WithDeskPage.tsx`, `AgentWorkspaceAdvancedPage.tsx` (all 3 — wrapper class only, no logic changes); `LeftNav.stories.tsx` (lyra-ui — `AgentNextGen` story's wrapper class, plus the new `EmptyStateExample` story). Verified: `npx eslint` on all 3 page files matched each one's own known baseline exactly (2 errors, 4 warnings each, unchanged). `npx vite build` — clean, 5047 modules, only the same pre-existing Tailwind ambiguous-class/chunk-size warnings. lyra-ui's `npm run lint` (`tsc --noEmit`) matched its standing 3-error baseline exactly, none in `LeftNav.stories.tsx`.

**Bug fix, caught from a screenshot with 7 assignments loaded**: `headerFillsHeight` (added earlier in this same entry) turned out to break internal scrolling once the assignment list actually got long — cards just bunched up with no scrollbar, pushing Settings (and everything else) down past the viewport instead of scrolling inside the rail the way a shorter list still did.

Root cause was in lyra-ui's `left-nav.tsx`, not anywhere app-side: `headerFillsHeight` applied `flex-1 min-h-0` to `header`'s wrapper. `flex-1` (grow-to-fill, for the empty-state centering this flag exists for) was correct; `min-h-0` was not — it let the wrapper shrink BELOW its own content's natural height once the scroll region ran out of room, which is exactly backwards for a long card list: instead of the list overflowing (which is what gives the surrounding `overflow-y-auto` region something to scroll), the wrapper just got compressed to fit whatever space was left, and the browser grew the whole nav to accommodate the content instead. Removing `min-h-0` (leaving `min-height` at its flex-item default, `auto`) fixes both cases at once: `auto` resolves to the wrapper's own min-content size as a floor, so `flex-grow` can still expand it to fill leftover space when content is light (the empty state), while a heavy card list still refuses to shrink below what it actually needs — overflowing the scroll region exactly like the pre-`headerFillsHeight` `flex-shrink-0` default already did. Fixed at the lyra-ui source (`left-nav.tsx`, both the overlay- and inline-mode branches) rather than app-side, so it's automatically corrected for all 4 current `headerFillsHeight` consumers (all 3 app tiers plus the lyra-ui story) with no app-side changes needed. See lyra-ui's own `PROJECT_SUMMARY.md` for the fuller writeup.

Verified: `npx eslint` on all 3 page files, `npx vite build` — both matched known baselines exactly, no new issues (no app-side files even touched). lyra-ui's `npm run lint` — matched its standing 3-error baseline, none in `left-nav.tsx`.

**Two more follow-ups, from real usage with 7 seeded assignments — both fixed at the lyra-ui source, no app-side changes**:

1. **Bug: scrolled `InteractionNavItem` cards painting over the sticky Home rail.** Root cause: a card can contain its own `position: relative` descendant deep inside it (e.g. channel-row.tsx's hover-only Consult/Transfer/Outcome button cluster) — `position: relative` with the default `z-index: auto` doesn't create a new stacking context, so that descendant gets promoted straight into `<aside>`'s own shared stacking context (from ITS `relative z-10`), painted in DOM tree order alongside every other positioned element in it, including the sticky Home rail. Since Home is earlier in the DOM (`itemsFirst` puts it before the cards) but a card's own nested positioned descendant is later in tree order, tree order alone let the descendant paint on top whenever the two visually overlapped mid-scroll. Fixed with an explicit `z-index` (`z-20`) on both sticky rails (`itemsFirst`'s top rail and the pre-existing bottom rail), which only has to beat the default `z-index: auto` every nested positioned descendant uses — sidesteps tree order entirely.

2. **Re-added the scroll-area fade, now on BOTH edges.** The `itemsFirst` restructuring earlier in this entry moved Settings out of the scroll region entirely (into `footer`), so the OLD bottom-sticky-rail fade (still present, unchanged, for the `!itemsFirst` arrangement) had nothing left to attach to at the top of the NEW arrangement, and the scroll region's bottom edge lost its own anchor too. Re-added per explicit request: a top fade under the sticky Home rail (mirroring the old fade, flipped — solid at the rail's own edge, fading to transparent into the cards below it), and a new bottom fade anchored to the scroll viewport's own edge (transparent at top, solid at the bottom, same as the old fade's gradient) since there's no sticky rail there anymore to carry one. Both gated on the same `listHasOverflow` flag as the original fade (not scroll-position aware — an established, deliberate simplification in this file). Per the same request's explicit constraint ("leave enough padding so it doesn't fade the top/bottom of the first/last interactionNavItems"): the top fade relies on `AssignmentsSectionCaption`'s own rendered height (comfortably taller than the fade's 32px reach) to never reach the first real card; the bottom fade is paired with `header`'s own reserved bottom padding, bumped from `pb-3` (12px) to `pb-8` (32px) — exactly matching the fade's height — so scrolled to the true end, the fade only ever covers that reserved empty space, never the last card's real edge.

Fixed once in `left-nav.tsx` (both render branches), so both are corrected/added for all 4 current `headerFillsHeight`/`itemsFirst` consumers (all 3 app tiers plus the lyra-ui story) with no app-side changes. See lyra-ui's own `PROJECT_SUMMARY.md` for the fuller writeup.

Verified: `npx eslint` on all 3 page files, `npx vite build` — both matched known baselines exactly (no app-side files touched). lyra-ui's `npm run lint` — matched its standing 3-error baseline, none in `left-nav.tsx`.

**Fourth follow-up**: per explicit request/devtools screenshot — "fix the assignments header under the home button so it doesn't scroll and update the padding-top:0 and bottom:4px." The "Assignments (N)" caption used to be `header`'s own first child, in normal scrolling flow with the cards under it — scrolling away exactly like they do, rather than staying put under Home.

New lyra-ui `stickyCaption` prop on `LeftNav`: content rendered inside the SAME sticky-top box as `items` (Home), directly after it — pinned together as one unit, exempt from scrolling. All 3 tiers (plus the lyra-ui story) moved their `<AssignmentsSectionCaption>` out of `header` and into this new prop.

That sticky box's own padding also changed, per the exact devtools values given: `pt-1` (a small gap under `pinnedHeader`/"New Outbound") → `pt-0` (Home now sits flush against it); no bottom padding when `header` was present → `pb-1` (4px gap before the caption/cards below).

Knock-on fix: with the caption gone from `header`'s scrolling flow, its own first child is now the empty-state block or the first real card directly — no longer buffered from the top fade (added earlier in this entry) by the caption's own height. `header` gained a matching `pt-8` (32px, same as the fade's own height) alongside its existing `pb-8`, so the always-on top fade still only ever covers reserved padding, never a real card's edge.

Fixed once in `left-nav.tsx` (new prop + both render branches), applied at all 4 call sites (all 3 app tiers, the lyra-ui `AgentNextGen` story, and the dedicated `EmptyStateExample` story). See lyra-ui's own `PROJECT_SUMMARY.md` for the fuller writeup.

Verified: `npx eslint` on all 3 page files, `npx vite build` — both matched known baselines exactly. lyra-ui's `npm run lint` — matched its standing 3-error baseline, none in `left-nav.tsx`/`LeftNav.stories.tsx`.

**Fifth follow-up, correcting the fourth's padding target**: per explicit correction, from a devtools screenshot showing a large visible gap above the first card — "you didn't update the padding like I wanted on the assignments header: padding-top:0, padding-bottom:4px. also - remove the padding top on the assignment container." The fourth follow-up above had applied `pt-0 pb-1` to the wrong box: the OUTER sticky rail (`items` + `stickyCaption`'s shared container), not `AssignmentsSectionCaption`'s own internal padding — so the caption itself kept its original spacing (`py-2` on its text row, `pb-2` on its wrapper) the whole time, and the devtools values never actually landed where intended.

Fixed with a new opt-in `compact?: boolean` prop on `AssignmentsSectionCaption` (lyra-ui) — default `false`, so the component's two other, un-restructured consumers (`AgentNextGenTemplate.stories.tsx`, `agent-next-gen-interaction-dashboard.tsx`) keep their original spacing untouched. With `compact` set: the text row's `py-2` (8px/8px) becomes `pt-0 pb-1` (0px/4px, the exact devtools values given), and the wrapper's own trailing `pb-2` (8px, the gap after the `Separator`) becomes `pb-1` (4px) to match. Wired `compact` into all 4 `stickyCaption` call sites (all 3 app tiers, plus both the `AgentNextGen` and `EmptyStateExample` stories in `LeftNav.stories.tsx`) — the only 4 places this caption now lives pinned under a sticky "Home," where the tighter spacing was actually asked for.

Second half of the request — "remove the padding top on the assignment container" — reverts the `pt-8` the fourth follow-up above had just added to `header`'s wrapper. That padding was reasoned through as a buffer to keep the always-on top fade off the first real card now that the caption (which used to serve as its own natural buffer) had moved into `stickyCaption`; in practice it created a visibly oversized gap between the sticky rail and the first card, worse than the trade-off it was meant to prevent. Removed — `header`'s wrapper goes back to `itemsFirst && "pb-8"` only (bottom padding for the bottom fade, unchanged), in both `left-nav.tsx` render branches. The top fade can now, in principle, touch the very top of the first card/empty-state block again — accepted as the better trade-off per this explicit request, since it's a soft gradient rather than a hard edge.

Fixed once in `left-nav.tsx` (both branches, `pt-8` removed) and once in `assignments-section-caption.tsx` (new `compact` prop), applied at all 4 `stickyCaption` call sites. See lyra-ui's own `PROJECT_SUMMARY.md` for the fuller writeup.

## 158. AppHeader icon buttons default to a floating "dropdown," not a docked panel — and remember a manual dock across close/reopen

Per explicit request, queued a few turns earlier and picked back up now: "update the appheader icons to open as dropdowns not panels by default and keep them open when assignments are added - if docked by the user then closed, on next open keep them docked." All 3 tiers share the exact same single-shared-panel architecture for the AppHeader icon row (Search/Customers/Accounts/Tickets/WEM/Screen Pop/Agent Chat/Schedule/Notifications) — one `Draggable` container, `panelOpen`/`panelVariant`/`activePanelKey` state, `handlePanelButtonClick` — so this fix is identical in shape across `AgentNextGenPage.tsx`, `AgentWorkspace2WithDeskPage.tsx`, and `AgentWorkspaceAdvancedPage.tsx`.

**"Open as dropdowns not panels by default."** `panelVariant`'s `useState` default flipped from `"docked"` (the full layout-pushing panel, added by an earlier request) back to `"float"` — `Draggable`'s floating/popover-style variant, which per `getPanelFloatStyle`'s existing fallback positioning already anchors to a sensible spot (the content container's own top-right corner) the very first time it's ever opened, with no extra positioning work needed for this to read as a dropdown near the header icons.

**"Keep them open when assignments are added."** Investigated first, since this could have meant adding new logic: a NEW assignment arriving passively (e.g. Premium's Marcus Webb "L"-keypress-triggered inbound chat, or `handleOpenAssignmentFromNotification`) never calls `setPanelOpen(false)` anywhere in any of the 3 files — only the agent's own explicit interaction-launch actions do (`handleStartCall`, `handleQuickDial`, `handleRedial`, `handleReopenContactHistoryEntry`, `handleOpenInteractionRow`, all per an earlier, separate, still-intentional request: "launching an interaction closes whichever app panel is open"). So this half of the request was already true of the existing code — confirmed via a search across all 3 files' `setPanelOpen(false)` call sites, nothing changed here.

**"If docked by the user then closed, on next open keep them docked."** `handlePanelButtonClick`'s open-from-closed branch used to unconditionally call `setPanelVariant("docked")` on every open, regardless of what variant the panel had been in before its last close. Removed that line entirely — `panelVariant` was already never reset anywhere else on close (confirmed via the same `setPanelOpen(false)` site survey above; none of them touch `panelVariant`), so simply no longer overwriting it on open is sufficient by itself: the state persists across a close/reopen cycle on its own. Concretely — first-ever open uses the new `"float"` default; if the agent later docks the panel by hand (`Draggable`'s own built-in dock button, wired to `handlePanelVariantChange`, unchanged) and then closes it, the next header-icon click reopens directly into `"docked"`, since that's simply whatever `panelVariant` was already holding.

No lyra-ui changes were needed — this is purely a default-value and one-line-removal fix in each of the 3 app-side `useState`/`handlePanelButtonClick` pairs.

Files touched: `AgentNextGenPage.tsx`, `AgentWorkspace2WithDeskPage.tsx`, `AgentWorkspaceAdvancedPage.tsx` — each file's `panelVariant` `useState` default (`"docked"` → `"float"`, plus updated doc comment) and `handlePanelButtonClick` (removed the `setPanelVariant("docked")` line, plus updated doc comment).

Verified: `npx eslint` on all 3 page files matched each one's own known baseline exactly (2 errors, 4 warnings each, unchanged). `npx vite build` — clean, 5047 modules, only the same pre-existing Tailwind ambiguous-class/chunk-size warnings. No lyra-ui files touched, so no lyra-ui verification needed.

## 159. Remove auto-close of the shared AppHeader panel on interaction launch

Per explicit request, prompted by a screenshot of the Search panel open as a floating dropdown mid-search: "I want to keep the panel open when an interaction starts - remove the auto-close." This reverses one specific half of an earlier, separately-requested behavior — "launching an interaction closes whichever app panel is open" — while leaving every other close path untouched.

Across all 3 tiers (`AgentNextGenPage.tsx`, `AgentWorkspace2WithDeskPage.tsx`, `AgentWorkspaceAdvancedPage.tsx`), the same 5 handlers had each been given a 3-line doc comment plus an unconditional `setPanelOpen(false);` as the very first side effect of the agent explicitly launching or reopening an interaction: `handleStartCall`'s outbound-flow continuation (the branch after the agent+chat early-return), `handleQuickDial`, `handleRedial`, `handleReopenContactHistoryEntry`, and `handleOpenInteractionRow`. All 15 call sites (5 handlers × 3 files) had both the comment and the `setPanelOpen(false);` line removed, leaving each handler's next real line of code as its new first line — no other logic in any of these handlers changed.

The 4 legitimate close paths per file were deliberately left alone, confirmed via a full `setPanelOpen(false)` grep across all 3 files post-edit: `handlePanelButtonClick`'s own toggle-close (clicking an already-open panel's header icon again), the docked panel's `ContainerHeader` `onClose`, the floating panel's `onClose`, and the full-screen variant's exit-fullscreen-and-close handler. None of these are "launching an interaction" — they're the panel's own explicit close affordances — so the panel now only closes when the agent closes it directly, never as a side effect of starting or reopening an interaction.

Files touched: `AgentNextGenPage.tsx`, `AgentWorkspace2WithDeskPage.tsx`, `AgentWorkspaceAdvancedPage.tsx` — 5 handlers each, comment + `setPanelOpen(false);` removed from all 15 sites total.

Verified: `npx eslint` on all 3 page files matched each one's own known baseline exactly (2 errors, 4 warnings each, unchanged). `npx vite build` — clean, 5047 modules, only the same pre-existing Tailwind ambiguous-class/chunk-size warnings. No lyra-ui files touched, so no lyra-ui verification or PROJECT_SUMMARY.md entry needed.

## 160. Show channel icon buttons on hover of a Search-panel customer row, launching an interaction the normal way

Per explicit request, prompted by a screenshot of the Search panel's simplified Customers results list (Premium/Advanced only — see §141's `simpleCustomerSearch` mode): "display the channel icon buttons on hover of customers in the search and allow the agent to launch an interaction from those buttons the same way they normally would."

Rather than building a new hover-icon treatment, this reuses `CustomerChannelCell` (agent-next-gen-customers-table.tsx) as-is — the same hover-reveal row of per-channel `CustomerChannelPopoverButton`s the Customers table's own "Channels" column already renders. Each icon already fades in purely via `group-hover`/`group-focus-within` (its own `!open && "opacity-0 group-hover:opacity-100 ..."` classes — see that component's own doc comment), so no new fade logic was needed; the only thing missing in `SimpleCustomerSearchBody` (agent-next-gen-search-panel.tsx) was an ancestor with `className="group"` for that selector to key off, since the plain `ListItem` rows there had never had one. Each icon opens the exact same `CustomerChannelPicker` launch popover (channel/skill selection → `onStartInteraction`) as every other channel-icon entry point in the app — not a new one-off flow — and its own `onClick` already `stopPropagation`s, so clicking a channel icon launches that channel without also firing the row's own `onClick` (opening the full customer record).

Concretely: `ListItem`'s `trailing` slot now renders `<CustomerChannelCell row={row} onStartInteraction={customers.onStartInteraction} />` immediately before the existing chevron, and the row itself gained `className="group"`. No `relative`/absolute-overlay positioning was needed here (unlike the table's separate `leadingChannelStack`/`CustomerChannelStack` overlay variant — see §90's own doc comment) since these render as normal trailing flex content, not painted over other text. A customer row with no supported channels still renders `CustomerChannelCell`'s own static "—" placeholder in that slot, same as every other consumer of that component — no special-casing added.

Files touched: `agent-next-gen-search-panel.tsx` only (import + `SimpleCustomerSearchBody`'s row rendering). No changes to `agent-next-gen-customers-table.tsx` itself (`CustomerChannelCell` was already exported and needed no modification) or to any of the 3 page files (they already pass `onStartInteraction` through `SearchPanelCustomersProps` unchanged).

Verified: `npx eslint` on `agent-next-gen-search-panel.tsx` — 0 errors, 1 warning (`react-refresh/only-export-components`, confirmed pre-existing via a stash/pop comparison — unrelated to this change). `npx vite build` — clean, 5047 modules, only the same pre-existing Tailwind ambiguous-class/chunk-size warnings. `npx tsc --noEmit` was not used as a gate here (it doesn't resolve the `@nicecxone/lyra-ui` Vite alias and reports a large, pre-existing, unrelated set of module-resolution/implicit-any errors across the codebase — eslint + `vite build` are this project's actual verification loop, per this file's own established convention). No lyra-ui files touched, so no PROJECT_SUMMARY.md entry needed.

## 161. Fix left-nav "Assignments" header height jump at zero assignments

Per explicit request: "make the min-height of the assignment header in the left nav 36px so it stays the same height when there are no assignments." `AssignmentsSectionCaption`'s heading row (`agent-next-gen-interaction-dashboard.tsx` — the local component all 3 tiers actually render, see §157/§158) only shows its expand/collapse-all + sort buttons when `showSort` (`count > 1`) — with 0 or 1 assignment those buttons don't render at all, so the row's rendered height used to shrink down to just the "Assignments (N)" text's own line-height, a visible jump right at this caption (and the `Separator` directly beneath it) whenever the last assignment was dismissed down to zero.

Added `min-h-[36px]` to the heading row's own className (alongside its existing `compact`-conditional padding). 36px matches the buttons' own rendered height in the normal (2+ assignments) case, so that case is visually unaffected — only the buttons-absent case (0 or 1 assignment) is now padded out to match instead of shrinking, keeping the caption's overall height constant regardless of assignment count.

Files touched: `agent-next-gen-interaction-dashboard.tsx` only (single className addition on `AssignmentsSectionCaption`'s heading row). Since all 3 tiers render this same local component (not 3 separate copies), no per-page mirroring was needed.

Verified: `npx eslint` on `agent-next-gen-interaction-dashboard.tsx` — 0 errors, 24 warnings (matches this file's own known baseline exactly — all pre-existing `react-refresh/only-export-components`). `npx vite build` — clean, 5047 modules, only the same pre-existing Tailwind ambiguous-class/chunk-size warnings.
